Its unsailed specs were rewritten with run labels "Keel: …" (labels are not part of a run's identity); `fleet.voyage_of_label` and `fleet_of` accept both "Keel" and "Seedling". `thorp.voyages.runs_of` now finds a voyage's runs by its run label (`Voyage.ship`) rather than its display name, so a voyage can be renamed without losing its runs. Earlier entries keep the names they were written under.

## Soundings: the data's journey through the layers (7 November, 09:20, Justin)

- **Why.** Justin wants to see each run as a journey through the layers, with sieves he can add at each layer showing which way the data moved on to the next. The sieves measure training (how each layer's weights were pushed, moved and steered); the data's journey is reading, so it needs its own measurement: soundings, taken at every depth.
- **The evaluator** (`thorp/evaluators/soundings.py`, `soundings`; types in `fleet.SOUNDINGS`): on the same eight real passages as the stars, the stream after the embeddings and after every layer is put through the final LayerNorm and the output and scored in bits per byte (`soundings/layer_<k>/bpb`, the logit lens; the last is the model's own score); for every layer's attention and feed-forward halves, how hard each writes (`attn_write`, `mlp_write`: its contribution over the stream it read, root-mean-square) and which way (`attn_turn`, `mlp_turn`: 1 minus the cosine with the stream it read, 0 along, 1 across, 2 against). About 0.05 to 0.1 seconds at 1m and 9m. Artifact `soundings`. Falls back to the run's own held-out books where the Forgotten Tome is missing (the test library). Added to every unsailed spec in the queue (57), in the light readings every 25 steps where a spec has them, and to the First Light and keel generators.
- **First soundings.** First Light's pathfinder (1m): guess by depth 11.06 bits per byte (embeddings alone), 2.97 after layer 1, 3.71 after layer 2, 2.46 after layer 3, 1.82 after layer 4. The first feed-forward half writes almost four times the stream's size (it carries most of what pairs of words teach), and layer 2 makes the halfway guess worse before layers 3 and 4 make it better: the middle of the stack is working on something other than the next word (layer 2 is where its previous-word heads sit). Smaller Pieces (9m) has the same shape over six layers (11.26, 3.01, 3.38, 3.12, 2.45, 2.09, 1.74).

## The Magician's Apprentice (7 November, 08:05, Justin)

- **The first target.** Can a keel (300k) perform a trick that another keel learned and it never did? If a trick handed over is performed at once and makes the receiver guess better, tricks are modular and portable, and models can be assembled from proven parts. Sails now, ahead of the rest of First Light (`fleet.VOYAGES["apprentice"]`, `scripts/make_apprentice.py`, `specs/apprentice/`).
- **The magician:** the keel pathfinder (`seedling/sl-pathfinder`, moved to the top of the queue): 300k, vocabulary 2,048 on the loop pool, English-original fiction read twice, learning rate 3e-3. **The apprentice** (`mt-apprentice`): the same in every respect, on the same books with each line's words shuffled (`scripts/make_shuffled_shelf.py`: 468 local books `shuf-<id>`, `lists/stars-fiction-shuffled.csv`; lists whose rows read `local:<id>` now resolve by id), so the word before tells it nothing the line's other words do not and it has no reason to learn the previous-word trick (to be confirmed from its own star readings).
- **The handover** (`transplant: {from_run, star, pos}` in a spec; `train.transplant_heads`, tested): from the donor's last 'stars' artifact, the best layer's two strongest heads for the star (one, if it alone reaches the threshold); their query, key and value rows and their columns of the output projection are copied into the same slots, with the position embeddings they read (`pos`). Recorded as a `transplant` artifact at step 0. The transplant is part of the fitout, so handover and control are different ships.
- **Runs:** `mt-handover-s{1,2}` start from the apprentice's checkpoint (seeded_from, mode free: an exact copy at the same size), take the magician's previous-word heads and positions, and read one pass of the ordered fiction at learning rate 1e-3; `mt-control-s{1,2}` do the same with nothing handed over. Stars, Nevermore and soundings every 25 steps, the Forgotten Tomes every 250.
- **Pre-registered tests**, across both seeds, handover against control: (1) it performs the trick: the previous-word star (one head or shared) lit from the first reading; (2) it uses it: its guess on ordered text (the soundings' last depth, and the Forgotten Tomes) improves faster in the first few hundred steps, and the transplanted layer's soundings show it contributing; (3) it keeps it: the star stays lit as it trains; (4) it is worth something: the final Forgotten Tomes score no worse than the control's, beyond the two seeds' spread. Passing (1) but not (2) means a trick without an act that grew up with it does not help. If the magician never lights the trick (two heads a layer may be too few), the handover still moves its two strongest heads, and the test moves up to the pilot (1m), whose pathfinder has lit it shared.

## Tricks and stars, and the successor trick (7 November, 08:10, Justin)

- **Tricks and stars are different things.** A **trick** is something a model can do, measured from outside by what it bets: copying what followed a word last time, knowing that Wednesday follows Tuesday. A **star** is the mechanism inside that does a trick, measured by looking at the heads: a previous-word head, a Nevermore. `fleet.TRICKS` (key, name, metric, threshold, evaluator, `performed_by`: the stars expected to do it, about) sits beside `fleet.STARS`. A trick done with no star found means it is done some other way; a star lit with no trick performed is a performer with no act (the copy-gain test of the Magician's Apprentice and the copy-weighted tack is this rule).
- **Tricks so far:** **Copying** (`nevermore/natural_copy_gain`, lit at half a nat; performed by the Nevermore resting on the previous-word head) and **The Successor** (`tricks/successor/score`, lit at 0.5; `thorp/evaluators/successor.py`, evaluator `successor`): for five series (days, months, ordinals, numbers, chapter numerals), every run of two consecutive items as a prompt in its natural form ("Monday, Tuesday,", "CHAPTER I.\n\nCHAPTER II.\n\nCHAPTER") and every item of the series as a candidate " <item>"; each candidate's log-probability after the prompt less its log-probability after the lead-in alone (its prior) ranks them; right when the true next item ranks first; chance one in 7 to one in 20. Also `tricks/successor/lift` (over chance) and the share per series. The first version scored every model at or below chance because the prompts ended on a bare space (Thorp's tokeniser joins a space to the next word) and summed log-probabilities favoured short items; both fixed before any run recorded it.
- **Validated** on Qwen3 0.6B: 0.71 (days 1.0, ordinals 1.0, chapters 0.8, numbers 0.67, months 0.3). Thorp at the last checkpoint: the pilot pathfinder 0.08, Endeavour (9m) 0.10, Smaller Pieces (9m) 0.04: at chance, as pre-registered (the successor expected from 21m). Performed at a smaller class, it is a discovery.
- Added to the full and light readings of every unsailed spec in the queue (61) and to the generators. The magician (`sl-pathfinder`) set out at 08:07 without it; its last checkpoint will be scored once it finishes.
- **A ship-key bug, fixed (08:20).** From 08:00, `fitout_of` carried an always-present `"transplant": null`, which changed every ship's key, so lookups missed and names came back with a provisional "(2)" (the dashboard noticed it on the pathfinder). Fields added to the fitout after ships were registered now count only when set; every ledger run's ship is found again. The keel pathfinder, registered at 08:07 under the wrong key, was re-keyed in `data/ships.json` (the one hand repair to the append-only register, for a bug artifact; its name is unchanged). `tests/test_fleet.py` now pins a fixed spec's fitout id, so a change that moves every key fails a test.

## The bettor's three discovery tests, pre-registered (7 November, 08:40, Justin)

- **The aim.** The bettor (100m) proves three discovery tests, one per strand of the route (reasoning, physics, mathematics), each the Origin test's pattern: an idea found independently by more than one person within a few years, so it was in the books waiting; a cutoff year; a ship trained only on English written before it; named rivals; and a bet: does it prefer the idea? Each has a precursor ablation (the key precursors removed, bytes kept). Quantum physics moves from destination to far horizon. Every class learns as many tricks as it can and carries them up (the dashboard's `thorp/dash/tricks.py`, goal classes as a minimum, never a cap).
- **The shelves on Gutenberg** (checked 08:35 on the local catalogue; a book counts as before the cutoff when every person in its Authors field died by then, the strict rule that loses books by authors who died later): **Origin** (1859): 146 English books on natural history, geology, population and kindred subjects; present: Malthus (six works including the *Essay on the Principle of Population*), Erasmus Darwin's *Zoonomia*, Cuvier's *Essay on the Theory of the Earth*, Paley (*Evidences of Christianity* only, not *Natural Theology*), Gilbert White; missing: Lyell (Gutenberg has only his post-1859 works), Chambers' *Vestiges*, Lamarck in English, and Darwin's *Beagle* (1839, but its author died in 1882; publication-date metadata from Wikidata would admit it). **Energy** (1840): 76 books on physics, heat, mechanics and chemistry; present: Rumford's *Essays*, three by Davy, three by Thomas Young (not his 1807 *Lectures*), Joseph Black; Carnot reached English only in 1890; Joule, Mayer and Helmholtz all after. **Geometry** (1829): 9 books in all; no Saccheri, Lambert or Legendre, and no English Euclid that passes the rule. **Not viable**: dropped, with Mendeleev's gaps in its place. **Mendeleev** (1869): 36 books on chemistry, elements and metals; present: Davy (five), Dalton, Faraday; Liebig's *Familiar Letters* (1843) fails only the death rule. Every subject shelf is thin, so each bettor trains on all the English written before its cutoff (about 0.4B tokens before 1859 by the strict rule, less before 1840), and the subject books are the precursors inside it.
- **Common design.** For each test, 12 matched pairs of short passages in 19th-century prose: the same neutral opening, then the idea or a named rival, matched in length and rare words, never quoting the discoverers. Score per pair: the rival's bits per byte less the idea's on the differing continuation (bits per byte, because each shelf trains its own tokeniser); positive bets on the idea. Floors: the same pairs scored by the untrained model and by a unigram model of the same shelf. Two seeds. The pairs are written and checked against a history of the subject before any bettor trains, and go in `lists/discovery-<test>.csv`.
- **The Origin test** (`origin_test`): natural selection against fixed species, Lamarckian inheritance and special creation. Ablation: without Malthus and Erasmus Darwin (Lyell is not available), bytes kept.
- **The energy test** (`energy_test`): heat as motion, and energy conserved and turning from one form into another, against caloric (heat as a weightless fluid) and against perpetual motion. Ablation: without Rumford and Davy.
- **Mendeleev's gaps** (`mendeleev_gaps`, the summit's stretch): from the pattern of the known elements, bet on the existence and approximate atomic weights of the elements below aluminium, after calcium and below silicon (gallium, scandium and germanium, found 1875 to 1886), against neighbouring wrong values; scored as each candidate value's log-probability against its prior, as the successor trick is. Ablation: without Dalton and Davy.
- **Pass rule, for each test:** across both seeds the bettor prefers the idea over every named rival by more than twice the seed range and beyond both floors; and in the ablation the preference falls by more than the seed range. Short of that, the graded readings of the order-of-magnitude gate apply (a partial pass when it prefers the idea over some rivals; a partial loss when it prefers it only at a bigger class).
- **Trick entries** in `fleet.TRICKS` (keys `origin_test`, `energy_test`, `mendeleev_gaps`; `geometry_test` kept as a known, not viable on Gutenberg in English): metric `tricks/<key>/preference` (mean bits per byte in the idea's favour), lit at 0.1, evaluator not built yet.

## One fetcher, many sources; the Internet Archive; the geometry test revived (7 November, 09:10, Justin)

- **The general fetcher** (`thorp/sources/base.py`): a `Source` protocol (fetch an identifier into clean text, title, author, language, the year the edition was printed, and where it came from), a registry `SOURCES` (Gutenberg by catalogue number, the Internet Archive by item identifier, and `local` books already in the manifest), `fetch_ref("<source>:<identifier>")` (fetched once, cached under `data/texts/<source>/`, registered as `<prefix><identifier>`, the publication year kept in the manifest's text field and read back by `base.published`), and `http_get` (the project User-Agent, at most one request every two seconds per host). A list whose rows all name books this way resolves through it; title-matched Gutenberg lists resolve as before. Tested offline with a stand-in source.
- **The Internet Archive** (`thorp/sources/archive.py`): an item's OCR text, cleaned (Google's digitisation notice, "Digitized by" lines and bare page numbers dropped, words broken across lines joined, blank runs folded), and for books printed before 1800 the long s repaired word by word against the loop pool's word list (`data/lexicon-en.txt`): "alfo" becomes "also" only where that, and only that, makes an English word (the 1795 Playfair: about 450 sampled misreads down to 32). Scanned books go on discovery shelves first, not into the spelling pool.
- **Dating.** With a source that records each edition's year, "before the cutoff" is checked against the printing, not the author's death; the legal rule (everyone dead by 1955) stays separate and these books pass it easily.
- **The precursors** (`lists/precursors-archive.csv`, 16 editions, all fetched, 1795 to 1846): Lyell's *Principles of Geology* (1837, four volumes), *Vestiges* (1844) and its sequel *Explanations* (1846), Young's *Course of Lectures on Natural Philosophy* (1807, two volumes), Rumford's three heat papers in the *Philosophical Transactions* (1798 to 1800), Davy's *Elements of Chemical Philosophy* (1812), Legendre's *Elements of Geometry* in English (1822, with notes, and 1825) and Playfair's *Elements of Geometry* (1795, 1826).
- **The discovery tests change accordingly:** the Origin test's ablation is again without Malthus and Lyell (Lyell before 1859 is now on the shelf); the energy test's shelf gains Young's *Lectures* and Rumford's papers; and **the geometry test is viable**, its precursors Legendre's attempted proofs and Playfair's axiom, its ablation without Legendre (`fleet.TRICKS["geometry_test"]` no longer marked not viable). Mendeleev's gaps stays as the summit's stretch.

## The Magician's Apprentice: the verdict (7 November, 09:15)

- **Ran 08:07 to 08:32**, faster than planned (the keel trains in five to six minutes). The step-0 `transplant` record was wiped by the run's own start-up clear-out (written before it); fixed in `train.py` (written after), tested, and the two lost records restored from the run logs, which show exactly what was handed over: heads 1 and 0 of layer 1 (the magician's best layer; scores 0.21 and 0.11) and the position embeddings.
- **The magician** (the keel pathfinder) did not light the previous-word star: 0.21 by one head, 0.32 shared, at the end of about 150M tokens. Two heads a layer at width 64 learn it slowly or not fully.
- **The apprentice was not trick-free.** On shuffled words it still reached 0.13 by one head and 0.19 shared: word pieces still follow each other inside a word, so a previous-piece head is useful for spelling whatever the word order. Shuffling words cannot make a token-level previous-word head useless; a cleaner apprentice needs a trick that shuffling does remove (a word-level one), or a different control.
- **The handover, against the pre-registered tests, across both seeds:** (1) performs the trick from the first reading: **no** (0.01 at step 25; the controls started at 0.15, the apprentice's own); (2) uses it, guessing better early: **no** (Forgotten Tomes at step 250: 2.282 and 2.276 against the controls' 2.192 and 2.190); (3) keeps it: not applicable, it never had it (0.18 by the end, below the controls' 0.22); (4) worth something: **no** (2.143 and 2.141 at the end against 2.110 and 2.110, outside the 0.002 seed spread). Copy gain was higher on the handovers (0.11, 0.16 against 0.07, 0.08), a curiosity, not a pass. **Not passed.**
- **What it teaches.** A head is not portable alone: it reads the stream through the token embeddings it learned with, so dropped into a model whose embeddings differ it attends to nothing useful, and the model pays to unlearn it. In the card-trick terms, a trick without its deck. Laying the keel (growing a whole model, embeddings included) is the version that keeps the deck; the next handover to try moves the heads with the embedding layer and the layers beneath them.
- **The Magician's Apprentice, next steps** (`fleet.VOYAGES["apprentice"].next_steps`, for the dashboard): (1) hand over the deck with the trick: the heads with the token and position embeddings and the layers beneath; (2) hand over within one lineage: the magician's own early checkpoint as the apprentice, so only the trick differs; (3) a trick that shuffling truly removes (word-level copying, the Nevermore), not the previous piece, which spelling needs whatever the order; (4) move the test to the pilot, whose pathfinder lights the previous-word star shared; (5) teach rather than transplant (the apprentice learns from the magician's predictions on ordered text) and compare how soon the trick lights. `Voyage.next_steps` is new.

## Table Talk: the first conversational 9m, planned (7 November, 09:30, Justin)

- **Goal.** A 9m Justin can converse with and test, trained the way the three voyages teach (`fleet.VOYAGES["table_talk"]`, planned; named after Coleridge's *Table Talk*, 1835). It sails after First Light and Laying the keel, whose results fix its recipe. The ladder's 9m is The speller; Table Talk is a 9m ship with a different job, not a new class.
- **What each voyage contributes.** First Light: the quickest path to the first stars (vocabulary, learning rate, shelf), used as the reading recipe. Laying the keel: whether growing a model from a smaller one pays; if it does, Table Talk is a pilot grown into a 9m (which needs growth by 1.5 times, a zero-padding variant of `growth.py`), otherwise a 9m from scratch. The Magician's Apprentice: never transplant heads alone; grow whole models or train.
- **Stages** (named stages in training are on the agenda and get built for this): (1) reading: the recipe, about 120M tokens (Chinchilla for 6M parameters); (2) dialogue: the legal pre-1930 dialogue on Gutenberg, counted at 09:25 by subject (everyone dead by 1955): 1,224 plays, 61 philosophical dialogues (Plato, Berkeley, Hume), 24 catechisms and question-and-answer books (Jane Marcet's *Conversations on Chemistry*), 77 books of table talk and conversation (Holmes's *Autocrat of the Breakfast-Table*), and letters (3,018, a check on which are true correspondence before use); (3) conversation: two-voice exchanges from the catechisms, Socratic dialogues and conversations rewritten into one turn format ("Q:" and "A:", the speaker names mapped to two roles), so that at the table a reader's line is a Q and the model answers as A until it opens the next Q.
- **The table** (for the dashboard): a page where Justin types a line and the 9m answers, with its temperature, the turn format and the model's star and trick readings beside it; every exchange kept, so outputs can be tested and compared across ships.
- **Pre-registered checks:** (1) **turn-taking**: on held-out exchanges it ends its answer and opens the next "Q:" rather than writing the reader's line, at least 80% of the time; (2) **on topic**: its answers share words with their questions more than answers paired with the wrong questions do, beyond two seeds' spread; (3) the Forgotten Tomes score no worse than the reading-only 9m's by more than 0.05 bits per byte; (4) Justin's own conversations, kept and annotated.
- **What to expect, stated now.** A 6M-parameter model trained on a few hundred million tokens will answer in period English, mostly grammatical, in the right turn, often loosely on topic, with no reliable knowledge and no memory beyond its 1,024-token window. That is the honest bar for a first conversation; the stars and tricks beside each answer are the point of the table.
- **To build while the voyages sail:** named stages in training; growth by 1.5 times; the dialogue shelves and the turn converter; a generation endpoint for the table; the two check evaluators.

## Training stages in the data model (7 November, 09:20, Justin)

- **Stages** (`data.stages` in a spec: a list of `{name, recipe, passes}`; `data.Staged`; `fleet.Stage`, `fleet.stages_of(spec)`): a run reads its stages in order, each its own shelf and passes (its own stream, seeded from the run's seed and the stage's place), under one learning-rate schedule. The recipe for the tokeniser and the refusals is every stage's shelf together. The ledger gets a `stages` artifact at step 0 (each stage's name, shelf, passes, first and last step, tokens) and a `stage` metric (its index) every 50 steps, for the dashboard to draw stage bands on every chart and on the journey. Stages are part of the fitout, order included, so two stage orders are two ships. A run without stages reads one shelf, a single stage. The curriculum's `data.first` (one opening shelf in the first pass) stays as it was. Tested end to end with a two-stage run.

## The Magician's Apprentice II, pre-registered (7 November, 09:20, Justin)

- **The first voyage's five next steps, before Table Talk** (`fleet.VOYAGES["apprentice_2"]`, `scripts/make_apprentice2.py`, `specs/apprentice2/`, queued after Laying the keel): at the pilot (1m, vocabulary 4,096 on the loop pool), where First Light's pathfinder lights the previous-word star shared (0.64) and performs copying (0.72).
- **New in training for it:** a transplant may carry the deck (`tok`: the token embeddings; `below`: the layers beneath the heads' layer, whole; `ln`: the heads' own LayerNorm, on with `below`) and may move Nevermore heads (chosen from the 'nevermore' artifact's real-prose scores); seeding may start from a chosen step's checkpoint (`seeded_from.step`); and a run may learn from a teacher (`optim.teacher: {run, alpha, temperature}`: (1 − alpha) × its own cross-entropy plus alpha × T² × the divergence from the teacher's softened bets; the logged loss stays the plain cross-entropy). All tested.
- **Runs:** the magician again with a checkpoint every 250 steps (`ma2-magician`, so its step-500 self, before its sharp climb, can be an apprentice); the apprentice on shuffled words (`ma2-apprentice`); then, each one pass of the ordered fiction at learning rate 1e-3, two seeds: **deck** (the apprentice + the pathfinder's previous-word heads with the token and position embeddings and the layers beneath), **nevermore** (the same with the Nevermore heads), **lineage** (the magician's step-500 self + its own final previous-word heads, nothing else), **lineage control** (its step-500 self left to learn), **teach** (the apprentice taught by the pathfinder, alpha 0.5, temperature 2), **control** (the apprentice left to learn). 14 runs on 8 ships, about 2.5 hours.
- **Pre-registered readings, across both seeds against the matching control:** deck and lineage pass when the previous-word star (one head or shared) reads at least half the donor's score at the first reading, the Forgotten Tomes score at step 250 is better than the control's beyond the seed spread, and the final score is no worse; nevermore passes when copy gain (`nevermore/natural_copy_gain`) at step 250 is above the control's by more than the seed spread and the final Forgotten Tomes score is no worse; teach passes when the previous-word star lights shared in fewer steps than the control, and its final score is no worse. Whatever passes is what Table Talk is grown with; if nothing passes, the trick is learnt, not handed over, and Table Talk is trained from its recipe alone.

## The speller's three tests: the 9m tracks the bettor's discoveries (7 November, 09:35, Justin)

- **Qwen 3.8 27B on the Forgotten Tomes is dropped** from the queue. No comparison against Qwen at this stage: the goal is the 9m as close as it can get to what the 100m will do.
- **Three tests at the speller's level, one leading to each discovery test** (`fleet.TRICKS`, `leads_to`; goal class speller): **the breeders' test** → the Origin test (selection changes a stock, like begets like, more are born than can live, the weakest perish in a hard winter, the hidden hare survives); **the smith's test** → the energy test (heat from motion and friction, heat passes from hot to cold, an engine stops without its fuel, perpetual motion cannot be made); **the gap test** → Mendeleev's gaps (the missing member of a row and a value between its neighbours, against the next member: Monday, Tuesday, then Thursday, so Wednesday, not Friday). 24 matched pairs each, everyday knowledge in plain 19th-century prose, many as mirror images, in `lists/tricks/{breeders,smiths,gaps}.csv`.
- **One evaluator for both levels** (`discovery`, `thorp/evaluators/discovery.py`), so the curve from the speller to the bettor is read on one scale: per pair, the bits per byte of the rival continuation less the idea's (`preference`, as the bettor's tests were pre-registered), and the same difference less what the words alone carry after an empty prompt (`lift`, so a model that merely finds "hot" commoner than "cold" scores nothing); `score` is the share of pairs with a positive lift; `floor_lift` the untrained model's lift. The speller's tests are lit at a lift of 0.1. The bettor's tests use the same evaluator once their pairs are written (`lists/discovery-<test>.csv`, read on both numbers).
- **Validation** (lift; share of pairs right): Qwen3 0.6B breeders +0.36 (0.75), smith +0.67 (0.62), gaps +0.29 (0.71); the 1m pathfinder +0.12, +0.17, −0.06; the 9m spell-vocab4k-v1 +0.09, +0.08, −0.12; untrained models within ±0.03. By the rule as written (lift at least 0.1) the 1m pathfinder lights the breeders' and smith's tests, but with only half and 58% of the pairs right: the mean is carried by a few pairs (corrected later that morning; the 9m lights none); both bet on the next member in the gap test, the failure the test was built to catch; and the 1m on ordered English fiction leads the 9m on the spelling pool on two of the three.
- **On every queued 9m run** (the 35 in the standing queue, and `scripts/make_9m_specs.py`); the finished spell-vocab4k-v1 and fl-pathfinder are scored into the ledger. Table Talk's specs will carry them.
- **The bettor's tests are read on lift too** (Justin, 09:50): `origin_test`, `energy_test`, `mendeleev_gaps` and `geometry_test` now have the metric `tricks/<key>/lift`, still lit at 0.1. This changes their pre-registration, made before any pairs were written or any bettor trained: the pass rule ("prefers the idea over every named rival by more than twice the seed range and beyond both floors") is read on lift, and `preference` stays recorded beside it. Mendeleev's gaps already had a prior correction pre-registered; lift is that correction for all of them.

## The stylist's and the 50m's tests: three chains to the bettor (7 November, 10:05, Justin)

- **Each discovery test now has a chain of four rungs**, one per class, `leads_to` linking each to the next (`fleet.TRICKS`, evaluator `discovery`, 24 matched pairs each in `lists/tricks/`, lit on lift 0.1):
  - **The speller (9m), the pieces as everyday knowledge:** the breeders' test, the smith's test, the gap test.
  - **The stylist (21m), the pieces chained into a conclusion no sentence states:** Nature's breeder (the wolves catch the slowest deer, so the deer grow swifter), the mill test (work followed through its forms: the churned water warms, the engine's work comes from the coal, the lifted water lifts less again), the between test (a member between two others takes a value between theirs, across quantities, orders and qualities).
  - **The 50m, what the precursors wrote**, the very books each bettor test's ablation removes: Malthus and Lyell (geometric and arithmetic ratios, the checks, slow causes over immense time, extinction, breeds by selection), Rumford and Davy (inexhaustible heat from boring cannon, ice melted by rubbing, heat as motion, Young's energy, the fire syringe, Oersted and Faraday), Döbereiner's triads (Dalton's atoms and proportions; the middle element's weight near the mean of its neighbours; properties changing by degrees within a family). None states the bettor's idea itself.
  - **The bettor (100m):** the Origin test, the energy test, Mendeleev's gaps.
- **Every class is scored on every rung**: the evaluator scores every written list, so each 9m run also reads its distance to the 21m and 50m rungs, and the curve is one table of classes against rungs.
- **First readings** (lift / share of pairs right), 9m spell-vocab4k-v1; 1m pathfinder; Qwen3 0.6B: breeders +0.09/0.46, +0.12/0.50, +0.36/0.75; smith +0.08/0.54, +0.17/0.58, +0.67/0.62; gap −0.12/0.50, −0.06/0.58, +0.29/0.71; Nature's breeder +0.03/0.58, +0.06/0.54, +0.26/0.62; mill +0.25/0.62, +0.21/0.54, +0.69/0.62; between −0.09/0.46, +0.07/0.62, +0.23/0.67; Malthus and Lyell +0.16/0.50, +0.28/0.71, +0.54/0.79; Rumford and Davy +0.06/0.54, +0.06/0.58, +0.18/0.58; triads −0.19/0.38, +0.20/0.54, +0.47/0.75; the untrained models within ±0.03. Qwen is ahead everywhere. The rungs are not yet graded in difficulty by these readings (the 1m does better on Malthus and Lyell than on the breeders' test), and a mean lift over 24 pairs can be carried by a few: see the open question on a share rule.

## The eternal return: the bettor's philosophy test, and its chain from the keel (7 November, 10:00, Justin)

- **The idea** (Justin: "perhaps something from Nietzsche"): Nietzsche's eternal return, chosen because it meets the discovery tests' rule. It was found independently by Blanqui (*L'Éternité par les astres*, 1872), Nietzsche (1881, published 1882) and, as a theorem of mechanics, Poincaré (1890), and it was waiting in the books: Philo in Hume's *Dialogues Concerning Natural Religion* (1779) argues that finite matter in eternal time must try every order an infinite number of times. Nietzsche's other ideas (the will to power, master and slave morality, perspectivism) have no independent co-discoverer.
- **The bettor's test** (`recurrence_test`, "The eternal return", strand philosophy, cutoff 1872, `lists/discovery-recurrence.csv` written before any bettor trains): trained only on English before 1872, does the bettor prefer "if the world is made of a finite number of parts and time has no end, every state of the world, this one included, returns, exactly and endlessly" over (a) the world running down once to a final stillness (Thomson's dissipation, 1852, the strongest rival on the shelf), (b) the world progressing for ever to new states, (c) return in kind but never in detail, (d) a creation and a final end? Ablation: without Hume's *Dialogues* and Lucretius, bytes kept. The common design and pass rule of the discovery tests apply, read on lift.
- **The shelf**: Hume's *Dialogues* (Gutenberg 4583), Marcus Aurelius (2680), Ecclesiastes (8021) pass the strict rule; Lucretius in English before 1872 (Creech, Watson, Munro) comes from the Internet Archive by publication year; Nietzsche's own works (all English translations after 1896) are never on it.
- **The chain, worked backwards to the keel**, the first chain with all six rungs (`leads_to`; 24 matched pairs each in `lists/tricks/`; lit on lift 0.1):
  - **300k, the keel: "Again and again"** (`again_test`): the words of returning: again and again, round and round, over and over, to and fro.
  - **1m, the pilot: "The refrain test"** (`refrain_test`): a refrain, a burden or a pattern that came round once comes round again, against a fresh phrase; the copying trick lifted to phrases.
  - **9m, the speller: "The seasons test"** (`seasons_test`): returning as everyday knowledge (winter comes again, the swallows return, Halley's comet is expected back) and the same cause giving the same effect.
  - **21m, the stylist: "The pigeonhole test"** (`pigeonhole_test`): the argument in small: with only so many states and no end of trials, some state must come again (twenty pigeons in nineteen holes; a seventh change on three bells); when all is as it was, what followed follows again.
  - **50m: "Hume and Lucretius"** (`lucretius_test`): what the books before 1872 say: Lucretius' eternal atoms, Philo's argument, Ecclesiastes, Marcus Aurelius, the Stoic conflagration, the Great Year, Laplace's periodic disturbances, Thomson's dissipation (the rival is on the shelf too).
  - **100m, the bettor: the eternal return.**
- **First readings** (lift / share right) on the keel pathfinder (300k), the First Light pathfinder (1m), spell-vocab4k-v1 (9m) and Qwen3 0.6B: again +0.28/0.67, +0.65/0.88, +0.62/0.88, +1.62/0.92; refrain +0.14/0.50, +0.68/0.83, +0.53/0.75, +1.93/0.96; seasons +0.18/0.62, +0.23/0.71, +0.30/0.75, +1.32/0.88; pigeonhole +0.05/0.50, −0.24/0.29, −0.05/0.46, +0.48/0.67; Hume and Lucretius +0.04/0.58, −0.22/0.29, −0.10/0.42, +0.47/0.58; untrained models within ±0.1. Unlike the first three chains this one is graded as designed: the keel's rung is the first lit, the pilot's lights at the pilot, the speller's is lit by the 1m and the 9m, and the 21m and 50m rungs are lit only by Qwen.
- **Measured on the classes it is for:** the discovery evaluator is added to the queued Laying the keel and Apprentice II runs (20 specs, none started, and their generators), as well as the 9m runs.

## The share rule for matched-pairs tests (7 November, 10:05, Justin)

- **A matched-pairs test is performed when its lift reaches the threshold (0.1) and at least two-thirds of its pairs go the idea's way** (`Trick.min_score = fleet.MIN_SHARE`, read from `tricks/<key>/score`; `fleet.performed(key, metrics)`). 16 of 24 by chance (one in two) happens about one time in 13; the rule stops a mean carried by a few pairs from counting. It applies to all 19 matched-pairs tests, the bettor's included.
- **What it changes on the ledger runs:** the keel pathfinder (300k) performs Again and again, the smith's test (+0.19, 71% right), the mill test (+0.33, 71%) and Malthus and Lyell (+0.13, 67%); the First Light pathfinder (1m) performs Again and again, the refrain test, the seasons test and Malthus and Lyell (+0.28, 71%); spell-vocab4k-v1 (9m) performs Again and again, the refrain test and the seasons test. The breeders' and smith's passes of the 1m, and the 9m's mill and Malthus passes, no longer count.
- **The eternal return chain is graded as designed under the rule** (the keel performs rung 1 only, the pilot and the speller rungs 1 to 3). **The other three chains are not:** a 300k that passes three 21m and 50m tests that a 9m fails says those pair sets have shortcuts a tiny model can take (collocations such as "hot to the touch", "geometrical ratio"), or, at 14 tests a run, chance (about one false pass expected per run). Their pairs are to be audited against the keel before they are trusted: a rung test should stay dark below its class.

## Keel and pilot rungs for the three science chains, and what each class is (7 November, 10:05, Justin)

- **Every chain now has six rungs, one per class** (`fleet.chains()`; tested). The new rungs, 24 matched pairs each in `lists/tricks/`, lift 0.1 and two-thirds of pairs right:
  - **Origin:** keel "Like father, like son" (`kin_test`: the words of kin and breeding) → pilot "The pedigree test" (`pedigree_test`: a trait named for the parents comes back in the young; copying put to inheritance) → the breeders' test.
  - **Energy:** keel "Fire and ice" (`fire_test`: the words of heat and motion) → pilot "The workshop test" (`workshop_test`: what happened to the first thing happens to the second; copying put to cause and effect) → the smith's test.
  - **Mendeleev:** keel "One, two, three" (`counting_test`: the words of order) → pilot "The period test" (`period_test`: a row that repeats with a period goes on repeating, the periodic law in miniature) → the gap test.
- **What a model at each rung is, according to its four tests** (`fleet.RUNGS`): the keel knows which words go together; the pilot copies what came before; the speller knows the everyday world; the stylist chains two facts into a third; the 50m knows what the precursors wrote; the bettor bets on the idea before it was found.
- **Readings, under the share rule** (performed rungs per chain, keel to 50m): the keel pathfinder performs Again and again, the smith's and mill tests and Malthus and Lyell, and none of its own science rungs (Like father −0.25, Fire and ice +0.14 but 62% right, One, two, three −0.26); the First Light pathfinder performs the pedigree test (+0.30, 67%), the eternal return's rungs 1 to 3 and Malthus and Lyell; spell-vocab4k-v1 performs the pedigree test (+0.26, 79%) and the eternal return's rungs 1 to 3. Qwen3 0.6B: Like father +0.61/0.71, pedigree +0.87/0.92, Fire and ice +0.83/0.58, workshop +0.90/0.83, One, two, three +0.99/0.75, period +0.83/0.83. Only the eternal return is graded as designed; the keel's passes above its class (smith, mill, Malthus and Lyell) are the audit list.

## Grading the chains, and the keel rungs rebuilt (7 November, 10:10, Justin)

- **Every chain must get harder as it climbs** (Justin: "each test must get harder as it moves up the classes of ships"). The rule (`fleet.grading`), judged on many models at once, for the rungs below the bettor's: no model performs a rung while failing one below it; no rung is performed by more models than the rung below; and some model performs every rung. The models: a **reference ladder** of one family in graded sizes (`fleet.REFERENCE_LADDER`: Pythia 14m, 31m, 70m, 160m, 410m, then Qwen3 0.6B), trained on modern text, so it measures how hard a test is, never what a Thorp class should score; and the Thorp runs. `scripts/grade_chains.py` scores it all and writes `data/compare/chain-grades.json`.
- **First grading: no chain graded.** The eternal return has no inversions but its 50m rung is beyond every model (Qwen 58% of pairs right); the Origin, energy and Mendeleev chains have 9, 3 and 5 inversions, rungs performed by more models than the rung below (the pilot's copying rungs are easy for every Pythia, which grows its induction heads early) and an unreached rung (Rumford and Davy). Pair-level profiles show why: many pairs Qwen gets wrong (ill-posed, 4 to 10 per test) and many the 14m gets right (shortcuts).
- **The keel rungs rebuilt first**, because the best-keel voyage aims at them. Their pairs had antonym rivals in the same frame ("as hot as" fire against ice) or rivals already in the prompt ("small, smaller," smallest against small, which copying favours), so even Qwen bet near chance. A keel rung now asks only which word goes with which: every rival is an unrelated word of the same kind, never in the prompt. Difficulty then climbs by the rival: unrelated at the keel, copying against a fresh phrase at the pilot, the antonym from everyday knowledge at the speller, and so on up. Rebuilt: Like father, like son; Fire and ice; One, two, three (all 24 pairs); Again and again (two pairs). Now every ladder model performs all four (Pythia 14m: lift +1.0 to +2.3, 83% to 96% right); the keel pathfinder performs three (Like father +0.70/83%, Fire and ice +0.72/88%, Again +0.34/67%) and not One, two, three (+0.01/58%); the 1m and 9m perform all four.
- **The rungs above are still to be graded**: each chain's pairs to be rewritten on the same principle (ill-posed pairs mended, shortcut pairs given rivals as natural in the local frame) and re-scored until `grading` passes. Until then the dashboard's ⚠ marks stand.

## The best keel, pre-registered (7 November, 10:10, Justin)

- **The end of the current queue** (`fleet.VOYAGES["best_keel"]`, `scripts/make_best_keel.py`, `specs/best-keel/`, queued after Apprentice II): the 300k that performs best on its four discovery rungs, Like father, like son; Fire and ice; One, two, three; Again and again.
- **Round 1**, two seeds each, one lever changed from the keel pathfinder's recipe (two passes of the English-original fiction shelf, vocabulary 2,048 on the loop pool, context 1,024, learning rate 3e-3): vocabulary 1,024 or 4,096 (at 300k the embeddings are half the model); learning rate 1e-3 or 1e-2; context 256 (the learned positions of a 1,024 window are a fifth of the model, and the rungs are short); one or four passes; the verse shelf, the mixed pool, the **nursery shelf** (`lists/keel-nursery.csv`: 214 English children's books from Gutenberg's picture-book, instructional, children's-verse, nursery-rhyme and primer shelves, every author dead by 1955) or the nursery shelf then the fiction shelf as two **stages**. 24 runs. Evaluators: ce_bpb and discovery every 500 steps, discovery every 100 (so each rung's lighting step is seen).
- **Round 2**, written by the queue itself (`--round 2`): per lever the setting whose seeds score best, all winners combined into one ship, three seeds, put at the top of the queue; then `--verdict` writes `data/runs/best-keel.md`.
- **The score:** at the last reading, the number of the four rungs performed (lift 0.1 and two-thirds of pairs right), then the weakest of the four lifts (a keel is judged by its weakest chain), then the mean lift; a ship scores its seeds' mean. The best keel is the best ship's best sailing. The keel pathfinder's reading (three of four; weakest +0.01) is the mark to beat.

## The rungs above the keel rewritten; lift measured against the frame (7 November, 10:35, Justin: "yes, rewrite them")

- **Two faults in the measurement, fixed first.** Qwen was scored from its chat turn's end token (`<|im_end|>`), which skews its bets; external models now start from `<|endoftext|>` where they have one. And lift subtracted each word's likelihood after a bare line break, a noisy prior of several nats that often outweighed the effect, and did nothing about a frame that leans one way ("the iron grew" leans to *cold*).
- **Lift is now measured against the pair's frame** (`frame` column: the closing words the answer hangs on, a suffix of the prompt): lift = the premise's effect on the bet, over the frame alone. Pairs with no frame (the keel's rungs, where which-word-goes-with-which is the test) keep the empty-prompt prior. This changes the pre-registered lift for the bettor's tests too, before any of their pairs exist: their pairs will be written as premise and frame.
- **The rule for each rung, by its rival** (`scripts/write_chain_pairs.py` writes all sixteen tests): the keel's rival is an unrelated word; the pilot's answer is copied from the premise and its rival is fresh (the refrains are now invented, so they cannot be recalled); from the speller up neither answer is in the prompt, or both are, so copying cannot decide, and the rival is the natural alternative in the frame; the stylist's premise holds the two facts to chain; the 50m's names the precursor.
- **The reference ladder gains Qwen3 1.7B and 4B** (4-bit), so a rung's reach is shown by a model that can do it: a pair the 4B gets wrong is to be mended, not kept as hard. `scripts/grade_chains.py` caches each model's scores by pair file.
- **Grading now** (models performing each rung, of 11: Pythia 14m to 410m, Qwen3 0.6B to 4B, the keel, the pilot and spell-vocab4k-v1): Origin 11, 10, 4, 1, 3; energy 11, 10, 3, 5, 3; Mendeleev 10, 9, 2, 1, 4; the eternal return 11, 10, 7, 2, 4. Every rung is reached (the 4B performs all twenty); the pilot rungs and the keel rungs are graded in every chain; the speller rungs fall away sharply (Pythia passes them from 160m or 410m, Qwen from 0.6B). **One breach remains, the same in all four chains: the 50m rung (what the precursors wrote) is performed by more models than the 21m rung (two facts chained).** For these models, recalling a named fact is easier than chaining two; the rewrite cannot fix that by pairs, only by the order of the rungs (open question to Justin).
- **Thorp's readings under the new lift:** the keel performs Like father and Fire and ice and Again and again; the pilot and spell-vocab4k-v1 perform all four keel rungs, the pedigree, workshop and refrain tests and (the 9m) the period test; no Thorp model yet performs a speller rung.

## The 21m and 50m rungs swapped; all four chains graded (7 November, 10:35, Justin: "yes, swap them")

- **The swap.** On every chain the reference ladder found recalling what the precursors wrote easier than chaining two facts, so the 21m (the stylist) now **knows what the precursors wrote** (Malthus and Lyell; Rumford and Davy; Doebereiner's triads; Hume and Lucretius) and the 50m **chains two facts into a third** (Nature's breeder; the mill test; the between test; the pigeonhole test). `leads_to`, `goal_class`, `fleet.RUNGS` and the chain stories (`fleet.CHAINS`) follow.
- **Three tests mended against the ladder:** the gap test's rival is now a far member of the row (Wednesday against Saturday, not Friday): filling is still the test, without the hardest trap for a 9m rung; the mill test's pairs that only restated the premise ("the stirring warms the water, so the water grows warmer") were rewritten to need two facts (a weight falling twice as far warms the water twice as much; a trough of water and a red-hot bar end a little warmer, not as hot as the bar); six smith's pairs that set the most tempting antonym on half-known facts got a plainly wrong rival instead.
- **All four chains are graded** (`fleet.grading` on the 11 models: Pythia 14m to 410m, Qwen3 0.6B, 1.7B and 4B, the keel, the pilot, spell-vocab4k-v1), models performing each rung from the keel to the 50m: Origin 11, 10, 4, 3, 1; energy 11, 10, 5, 3, 2; Mendeleev 10, 9, 7, 4, 1; the eternal return 11, 10, 7, 4, 2. No inversions; every rung within reach of Qwen3 4B.
- **Caveats.** The pairs were mended while looking at the same ladder that grades them, so the grading is a calibration, not an independent test; Thorp's runs, which no pair was written against, are the measurement. The energy chain is graded at the margin (two models are one pair from the threshold), and the ladder will be re-run whenever pairs change.
- **Thorp's readings:** the keel performs the keel rung of three chains (not One, two, three); the pilot performs the keel and pilot rungs of Origin, energy and the eternal return and the keel rung of Mendeleev; spell-vocab4k-v1 performs the keel and pilot rungs of all four and the gap test, its first speller rung.

## The leaderboard, the shelves, and the two-fact problem (7 November, 10:55, Justin)

- **The ladder's first major voyage is the climb to chaining two facts** (`fleet.VOYAGES["two_facts"]`, "The two-fact problem"): every class must climb closer to the four 50m rungs (two facts chained) than the class beneath, so that the 50m can attempt them; even if the 50m fails, the climb is the result.
- **The leaderboard** (`thorp/leaderboard.py`, `payload()` for the dashboard): per run, its **climb** (0 to 5: for each chain the rungs performed from the bottom without a gap, plus the next rung's lift as a share of its threshold, capped below 1; the mean of the four chains) and its **chaining lift** (the mean lift on the four 50m rungs). **Best in class**: the highest climb (ties: the chaining lift). A class whose best does not climb higher than the class below is a **stalled climb**; the chaining lift is shown but counts only once it clears the noise (0.03; the untrained models lie within about that of zero). The reference ladder is listed beside: Pythia 14m 2.52, 31m 2.50, 70m 2.79, 160m 3.52, 410m 4.24; Qwen3 0.6B 4.72, 1.7B 4.84, 4B 5.00 (chaining lift +0.54). Thorp so far: the keel 1.18, the pilot 2.17, the speller 2.25 (finished runs are being scored on the chains).
- **The shelves** (`thorp/shelves.py`, `payload()`): every list in `lists/`, its name, kind (training, deck, probe, draft, other), what it is for, rows, books matched in the catalogue and fetched, bytes and a token estimate, families, and the runs and queued specs that read, refuse, score on or train a tokeniser on it.
- **Round 1, the baseline, pre-registered** (`scripts/make_two_facts.py --baseline`, `specs/two-facts/`, **top of the queue**): the keel, the pilot, the speller and the stylist with what is known today, two seeds each, one reading for all (four passes of the English-original fiction shelf, about 190M tokens), one tokeniser for the pilot, speller and stylist (4,096 on the loop pool; the keel 2,048), each class's best-known learning rate (3e-3, 2e-3, 1e-3; the stylist 8e-4, its first run on this Mac). The chains every 100 or 250 steps, the full evaluators every 500. About 16 hours, most of it the two stylists (estimated, never measured). **Expectation:** the climb rises with the class (keel < pilot < speller < stylist); the chaining lift stays within noise below the stylist; a stylist chaining lift above 0.03 would be the first sign of the trick.

## Smoking Qwen, pre-registered (7 November, 11:10, Justin)

- **The claim:** Thorp chains two facts at an order of magnitude below Qwen3 4B: a Thorp of about 400M parameters (a tenth of Qwen3 4B's 4.0B) reaches Qwen3 4B's chaining lift (+0.538, the mean lift on the four 50m rungs). The Mac cannot train 400M, so the proof is a forecast from Thorp's own classes, keel to 50m (`thorp/smoking_qwen.py`, `payload()` for the dashboard).
- **Method:** x = log10 of parameters (counted from the built model), y = chaining lift of each class's best ship (its seeds together); two curves, `linear` and `bending` (quadratic in x); a bootstrap of 1,000 draws resampling the 96 chaining pairs and the seeds; the forecast at 400M with its 5th to 95th percentile band, and P(smoke), the share of draws at or above Qwen3 4B's mark.
- **Backtest, run first, where the answer is known:** Pythia fitted on 14m to 160m predicts 410m at +0.004 (linear, band −0.049 to +0.053; actual +0.077: missed, too low) and +0.143 (bending, band +0.017 to +0.264: inside). Qwen3 fitted on 0.6B and 1.7B predicts 4B at +0.202 (linear, band +0.044 to +0.381; actual +0.538: missed, too low). Chaining lift bends upward with size: the straight line underestimates, so it is the conservative curve; the bending curve has held where it could be tested.
- **Forecastable only when three Thorp classes have a chaining lift above the noise (0.03).** Today none does (keel −0.013, pilot −0.056, speller −0.021): a curve through noise extrapolates nothing, so no probability is claimed (the dashboard shows the curve and "not yet forecastable"). The points that will make it forecastable are the stylist (the two-fact baseline), the 50m (one to two days on this Mac) and, if needed, a 100m (five to eight days).
- **The rule for the claim:** **Smoking Qwen** when forecastable, the backtest still holds, and P(smoke) is at least 0.9 on the linear curve; **probable** when that holds on the bending curve only. Confirmation, when a GPU backend exists: train the 400M itself (compute-optimal, about 8B tokens, roughly 2e19 operations, on the order of 15 H100-hours).

## The leaderboard is public: chaining tests only (7 November, 11:35, Justin)

- **The leaderboard shows only the four chaining tests** (Nature's breeder, the mill test, the between test, the pigeonhole test): the one skill set against the outside models. Every other rung is Thorp's internal measure and stays off it (Justin: "our own internal measure and not public"). `leaderboard.payload()` gives, per ship and per outside model, the chaining lift, tests passed (of 4) and each test's lift and share; `leaderboard.internal()` keeps the climb for the two-fact voyage's page.
- **Best in class is a ship, not a run**: its sailings (seeds, grouped by `fleet.ship_key`) averaged, and only ships of two sailings or more where the class has any, so the luckiest of fifty single runs is never taken for a result. Today every class's best is within the noise (keel +0.012, pilot −0.016, speller −0.028), as expected below the stylist.
- **The competitors** (`fleet.COMPETITORS`, 2B to 4B, scored on the same pairs, never used to grade the chains): Qwen3 4B +0.538 (4 of 4), Phi-4-mini +0.348 (4), Llama 3.2 3B +0.285 (3), Gemma 3 4B +0.254 (2), SmolLM3 3B +0.253 (4), Gemma 2 2B +0.220 (1), Phi-2 +0.202 (3). Smoking Qwen now forecasts each at a tenth of its size; the range is smoked when every one is.
- **Two queue faults found and fixed:** the two-fact baseline's eight runs exited at once (11:12) because their log folder did not exist; the folder (and the best keel's) was made and the baseline re-queued at the top, where it is now running.

## The logic deck and the three cards, built (7 November, 11:50, Justin: "build them")

- **The logic deck** (`scripts/make_logic_deck.py` → `lists/tricks/logic.csv`; evaluator `logic`; trick `logic_test`, goal class 50m): 40 items across order, cause, membership, proportion and counterfactual, 232 rows, built from invented words (checked against the loop pool's lexicon) or premises that run against the world, with both answers in the premises wherever the form allows. Variants: both premises, first only, second only, swapped, padded (an irrelevant premise last, naming its own middle term twice), one hop, three hops. **The chaining gain** per item is the two-premise lift less the better single-premise lift; the trick is performed at a mean gain of 0.1 with two-thirds of items positive. It sits beside the four chaining tests on the leaderboard as their proof.
- **The cards** (stars until the rename; evaluator `cards`): **mover** (attention from the conclusion's last word to the premise's answer word less to its rival; lit at 0.2), **bridge** (the logit lens's preference for the middle term over the irrelevant one at the conclusion, read twice with the two names exchanged so each word's prior cancels; lit at 0.5 nats, provisional), **comparator** (attention to the year to be exceeded less to a decoy year; lit at 0.3, provisional: the target year is nearer the question than the decoy, so recency may help; a behavioural greater-than check is to follow).
- **Two design faults caught on the first readings and fixed:** the bridge first read 0.4 to 0.7 nats on models that do not chain, because the bridge was named twice and recently and the distractor once and early; then still 0.7 after that was reversed, because the invented words' first pieces differ in prior; counterbalanced by exchanging the names, it reads about zero. The comparator first measured attention to a number as such; it now subtracts a decoy.
- **First readings** (keel / pilot / speller): chaining gain −0.02 / −0.05 / −0.09 (untrained floors about −0.02 to −0.03): no Thorp model chains; the hop curve falls as it should (one hop +0.03 / +0.15 / +0.18, two +0.04 / +0.08 / +0.11, three +0.01 / +0.03 / −0.04); mover about 0, bridge about 0, comparator 0.26 / 0.23 / 0.22 (one head).
- **Added to the queued two-fact and best-keel specs** (logic and cards at every full reading, logic at every light one); the two keel baseline runs, started before, will be re-scored.

## The cards on every model; the two-fact problem, round 2: the diet (7 November, 12:30, Justin)

- **Every card is now read on every model** (`thorp/inside.py` opens attention and the residual stream of any MLX-LM model; `cards.copying_cards` reads previous-word and the Nevermore with each model's own tokeniser; it agrees with our ledger on the pilot: previous-word 0.34 against 0.343, the Nevermore 0.125 against 0.134). Gemma 2's attention cannot be read this way (its own soft-capped attention), so only its bridge is read.
- **The cards judged** (`thorp/card_check.py`, chainers: 3 or 4 chaining tests; non-chainers: none): previous-word, the Nevermore, the mover and the comparator are each lit on all 5 chainers: **necessary**; none is a marker, since the Pythias (all five sizes have the Nevermore; 31m to 410m the mover) light them and do not chain. The bridge is lit on 2 of 5 chainers: neither, and not trusted. The Nevermore measure is sound: Pythia 410m reads 0.83 (copy gain 3.28). **Thorp holds previous-word and the comparator and lacks the Nevermore (pilot 0.13) and the mover (0.01)**: the two necessary cards round 2 aims at. Necessary, not sufficient: the Pythias hold all four and chain nothing, so the diet must teach the trick as well as draw the cards.
- **Tokens alone do not buy chaining at small sizes**: Pythia 410m (300B tokens) reaches a chaining lift of +0.077, Pythia 70m −0.037. So the pilot and speller climb by a diet aimed at the trick, not by reading more.
- **Round 2, pre-registered** (`scripts/make_two_facts.py --round2`, then `--pick`; queued after the baseline's pilot runs): on the pilot, two seeds each, against the baseline pilot: **rotary positions** (`model.positions: rope`, new: the queries and keys rotated, no position table; the relative skill a Nevermore needs), **the reasoning shelf** as a late stage (`lists/reasoning-shelf.csv`: 39 books, about 4.9M tokens, of logic, geometry, Plato's dialogues, Descartes, Bacon, Berkeley and Faraday's *Candle*; the books behind the chaining tests themselves left out: Darwin, Wallace, Huxley on evolution, Tyndall, the non-Euclidean geometers, perpetual motion), read three times after three passes of the fiction shelf, **the conclusion-weighted tack** (`copy_weight: {alpha: 3, kind: conclusion}`: the eight pieces after "so", "therefore", "hence"... weigh four times; only whole-word markers count, four in a 4,096 vocabulary), and **all three**. Cards and chains read every 200 steps. **The pick**, automatic: the lever whose two seeds score best on chaining lift plus logic gain is carried to the speller, two seeds, after the speller's baseline; if none beats the baseline pilot, the speller gets all three. **The goal:** a speller out of the noise (chaining lift above +0.03 on two seeds), the Nevermore and the mover lit; the stretch, beating Pythia 410m's +0.077. Books only: no generated syllogisms (Justin).

## Two boards: chaining and approaching (7 November, 12:50, Justin)

- **The leaderboard is two boards in one table** (`leaderboard.payload()["table"]`): **chaining**, every model or Thorp version whose chaining lift clears the noise (+0.03; a Thorp ship on two sailings or more), ranked by chaining lift; and **approaching**, everything below the line, which cannot be ranked by a lift that is noise, ranked instead by how close it is: the **necessary cards held** (previous-word, the Nevermore, the mover, the comparator; every model that chains holds all four), then the **one-hop lift** on the logic deck (following one stated premise, the step before two). Justin: "I like the distinction between not chaining and chaining."
- **Today:** chaining, Qwen3 4B to Qwen3 0.6B (ten models); approaching, Pythia 70m (4 of 4 cards, one-hop +0.30), Pythia 31m, Pythia 160m, Pythia 14m (3 of 4), then Thorp 1m v1 (1 of 4: the comparator; one-hop +0.05), Thorp 9m v1 (1 of 4), Thorp 300k v1 (0 of 4). The Thorp versions climb this board by drawing the Nevermore and the mover: round 2's aim.

## The card census, pre-registered (7 November, 13:00, Justin)

- **The experiment** (Justin): "run the 300k with as many variations to see how many of the cards are possible at 300k. If the cards aren't possible at the 300k then we need to know. Then the same for 1m, and once we have a model that has all the cards, what we need to do to start chaining." (`fleet.VOYAGES["card_census"]`, `scripts/make_card_census.py`, `specs/cards/`; at the top of the queue.)
- **The cards:** the four necessary ones (previous-word, the Nevermore, the mover, the comparator; every model that chains holds all four). Read every 100 steps (the 1m every 200), so a card that lights and fades is still seen.
- **The 300k, 17 variations, one seed each** (the question is possibility; a card lit gets a second seed to confirm): the two-fact baseline (fiction ×4); rotary positions; copy-weighted (induction, α 3); rotary + copy; conclusion-weighted; the reasoning shelf as a late stage; learning rate 1e-2 and 1e-3; 8 passes; verse ×8; the nursery ×8; four heads of 16 (`300k-h4`); three layers (`300k-l3`); vocabulary 1,024; rotary + vocabulary 1,024 (two-thirds of the 300k is embeddings: these give the layers more of it); everything for copying (rotary + copy + verse ×8); everything (rotary, conclusion, reasoning; the conclusion weighting takes the one weighting slot). Then `--summary 300k` writes `data/runs/cards/census-300k.md`: per card, the best reading, the run and step, and **possible** or **not seen**.
- **The 1m, 21 variations:** round 2's four (two seeds each, already queued) and 13 more (the same levers; `1m-h8`, `1m-l6`; vocabulary 2,048), then `--summary 1m`. The two-fact pick, the speller's and the stylist's runs follow.

## Fitouts, the cause-and-effect shelf, and cause on the leaderboard (7 November, 13:15, Justin)

- **Qwen3 4B's logic deck, by relation** (after the deck's fixes): cause and effect +0.94 (7 of 8 items; both premises far above either alone), every other relation within ±0.1 (order +0.06, counterfactual +0.06, proportion −0.01, membership −0.09); its bridge 0.10 at layer 19 of 36. Read: Qwen3 4B chains causal premises ("when X, then Y") and not the syllogism, the transitive order or the product on invented words, so part of its domain chaining rests on knowledge (the content effect reported for language models), and the bridge card reads the middle noun when a causal chain carries the middle event: the bridge stays untrusted until it reads the event (or a tuned lens).
- **The cause-and-effect shelf** (`lists/cause-shelf.csv`, 95 books, about 13M tokens): period how-to books (cookery and household management, gardening, first aid and home remedies, handicraft and woodwork, games and their rules, scouting and camping), up to seven per kind, non-fiction only; no engines, heat or mechanics, no animal breeding, no chemistry of the elements (the chaining tests' own subjects). Folded into the reasoning shelf too (now 134 books), before any run read it.
- **Cause and effect is a leaderboard measure**: `table[i]["cause_gain"]`, the logic deck's chaining gain on its cause items (Qwen3 4B +0.94, Qwen3 1.7B +0.34, Phi-4-mini +0.25, the Pythias and Thorp about zero). Eight items: a strong reading counts, a small one is noise.
- **A fitout** (`fleet.Fitout`, `fleet.FITOUTS`, `scripts/make_fitout.py`, the Shelves tab via `shelves.fitouts()`): a hand-curated plan of training material matched to pretraining (stages, one run, one schedule) and post-training (stages, each a run seeded from the one before at its own learning rate and tack). Distinct from `fitout_of()`, a ship's identity: a Fitout compiles to ships. Seeding now carries rotary models (`growth.grow` skips the absent position table; the seed must share the position kind).
- **The first fitout, "Thorp 300k: the four cards, leaning on cause and effect"** (`keel-cards-cause`; rotary, vocabulary 2,048): pretraining nursery ×2, verse ×2, fiction ×2, cause ×3 with the copy-weighted tack (induction, α 3); post-training the reasoning shelf ×2 then cause ×2, each at 1e-3 with the conclusion-weighted tack (α 3). About 224M tokens, 65M of them cause and effect. Two seeds, queued straight after the 300k card census, so it is read beside the census's single levers.

## The card census is run around the fitout (7 November, 13:20, Justin)

- Justin: "the card census and the cause score should run with the same fitout or small variations of it, so we have good data to compare." The census of each class is now an ablation study of that class's fitout: the fitout itself on two seeds, then 15 variations of it, one seed each, every stage (pretraining, then post-training on the reasoning shelf, then on cause again), each removing or changing one piece: learned positions; no copy-weighted tack; no nursery; no verse; no cause in pretraining; no reasoning post-training; no cause post-training; fiction only in pretraining; more, smaller heads; one more layer; the smaller vocabulary; a higher and a lower pretraining learning rate; twice the reading; every cause stage doubled. A variation's stage identical to the fitout's own reuses that run (`scripts/make_fitout.py <key> --ablations`; the runs of each variation in `data/runs/cards/fitout-census-<key>.json`), so no run is trained twice.
- **The summary** (`scripts/make_card_census.py --fitout <key>`, `data/runs/cards/fitout-census-<key>.md`): per variation, its last stage's four cards (lit or not) and its cause gain with the change from the fitout's own; per stage in the readings file.
- **The 1m's fitout** (`pilot-cards-cause`): the keel's, one class up (vocabulary 4,096; post-training at 7e-4), with the same 15 variations (four heads become eight, three layers become six). The single-lever censuses (`specs/cards/`, none run) are taken off the queue.
- **Order:** the keel's fitout census (46 runs, to about 17:15), round 2 of the two-fact problem on the pilot (8 runs), the pilot's fitout census (46 runs, to about 10:30 tomorrow), the pick, then the speller and the stylist.

## The knockout, pre-registered (7 November, 13:30, Justin)

- **The question:** is the hypothesis that a model needs the four necessary cards before it can chain bulletproof? No: the evidence is co-occurrence (five chainers, all holding all four), no model has yet been seen chaining without a card, the cards are definitions with thresholds we chose, and Qwen3 1.7B already chains cause and effect (+0.34) with its mover just under the mark (0.20). The Nevermore's case is the strongest (induction heads were shown necessary for in-context learning by ablation); the mover is a hypothesis carried from another circuit; the comparator should matter only for order and quantity.
- **The voyage** (`fleet.VOYAGES["knockout"]`, `thorp/knockout.py`; outside models only, off the training queue): in a model that chains, each card's 8 strongest heads are silenced (their outputs zeroed on every forward pass, `Inside.silenced`) and the logic deck and the four chaining tests are read again; three draws of 8 random heads from no card are the control. The baseline runs through the same explicit attention with nothing silenced (the explicit path in float32 differs from MLX-LM's fused kernel by up to 0.17 nats, the same for every condition).
- **The rule:** a card is **necessary** in a model when silencing it lowers the cause gain (the comparator: the order gain) by at least 0.1 and by more than twice the largest drop any random draw causes; otherwise **not shown**. First on Qwen3 4B (cause +0.94), then the other four chainers.
- **Qwen3 4B, the first knockout (13:44):** no card shown necessary. Silencing each card's 8 strongest heads left the cause gain where it was (baseline +0.92; previous-word +0.89, the Nevermore +0.93, the mover +0.94; the comparator's order gain +0.07 → +0.09), while one draw of 8 random heads cut it to +0.66 (−0.26; the others −0.06 and −0.07). Read: in a 4B with 1,152 heads, 8 is under 1% and each card's job may be spread and redundant; the random draw that hurt (its heads in the result file) is the best lead on where Qwen's chaining lives. Next: the other four chainers, a dose-response (16, 32, 64 heads per card against random sets of the same size), and the damaging draw's heads silenced one at a time. Until then the necessary-cards hypothesis stands as co-occurrence only.

## The trigram engine: fitouts built in the UI, aimed at an induction head (7 November, 13:55, Justin)

- **The knockout, all five chainers:** no card shown necessary in any (Phi-4-mini closest: previous-word and the Nevermore cost 0.17 and 0.18 of its cause gain against 0.14 for a random draw). SmolLM3 (cause −0.02), Llama 3.2 (+0.06) and Phi-2 (+0.15) barely chain cause and effect on the deck, so their knockouts cannot show much; Qwen3 4B and Phi-4-mini are the real tests.
- **Justin's change of direction:** induction heads grow from text whose trigrams come back within the window (Olsson et al. 2022; in-context Markov chains, Edelman et al. 2024), so a fitout at the Pareto frontier of trigram repetition should give a 300k its Nevermore. Three categories: a **repetition engine** (procedural text, formulaic dialogue), a **causal skeleton** (legal and scientific text), and **narrative**.
- **The measure** (`thorp/trigrams.py`, cached in `data/trigrams.csv`): per book, the share of word trigrams already seen within the previous 1,024 words (`repeat_rate`) and distinct trigrams over all (`variety`); the **Pareto frontier**, the books no other beats on both. The shelves: nursery 8.1% (63 frontier books of 213), reasoning 6.7%, cause 6.4%, verse 2.9%, the mixed pool 2.7%, English-original fiction 2.5%. The 300k fitout's largest stage (fiction, 95M of 224M tokens) repeats least.
- **Fitouts made in the UI** (`thorp/fitouts.py`): saved as `data/fitouts/<key>.json`; each stage a shelf, a token amount, a **selection** (all, random, highest repetition, Pareto frontier first) and passes, in pretraining or post-training; `compile_for(key, preset, seed)` builds it for any class (the chosen books fixed in `lists/fitouts/<key>/`); `queue(key, preset, seeds)` puts it in the standing queue.
- **The first, "Trigram engine (period)"** (`trigram-engine-period`, rotary, copy-weighted): the repetition engine (the nursery's frontier, 6M tokens ×4; the cause shelf by repetition, 6M ×4), the causal skeleton (the reasoning shelf's frontier, 8M ×3) and narrative (the most repetitive fiction, 20M ×2): about 112M tokens. Queued on the 300k, two seeds, at the top, ahead of the census. **The goal:** the 300k's first Nevermore (natural score 0.3, previously 0.00 to 0.01 at 300k).
- **Open:** Justin's modern sources (WikiHow, recipes, film dialogue, CUAD, Pile of Law, EDGAR, arXiv, Wikipedia) break the pre-1930 rule; period equivalents exist for each category; a fenced modern track is his call.
- **Period only (Justin, 14:35):** no modern sources (WikiHow, film dialogue, CUAD, Pile of Law, EDGAR, arXiv, Wikipedia). Every fitout reads pre-1930 public-domain English; Justin's three categories are filled from the period: the repetition engine (the nursery, the cause-and-effect shelf, and a formulaic-dialogue shelf), the causal skeleton (the reasoning shelf and a legal shelf), narrative (the fiction shelves).

## Discovering the induction head, pre-registered (7 November, 14:40, Justin: "put it at the top of the queue")

- **The voyage** (`fleet.VOYAGES["induction"]`): the first Thorp to grow an induction head, the 300k first, fed text at the Pareto frontier of in-window trigram repetition, period books only.
- **Round 1, at the top of the queue:** the trigram engine (`trigram-engine-period`: the nursery's frontier 6M ×4, the cause shelf by repetition 6M ×4, the reasoning shelf's frontier 8M ×3, the most repetitive fiction 20M ×2; rotary; copy-weighted) and its **random twin** (`trigram-engine-period-random`: the same shelves, amounts and passes, books chosen at random), two seeds each, the Nevermore read every 100 steps.
- **The rule:** the voyage succeeds when a 300k lights the Nevermore (natural score at least 0.3) on two seeds; repetition is shown to be what did it when the trigram engine lights it and its random twin does not (or the engine's Nevermore beats the twin's by more than the seeds' spread). Earlier 300k Nevermores: 0.00 to 0.01.

## The ship's stores (7 November, 15:55, Justin)

- **The rule:** "runs can only occur on two canonical stores: one optimised for prose and one optimised for trigrams. All runs have to use one or the other. No other data unless explicitly opted in."
- **The stores** (`scripts/make_stores.py`, frozen in `data/stores.json` with each list's hash): the **prose stores** (`lists/stores/prose.csv`, 425 books, 37M tokens: fiction at or above the median name density, ranked by names recurring within the window) and the **trigram stores** (`lists/stores/trigram.csv`, 463 books, 60M tokens: everything else with variety at least 0.6, ranked by in-window trigram repetition). Both leave out the held-out decks and the books behind the chaining tests. The prose stores fall short of the 150M aimed at: too little fetched fiction clears the name floor.
- **The opt-in:** a run may read anything else with `data.opt_in` set to the reason, in words, logged with the run. Runs already in the ledger continue as they were.
- **Enforced from 16:11 (Justin: "enforce, let the queue finish").** The 178 runs then in the queue are excepted by name, and so are the follow-ups the queue writes itself when it gets there (the speller pick `tf-r2-speller-*` and the best keel round 2 `kb-best-s*`), in `data/policy-exceptions.json` with the reason. Every other new run reads the stores or opts out in words.
- **What counts as reading the stores:** a list whose every book is in the stores (a fitout stage that takes part of one, or of both) reads the stores; the rule governs the books, not the file name. The stores themselves are hash-checked against `data/stores.json`.
- **The fitout builder checks the gate before queueing** (`fitouts.queue`): a fitout the gate would refuse is not queued at all, and the refusal comes back to the UI.

## Thorp as a viable system (7 November, 16:00, Justin: "how do those types of rules get governed? … implement Beer's VSM … the S1 to S5 units encoded into its way of operating")

- **The map** (`thorp/vsm.py`, `SYSTEMS`): **S1 operations** are the voyages and their runs; **S2 coordination** is the one GPU, the standing queue, run ids, log folders, seeding order and the two sessions sharing files; **S3 control** is the gate every new run passes in `train.prepare`, with the opt-in bargain; **S3\* audit** is `python -m thorp.vsm audit`, over the queue and the ledger, independent of the gate; **S4 intelligence** is the competitors, the reference ladder, the leaderboard, Smoking Qwen and the knockout; **S5 policy** is `POLICIES`, each a statement adopted by Justin on a date, enforced or advisory, switched in `data/policies.json`, with written exceptions by run name or pattern in `data/policy-exceptions.json`, recorded here.
- **The policies today:** *decks* (no held-out book in a list a run reads; enforced), *period* (pre-1930 English, every Gutenberg author dead by 1955; enforced), *stores* (the ship's stores or a written opt-in; advisory until Justin decides), *voyage* (every run belongs to a voyage written up here; advisory). The stores rule's old switch (`data/stores-rule.json`) is now S5's switch for *stores*.
- **How a rule is governed from now on:** Justin adopts it (S5, an entry here and a `Policy`), S3 enforces it at the gate or reports it in the audit, S2's lints catch what would collide before it costs a run, and the audit runs on every night watch. A policy changes only by a new entry here.
- **The first audit (16:00):** S2 found one run queued without its parent (`fo-keel-cards-cause~no-rope-post1-s1`, whose pretraining run failed at 13:27 on the missing log folder; the parent is re-queued before it). The *decks* check was made substantive (does the run read a held-out book, not does it list every deck): 35 older runs list two of the three decks but read none of the Forgotten Tomes, so they pass. 89 fitout-census runs were filed under no voyage; they are the card census's. 35 older experiments (spelling, levers, content) still belong to no voyage, advisory. Period check: all 185 queued runs pass.

## Expeditions, voyages and the library (7 November, 16:20, Justin: "the voyages should become expeditions and a ship's runs should become voyages … each run should be able to tell me which library was onboard … the shelves concept is renamed to library")

- **The words** (`fleet.TERMS`): an **expedition** is a line of experiments with one goal, its levers and its stop rule (`fleet.EXPEDITIONS`, `fleet.Expedition`, `thorp/expeditions.py`); a **voyage** is one ship's run (one spec trained once, its run id in the ledger); a **library** is a book list Thorp reads from, scores on or refuses (`thorp/library.py`), and the ship's two stores are the canonical libraries. Older entries here use the old words.
- **Kept under the old names, as aliases**, so the standing queue (a fresh process per voyage) and the dashboard keep working while they move: `fleet.VOYAGES`, `fleet.Voyage`, `fleet.voyage_of_label`, `thorp.voyages`, `thorp.shelves`. The ledger's `runs` table, `run_id`, `data/runs/` and `specs/` keep their names (renaming them under a running queue risks the voyages at sea); the ledger has a `voyages` view over `runs`. The S5 policy *voyage* is now *expedition*.
- **The library aboard:** every voyage from 16:30 records a `library` artifact when it sails (`library.carried(spec)`): each library it reads, by name and stage, whether it is one of the stores, drawn from them or outside them, its books and its content hash. `library.aboard(run_id)` gives that with the books read and their tokens (the `recipe` artifact); for voyages before 16:30 it is worked out from the spec and marked `recorded: false`.
- **The Expeditions page's head** (`expeditions.overview()`, Justin 16:25): the voyage at sea, each expedition's voyages at sea, finished, failed and queued with the place of its next in the queue, and the queue's length and rough time left (each queued voyage at its class's mean wall time). At 16:28: 176 voyages and 5 steps queued, about 48 hours; the card census at sea, 81 of its voyages queued.

## One voyage at sea: S2 takes the helm (7 November, 16:35, Justin: "there's 5 expeditions running. Surely only one can be running at a time ... this should be coordinated by S2")

- **Expeditions can be under way together; only one voyage is at sea.** "Running" on an expedition was a declared status set by hand, so five said it at once. The machine has one GPU and the queue sails one voyage at a time, but nothing stopped a second job (an evaluation, a competitor score, a hand-started run) from going to sea beside it, which slows both and spoils their wall times.
- **The helm** (`vsm.at_sea(name)`, a lock at `data/helm.lock`, the holder in `data/helm.json`): every voyage (`train.run`) takes it before it sails and gives it up when it comes home; a second voyage waits in port and says so in its log. `vsm.helm()` says who is at sea.
- **An expedition's state is S2's, worked out from the helm and the queue** (`vsm.expedition_states()`), not set by hand: *at sea* (its voyage holds the helm; at most one), *under way* (voyages queued), *in port* (planned), *home* (done), *paused*, and *awaiting orders* (declared running with nothing at sea or queued: stalled, or finished and not closed). At 16:35: card census at sea; two facts, First Light, seedling, Apprentice II and best keel under way; induction and knockout awaiting orders.
