- **S2's lint, lost at sea:** a voyage the ledger has running whose process holds no helm and is not alive (a crash) is reported in the audit. The voyage at sea when the helm was added (launched before it) is recognised by its live process.

## No voyage without a named fitout (7 November, 18:10, Justin: "for the others assign them a fitout, no run occurs without a named fitout ... use the beer vsm system to ensure that the rule flows through")

- **S5, policy `fitout`** (enforced): every spec names the fitout it is a variation of, `fitout: {key, variation}`, and that fitout exists (`fleet.FITOUTS` or `data/fitouts/`). `fitout` is a label (`train.NOT_HASHED`), so naming it moves no run id.
- **The assignment** (Justin: "align them with the 2 fitouts we currently have and just variations of those 2"): a voyage compiled from a fitout is that fitout, its census variation from `~<variation>`; every other voyage is a variation of one of the two four-cards fitouts, the keel's (`keel-cards-cause`) for the 300k and the pilot's (`pilot-cards-cause`) for the 1m and up, the variation its name less the seed (`fleet.assign_fitout`). `scripts/assign_fitouts.py` wrote it into every queued spec: 42 the keel's, 117 the pilot's.
- **How it flows:** S3's gate refuses a spec without one; S2's lint flags a queued spec without one before it sails; S3\*'s audit counts queued voyages breaking it (0); the generators the queue runs itself (`make_two_facts.py --pick`, `make_best_keel.py --round 2`) and both fitout builders write it (`fleet.with_fitout`). The queue table has a fitout column (its variation and stage).

## The ledger swap (7 November, 18:11 to 18:20)

- A `git stash` (to compare test results) swapped the tracked `ledger.sqlite` for the committed copy while a voyage was at sea; the queue read the committed `queue.txt` and re-sailed finished census voyages against it for two minutes, writing to the journal. The ledger was rebuilt from the file before the swap, the journal's four commits from before it, and SQLite's `.recover`: integrity ok, 92 runs, every run's rows matching its siblings'; lost only part of `fo-keel-cards-cause~deeper-pre-s1`, marked lost at sea and re-sailed first. The gap's false re-runs are commented in `queue-done.txt`; the damaged files are kept outside the repo.
- **Justin: move the ledger to Postgres** ("it's the best longer term choice"), its data outside the repository. Until then, no git command that touches `ledger.sqlite` while a voyage is at sea.

## Cleaning: the text as Thorp reads it (7 November, 18:45, Justin: "do it")

- **Why:** Gutenberg prints books hard-wrapped at about 70 characters and `gutenberg.strip` kept every line: a line break every eight words, 56% of them inside a sentence, about 8% of the pieces a 2,048-vocabulary model predicts. The best 300k's commonest guess after almost anything was the line break.
- **Cleaning versions** (`thorp/cleaning.py`): `raw` (the source's text; every voyage before this, and any spec that names none) and **`unwrap-v1`**: per book a wrap width (the 90th percentile of its line lengths); a paragraph is prose when its lines mostly reach that width and at least 30% carry on with a lowercase word; in prose a line reaching 60% of the width joins the next (a word hyphen at the margin joins whole), a shorter line keeps its break; verse, lists, tables and headings are kept line for line; blank-line runs become one paragraph break. Measured: prose stores 9 → 24 words per break (breaks inside paragraphs 62% → 3%), reasoning 55% → 1%, cause 56% → 9%, verse kept (75% → 73%), nursery rhymes and primers kept. Known miss: a few prose reports half unwrap.
- **Where it is applied:** at read time, in the one reader of book text (`sources.load_text(book, clean)`); the source's raw text stays the record. The spec names it (`data.clean`, hashed: a voyage on clean text is a different voyage); it is a tokeniser setting, so a cleaned tokeniser and its shards live apart from the raw ones (raw moves no id); the held-out decks are encoded with the voyage's tokeniser, so they are cleaned the same way; bits per byte divides by the bytes of the text as read (`Tokeniser.text_bytes`). The tokeniser trainer's corpus cuts lines over 8,000 characters at spaces (SentencePiece silently skips a sentence over 16,384; an unwrapped paragraph is one line). The cards' natural book and the laws stay raw: they are instruments.
- **The VSM:** S5 policy `clean` (enforced, opt-out in words): new voyages read the current version; S3's gate refuses one that does not; S2 lints a voyage seeded from one that read different text (a model carries its tokeniser); S3\* `clean_audit()` checks on the two stores that cleaning still does its job and is deterministic; S4 owns whether a better cleaning is worth a new version. The 158 voyages queued before the rule are excepted by name, as the stores rule was at 16:11 ("let the queue finish"), with the two follow-up patterns; the two four-cards fitouts stay `raw` (`Fitout.clean`), and every new fitout reads `unwrap-v1`.
- **Comparisons:** a voyage on clean text is not compared with a raw one on bits per byte; the first clean voyages should be a fitout's own two seeds, a new baseline.

## Acquisition as a viable system, and S3 built out (7 November, 19:00, Justin: "let's build out s3 in the UI and then have the cleaning and aquiring of books built out as per beers VSM and then I want to be able to see that in the UI")

- **Acquisition is the second operation in Thorp's S1** (the voyages are the first): the books the voyages read, a viable system one level down (Beer's recursion) with its own five systems in `thorp/acquisition.py`. **S1**, intakes: `thorp acquire LIST [--dry-run] [--limit N]` puts every row through the intake gate before anything is fetched, then fetches, registers and checks what is admitted; each intake is recorded in `data/acquisition/intakes/`, and each book's verdict in `data/acquisition/log.jsonl`. **S2**: one intake at a time (the intake lock, `data/acquisition/intake.lock`); acquisition never takes the GPU's helm, so an intake can run beside a voyage at sea; one request every two seconds per host; one cache per source; one writer of the manifest. **S3**: the intake gate (`admit`), and the library audit (S3\*, `audit()`, `python -m thorp.acquisition audit`). **S4**: the sources beyond Gutenberg and the legal ceiling (`SOURCES_KNOWN`, `ceiling()`, from `plans/corpus-ceiling.md`). **S5**: the rules.
- **The rules either serve an adopted policy or are proposed.** Serving `period`: *english* (a book read is English; a held-out deck may not be), *dead-by-1955* (every named person, translators and editors included), *printed-before-1930* (where the source records the printing). Serving `decks`: *not-a-deck-book*. Serving `clean`: *cleans* (the book cleans under the current version, deterministically). Proposed, recorded only until Justin adopts them: *admitted-source* (a new source enters by an entry here), *signed* (an unsigned work enters only with a known printing year before 1930), *not-duplicate*, *provenance*.
- **One reading added to *dead-by-1955*, for Justin to confirm:** a person with no dates passes only in a book printed before 1870, since anyone who wrote a book printed by then was dead by 1955. Without it, every scanned book whose record gives no dates would be refused (the Lyell precursors among them). Unsigned work is left to the proposed rule *signed*, because the death rule cannot speak to it.
- **Where the gate refuses:** intakes refuse on the adopted rules, switched in `data/policies.json` ("intake", on by default). The older paths (`thorp fetch`, `train.prepare`'s fetch) only observe: a book new to the library is logged with the verdict the gate would give and is never refused, so no queued voyage can be stopped by this. Cleaning stays where it is (at read time, the source's text the record), per the cleaning entry above. Acquisition checks that each book it admits cleans; it does not clean a second time.
- **The library audit's first reading (19:05), 2,610 books:**
  - 7 Gutenberg books read in training have a translator or co-author who died after 1955, so they are not public domain in Australia: *The Art of War* (Giles, 1958), *Oedipus King of Thebes* (Murray, 1957), *The Social Contract* (Cole, 1959), *Pinocchio* (Della Chiesa, 1972), *The Nibelungenlied* (Needler, 1962), *Botchan* (Mori, 1959) and *Swimming Scientifically Taught* (L. C. Dalton, 1974). The voyage gate's `period` check catches them when a new voyage reads them, but they are in the library and in earlier recipes.
  - 52 books have a person with no dates (48 read, 4 in held-out decks), mostly translators and church bodies.
  - 3 books read in training are not English, and 133 are unsigned (the proposed rule).
  - No duplicates, no missing files; a sample of 24 still hashes as registered and has no boilerplate left.
  - **For Justin:** the voyage gate (`vsm._deaths`) lets undated people through, while the intake gate does not. One of the two should change, by an entry here.
- **S3 built out:**
  - Every decision of the voyage gate is now recorded in `data/gate-log.jsonl` (`vsm.gate_log()`): the voyage, where it was asked (a voyage sailing, or the fitout builder, `gate(..., where=)`, keyword-only), and each policy that spoke and how (refused, bargain, excepted, advisory).
  - `vsm.audit()["S3"]` gains the bargains (queued voyages that opted out in words, with the reason), the written exceptions, each policy's bargained count and its refusals over the last 400 decisions, and `opt_in` per policy. `audit()["S1"]` gains acquisition's summary.
  - Tests keep these records in their temporary folder (`tests/conftest.py`, autouse), after a test run had written into the real gate log; those lines were removed.
- **The page** (UI.md, "Governance"): Governance has three tabs, the five systems, **S3 control** (`#/governance/control`) and **Acquisition** (`#/governance/acquisition`, five bands of its own). The Library's fitouts are one DataTable of every fitout, curated and saved, with the picked row's card beneath (UI.md, "Shelves").
- **The corpus ceiling** (`plans/corpus-ceiling.md`): Gutenberg holds about 3.9 to 5.3B legal tokens, and the open scanned-book sources about 12 to 18B. Its proposed correction of "Thorp against Qwen" awaits Justin's adoption; it is not made here.

## Ogden v0.1: one fixed corpus for every class (7 November, 19:20, Justin: "the fitout is a new one called Ogden ... fixed as the pile was for Pythia"; "run as a baseline against 300k, 1m and 9 million")

- **What a 300k can hold, measured first.** Thorp 300k is 2 layers, 64 wide, 2 heads, 229,696 parameters (131,072 of them the 2,048-piece table). On the Forgotten Tomes, a word bigram table built from 80 prose books (6.6M word pairs, 1.3M distinct) guesses the next word right 12.6% of the time, "the" every time 7.7%, and the best 300k (`tf-base-keel-s1`) 11.6%: by memory it is already near what two-word statistics give. The commonest 10,000 pairs cover 45% of running text, 100,000 cover 71%.
- **Ogden-ness** (`thorp/ogden.py`, `lists/ogden-850.txt`, Ogden's 850 words from Wiktionary's appendix): the share of a book's ordinary words that are Ogden words in their regular forms and Ogden's operator and pronoun forms; names and numbers are free (Basic English allows them, and repeated names are what the Nevermore copies). The **skeleton keys** (Justin's): a word outside the 850 is `<name>`, `<number>` or `<thing>`, so the keyed bigram table is finite, 853 × 853 = 727,609 cells, the same for every book and model. A low-rank fit of it from the prose books: 16 numbers per word hold 90% of the table's gain over the unigram, 64 (the 300k's width) 98%: the 300k has room for the whole table; its spare capacity is for looking further back (copying, the Nevermore).
- **The store books' Ogden-ness** (`lists/ogden-v0.1-scores.csv`, all 883): median 70%, top 88% (Johnny Crow's Party); strict Ogden text barely exists in period English (1% of sentences are wholly Ogden), so the corpus is whole books ranked by it. **The corpus** (`lists/ogden-v0.1.csv`, Justin's cut): the 592 store books at 68% or more (The Adventures of Pinocchio left out by the period gate: a credited name dead in 1972), 36.8M words, about 50M tokens; by kind prose 29%, fiction 29%, trigram store 27%, nursery 7%, cause 7%, reasoning 2%; by word Ogden 66%, `<thing>` 27%, `<name>` 5%, `<number>` 2%. Its keyed table has 200,210 cells that occur (27.5%), 86,030 five times or more. Every book is in the stores, so it reads the stores without an opt-in.
- **The fitout** (`data/fitouts/ogden-v0-1.json`, "Ogden v0.1") and **the expedition** (`fleet.EXPEDITIONS["ogden"]`, ship label "Fitout: Ogden v0.1"): pretraining only, the corpus four times (about 200M tokens read, 20 a parameter for the 9m), `unwrap-v1` clean text, one shared 2,048-piece tokeniser trained on the corpus's clean text (so the classes differ in size alone), rotary positions, no tack, each class's own learning rate and warm-up from the two-fact baseline, the same 32,768-token batch, so step k is the same tokens read in every class. Read every 64 steps (~2.1M tokens): the four cards, the stars and the logic deck; every 640 the Forgotten Tomes. 300k, 1m and 9m, two seeds each (`fu-ogden-v0-1@<class>-pre-s<seed>`), queued after the clean-text keel baseline, 300ks first.
- **The test, pre-registered:** at points of the Forgotten Tomes whose next word is an Ogden word, the best Ogden-word guess, top 1 and top 5. Today: the corpus's keyed bigram table 16.5% / 43.3%; `tf-base-keel-s1` (raw text, not this corpus) 17.3% / 38.4%. With every word keyed, "always `<thing>`" scores 28.2% and the table 30.3%, so the Ogden-word score is the one that counts. And the cards and stars by tokens read across the three classes: which form at 9m, and whether the 300k's trends point there.
- **Found on the way, not fixed:** three store books break the period rule (two in the prose store, one in the trigram store; Pinocchio among them). The stores pass the stores rule by construction, so only the period gate caught it; fixing the frozen stores is S5's call (Justin) and acquisition's audit.

## Only Ogden sails (7 November, 19:30, Justin: "all those voyages in the queue can be removed as they're not using the ogden fitout and everything going forward only runs the ogden fitout")

- **The queue was emptied of everything not on the Ogden fitout:** 154 voyages (the card censuses around the keel and pilot fitouts, the two-fact rounds, First Light, Seedling, the Magician's Apprentice II, the best keel, the 9m sweep and levers, the speller curriculum, the content experiment, the traits ships) and 5 commands (the two fitout census summaries, the two-fact pick, the best keel's round 2 and a traits re-score, three of which would have written more non-Ogden voyages back into the queue). `specs/queue.txt` now holds the six Ogden v0.1 voyages (300k, 1m and 9m, two seeds). Every removed line is kept, in order, in `specs/queue-removed-2026-11-07.txt`, so any of them can be put back by copying a line.
- **S5 policy `ogden`, enforced** (`vsm._ogden_only`): a new voyage's named fitout is Ogden or a version of it (`ogden`, `ogden-<version>`, today `ogden-v0-1`). S3's gate refuses any other, and records the refusal in its log. The fitout builder's queue goes through the same gate, so a non-Ogden fitout can no longer be queued from the page. The S3\* audit counts queued voyages breaking it.
- **The voyage already started:** `fo-keel-cards-cause~lr-low-post1-s1` had been taken off the queue at 19:25:57 and was waiting in port for the helm, behind the Maincoder-1B scoring. The gate runs only once a voyage has the helm, so it was expected to be refused there. **It was not:** its process had started at 19:25:57 and loaded the gate before the rule existed, so it passed at 19:30:55 and sailed. Justin ordered it stopped, but it had come home at 19:33:27 after 2.4 minutes (finished, in the ledger), before the order reached it; it is the last non-Ogden voyage. A voyage already started keeps the rules it started with, so a new policy reaches only processes that start after it. `fo-keel-cards-cause~lr-low-pre-s1` came home at 19:25:57, before the rule.
- **The expeditions left without voyages** (the card census, the two-fact problem, First Light, Seedling, the Apprentice II, the best keel) now read *awaiting orders* in S2's view. Closing or pausing them is Justin's call.
- **Tests:** the rule is about which voyages may sail, not how the harness behaves, and the Ogden fitout exists only as a saved fitout in the real data folder, so it is advisory inside tests (`tests/conftest.py`), except in its own test, which enforces it.

## Ogden v0.1 sets sail: prechecks in Postgres, the goals pre-registered (7 November, 19:50, Justin: "these cleanup tasks should be in a data model in postgres that represent how a fitout can have precheck items ... and then let's set sail")

- **Postgres** (17, Thorp's own cluster in `data/postgres`, outside git, `localhost:5433`, database `thorp`) holds the fitout model; **SQLAlchemy** models (`thorp/viable/s3_control/models.py`) and **Alembic** migrations (`migrations/`, `alembic.ini`; `uv run alembic upgrade head`) own its schema (Justin: "use alembic to manage the python data migrations and models"). Schema `fitout`: `fitouts`, `stages`, `precheck_items` (what is measured, the pass rule, the VSM system that owns it), `fitout_prechecks` (required or planned), `precheck_results` (every reading kept), views `current_results` and `readiness`.
- **The code laid out by system** (Justin: "structure it like VSM in the file directory"): `thorp/viable/` with `s1_operations` (runs a fitout's prechecks, marks it ready), `s2_coordination` (the shared tokeniser: trained, lossless, Ogden words one piece), `s3_control` (the store and models; the library: period, decks, stores, enough text for the biggest class), `s3star_audit` (cleaning deterministic; every comma and letter kept), `s4_intelligence` (planned v0.2 items, measured, not required), `s5_policy` (the cleaning standard). The older modules stay where they are until the repo split.
- **unwrap-v2** (Justin, 19:40: punctuation and capitals "provide context ... Jesus, wept needs the comma"): unwrap-v1, then one quote style, one dash, `_italics_` as `*italics*`, single spaces; nothing that carries meaning is changed (the audit checks every comma and letter survives). It is `cleaning.CURRENT`; the Ogden specs moved to it (new run ids; none had sailed). The two tokeniser steps (a line's first word as mid-sentence; a paragraph break as one piece) are S4's planned items for v0.2: today every line-start word differs from mid-sentence and a paragraph break is two pieces.
- **S5 policy `prechecks`** (enforced): a voyage sails only when its fitout is ready in the store and it reads the cleaning its fitout was checked on; an unreachable store refuses. **Ogden v0.1's prechecks** (19:48): 16 of 16 required pass (clean text 21.6 words per line break; no curly quote, hyphen dash, underscore italic or doubled space left; punctuation and letters kept exactly; the period, decks and stores rules; 67.4M tokens, 4.4 times what the biggest class needs; one lossless tokeniser, 89% of Ogden-word uses one piece); 2 planned.
- **The classes' real sizes:** with the shared 2,048-piece tokeniser the speller (`9m`) is 3.05M parameters, the others smaller in proportion: most of a 9m's parameters are normally its 16,384-piece table. The comparison is still size alone; the names are the presets'.
- **The goals, pre-registered before sailing** (Justin, 19:45: "the goal for 300k is to be the best 2 word guesser and then 1m to be the best 3 word guesser and 9m to be the best 4 word guesser"; "Ogden doesn't need to know about the world. Just the shapes of the world"): scored in the keyed space (Ogden's 850 words and `<name>`, `<number>`, `<thing>`: the shape of a sentence, not which thing or name), on the Forgotten Tomes. The keel's top guess of the next keyed word beats the corpus's own keyed table with one word of context; the pilot's with two; the speller's with three (each table backing off to fewer words where it never saw the context), on both seeds. The tables are built from the corpus alone, so the bars are fixed before any voyage sails; their scores are recorded beside the first results.
- **Set sail:** the hold (`specs/ogden.hold`, the queue's waiting step) removed at 19:50.
- **The bars, measured (19:52, the corpus's keyed tables on the 16 Forgotten Tomes, 1,303,877 keyed next words, 64% of them Ogden words):** "always `<thing>`" 27.9%; the 2-word table (the keel's bar) 30.0% of all words, 7.3% of Ogden words; the 3-word table (the pilot's) 31.3% and 9.4%; the 4-word table (the speller's) 31.2% and 10.7% (its 3-word contexts are seen in the corpus 97% of the time; 2.1M distinct). Read: over all words the bars sit close to the `<thing>` floor and barely separate, because a third of next words are keys; on Ogden words they separate clearly (7.3, 9.4, 10.7). Both are reported for every class; a class meets its goal when it beats its table on both. Class sizes with the shared tokeniser: keel 229,696, pilot 1,049,728, speller 3,049,920 parameters.
- **Sailed:** `fu-ogden-v0-1@300k-pre-s1` took the helm at 19:46 (the shared tokeniser `20e2cd56c6a5`, 2,048 pieces, `unwrap-v2`).

## The logic tutor, pre-registered (7 November, 19:58, Justin: "Pre-register it")

- **The question.** Can a trick of the reasoning waypoint be *taught* to an Ogden ship rather than read into it, and does a bigger model's writing teach it better than a program's? The trick is chaining two facts (the logic deck, `tricks/logic_test`); its class on the ladder is the 50m, and the base here is the speller on Ogden v0.1 (3.05M parameters on the shared tokeniser), so a pass would bring it down two classes. Reading alone does not buy it: Pythia 410m reaches a chaining lift of +0.077 after 300B tokens.
- **Why teach the trick and not hand over the star.** The Magician's Apprentice moved two heads between keels of one size and failed all four tests (a trick without its deck); the knockout on Qwen3 4B found its chaining in no card (8 random heads hurt more than any card's 8); and the Pythias hold all four necessary cards and chain nothing. So the trick is taught as text, and the stars and cards are read to see whether a mechanism formed, never transferred. Each arm continues from the base's own weights: one trunk, the trick as a change on top of it.
- **The base:** each seed of `fu-ogden-v0-1@9m-pre` at its last checkpoint (`seeded_from`, mode free, the same 2,048-piece tokeniser and `unwrap-v2`). Its own last readings say whether it already holds the Nevermore and the mover, the two cards chaining rests on that Thorp lacks today.
- **The skeletons** (a seeded generator, to be written): two facts about invented things and the conclusion that follows from them, in four of the logic deck's relations: order, cause, membership and proportion. Each conclusion is checked by its relation's rule (transitivity, the chain of events, inclusion, multiplication) before use. **Held out of all teaching:** the counterfactual relation and every three-fact chain, so both are transfer readings. The invented words are drawn fresh, disjoint from the logic deck's and absent from the Ogden corpus's lexicon.
- **The same skeletons written two ways, at equal tokens:**
  - **tutor:** a bigger model writes each skeleton as a short passage of strict Basic English: Ogden's 850 words in their regular forms, names, numbers and the skeleton's invented words, nothing else; varied wording, the facts in either order, the conclusion drawn with a marker ("so", "because of this"). The teacher is Qwen 3.8 27B (4-bit, on this Mac, the weights already downloaded for the Forgotten Tomes), its licence checked before generation. It returns each passage with its facts and conclusion restated as triples, which must match the skeleton; a passage with any word outside the allowed set gets one rewrite, then is dropped. A 100-passage sample is read by hand before any voyage sails: if fewer than 95% keep the skeleton's logic, the prompt is rewritten.
  - **template:** the same generator's skeletons in fixed sentence frames, written fresh (none of the logic deck's frames). Frames are shorter than prose, so this arm draws more skeletons to fill the same tokens. No model's text is in it.
- **Kept apart from the tests:** no run of six words shared with any row of the logic deck or of the chains' pair files (`lists/tricks/`). **The four inventions kept out:** no skeleton has the form of a discovery the bettor is tested on (variation, inheritance and selection; a quantity kept through a change; a row repeating with a period; a return of the same). The content words (numbers excepted) of the four chains' 50m rungs (`nature.csv`, `mill.csv`, `between.csv`, `pigeonhole.csv`) are listed before generation, and a passage that uses one is dropped. The list is recorded with the fitout. Those four tests are also a reading below, so they must not be taught.
- **The stage, the same for every arm:** 16M tokens (about 490 steps at 32,768 a batch; the base read about 200M), half new text and half the base's own corpus. Ogden draws are fixed by seed and taken in whole books.
  - **tutor:** 2M tokens of tutor text and a 2M-token draw of Ogden v0.1, read four times, shuffled.
  - **template:** 2M tokens of template text and the same draw, four times.
  - **replay** (the control): the same draw and a second 2M-token Ogden draw, four times. The base reads on, untaught.
  - The base's optimiser (AdamW, batch 32,768, clip 1.0, bf16), warm-up 50 steps to 3e-4 (between the base's final 1e-4 and its peak 1e-3), cosine to a tenth. The light set (stars, Nevermore, cards, logic) every 32 steps; the full set (adds the Forgotten Tomes and the chains) at the end. The base's last full reading is step 0.
  - Three arms, two seeds, six voyages, each seed's arms from that seed's base: `fu-ogden-v0-1@9m-<arm>-s<seed>`.
- **The verdicts, pre-registered, at the last reading across both seeds:**
  1. **The trick is taught** (per taught arm, against replay). PASS if both seeds perform chaining on the logic deck by the deck's own rule (gain 0.1 or more, two-thirds of items right), and both seeds' gain is above both replay seeds'.
  2. **The teacher is worth having** (tutor against template). PASS if both tutor seeds' gain is above both template seeds'.
  3. **Nothing lost** (a guard on each taught arm). The mean Forgotten Tomes score of its two seeds is no worse than replay's by more than 0.02 bits per byte. An arm that passes 1 and fails 3 is a partial pass: it taught the trick at the cost of the reading. The speller's goal reading (the keyed 4-word guess on Ogden words) is reported beside it.
- **Readings reported, never verdicts:** transfer (`gain_counterfactual` and `lift_three_hop`, both never taught; `lift_padded`, does the chain survive a distractor); the cards over the stage (the Nevermore, the mover and the comparator, and which lights first, the mover or the gain; a trick performed with no card lit is done some other way); the four chains' 50m rungs (Nature's breeder, the mill, the between and the pigeonhole tests); kinks at the stage boundary (the named-stages question).
- **What each outcome means:**
  - **Tutor passes 1 and 2:** a bigger model's writing teaches reasoning that reading does not; the taught line is worth building. Next: the same at the 1m (how far down it goes) and at the grown 21m with all five relations.
  - **Template passes 1, and the tutor does not beat it:** the forms teach, not the teacher. Thorp's logic comes from programs, which carry no modern knowledge, and no bigger model is needed for it.
  - **Neither passes 1:** at 3M parameters chaining is not taught in 16M tokens. The test moves to the 21m once grown, or the stage lengthens.
- **Before it sails (not queued):**
  - The skeleton generator, the two writers and the six specs.
  - The teacher's generation rate, measured on 1,000 passages before the full 2M tokens are drawn.
  - The two Ogden 9m voyages home.
  - **An S5 policy for taught text (Justin's decision, open):** which fitouts may read text that is not a period book; how such voyages are marked; that they stay off the period leaderboard and are never compared with their teacher on the Forgotten Tomes. Today the `ogden` policy matches fitout names by prefix, so an `ogden-…` taught fitout would pass it unmarked, and the stores rule would need a `data.opt_in` for each taught arm. The replay arm is period-pure and needs neither.
- **Registered** as the expedition `logic_tutor` (`fleet.EXPEDITIONS`, status planned, ship label "Logic tutor").

## The repository in three parts: backend, frontend, ships (7 November, 20:00, Justin: "split the repo out into front end and backend and then the ships")

- **The layout:** `backend/` (the `thorp` package, `tests/`, `scripts/`, Alembic's `migrations/` and `alembic.ini`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`), `frontend/` (the Astro dashboard, was `thorp/dash/web`; builds into `backend/thorp/dash/static`), `ships/` (`specs/` with the standing queue, `lists/`, `fitouts/`, `plans/`, `reports/`). Runtime state stays at the root, outside git: `data/` and `ledger.sqlite`. `DECISIONS.md` and `README.md` stay at the root.
- **Paths:** `thorp/paths.py` has three roots (`BACKEND`, `ROOT`, `ships_dir()`, THORP_SHIPS to override) and finds a path by its first folder (`resolve`; `ROUTER` for code that joins onto a root: `ROUTER / "lists/x.csv"`, `ROUTER.glob("lists/...")`), so every spec, list and script names its files as before. The scripts' and the dashboard server's hand-made roots (`Path(__file__).parents[...]`) now use the router. Two tests that opened `lists/...` relative to the working directory now go through `paths`.
- **The saved fitouts** moved from `data/fitouts/` (runtime, untracked) to `ships/fitouts/` (in git): a fitout is ship material. Everything else in `data/` stays runtime.
- **Commands run from the root:** `uv run --project backend ...`; the queue scripts `cd` to the root and name `ships/specs/`, `ships/lists/`, `backend/scripts/`. A `cmd:` line in the queue runs from the root too.
- **Done in a worktree** (`split-frontend-backend-ships`) while Ogden v0.1 sailed from the old layout; merged between voyages with the queue stopped, so no voyage reads half a layout.

## Maincoder-1B on the Forgotten Tomes (7 November, 19:30, Justin: "how can i smoke maincodes maincoder 1 b" … "Yes, score it")

- **Why this model:** Maincode's `Maincoder-1B` (Melbourne; 1.03B parameters, Apache 2.0, a code model, 76% on HumanEval) is the smallest model an Australian company has released that I could find. Sovereign Australia AI's Ginan is described as an 8B Llama 3.1 fine-tune; Maincode's Matilda has no published size or weights. "Smoking" it, in Thorp's terms, is a Thorp at a tenth of its size (about 100M) reaching its mark, on Thorp's own measures: not on code, which nothing printed before 1930 contains.
- **The port** (`backend/scripts/mlx_ports.py`): mlx-lm cannot load its architecture (`MaincoderForCausalLM`). It is Qwen3-like (GQA 16/4, head 96, SwiGLU, RMSNorm, tied head), with two differences: the rotation pairs adjacent dimensions (the "traditional" form), and the query and key norms come *after* the rotation, so mlx-lm's Qwen3 would score it wrongly. The port downloads only the weights (`model.safetensors`, 2.05 GB), the config and the tokenizer (Qwen2's; start token `<|endoftext|>`), and never runs Maincode's Python.
- **The port checked before it was trusted:** on 6 KB of this repository's Python and 6 KB of *Pride and Prejudice*, 1.041 and 1.232 bits per byte with adjacent pairs, against 2.406 and 2.005 with rotate-half. Norms before the rotation give 1.046 and 1.230, so that difference barely shows. A greedy Python completion is coherent.
- **The score** (`data/compare/Maincode--Maincoder-1B__hole-cards-unseen.json`; raw text, 1,024-token windows from an empty context, as every outside model; 297 seconds): **1.123 bits per byte**. Beside it: Qwen3.5 0.8B 1.138, Qwen3 0.6B 1.234, and Thorp's best 9m (`pool-400-v1`) 1.632, behind on all 16 books, by 0.34 (*A Happy Woman*) to 0.73 (Wilson's *History of the American People*, vol. 5). Fiction is closest; history and reference books are furthest. No book is flagged as probably seen elsewhere: its best is 0.19 below its own mean, against the deck's 0.5.
- **What smoking it takes:** a Thorp of about 100M parameters at or below 1.123 on the Forgotten Tomes, 0.51 below the 9m today, and a harder mark than Qwen3 0.6B's. On the 0.22B legal tokens fetched so far it is not expected; the corpus ceiling (`ships/plans/corpus-ceiling.md`) puts 3.9 to 5.3B legal tokens in Gutenberg alone.
- **S2:** outside-model scoring now takes the helm (`vsm.at_sea`, kind "score", in `score_external.py`), so it waits in port while a voyage is at sea, and the next voyage waits for it.

## The $100 bet (7 November, 20:10, Justin: "I believe I can take a ladder of model sizes so that at a 350m model I can get it for $100 on a rented H100 and get it to be the right way to discover quantum physics")

- **The bet** (`backend/thorp/bet.py`, `/api/bet`, the dashboard's **$100** tab at `#/bet`): a ladder of model sizes, each rung proved on this Mac, makes a 350m trained for $100 on a rented H100 bet the right way on quantum physics. "What we model against is the journey from 300k upwards to 50m to make sure 50m can solve its first impressive human thing."
- **Justin's goals for the rungs (20:05):** 300k the best two-word guesser, 1m the best three-word guesser, 9m the best four-word guesser, 21m the best model in the world at chaining two facts, 50m the first eureka. Above them, in the bet: 100m the classical discoveries (the Origin test, the energy test, Mendeleev's gaps) and 350m the quantum bet. This moves chaining two facts down to the 21m and the first discovery down to the 50m; `fleet.TRICKS`' goal classes are not changed yet, so the class pages still show the old assignment.
- **The first eureka (proposed for the 50m):** the eternal return (cutoff 1872), because its last step is one two-fact chain, only so many states and no end of time, and that is the same move as the quantum bet: a few allowed states explain a pattern.
- **The quantum bet, a draft** (`bet.QUANTUM`, no pairs written): **the discrete atom, cutoff 1910**. Haas (1910), Nicholson (1912) and Bohr (1913) each put Planck's constant into the atom within three years, so it meets the discovery tests' rule; every precursor's author died by 1955. The idea: an atom keeps only certain states and gives out light only when it jumps between them. Rivals: the classical atom that radiates as it circles; Stoney's overtones (1871); Thomson's vibrating charges (1904). Precursors: Kirchhoff and Bunsen, Balmer, Rydberg, Planck, Thomson and Nagaoka. Ablation: without Balmer and Planck, bytes kept. Its chain, one rung per class: strings and notes, the overtone series, the harmonics test, the ring test, the eternal return, the spectroscopists, the discrete atom.
- **The budget** (`bet.budget()`): a 350m at twenty tokens a parameter (304M parameters on Ogden's tokeniser, 6.08B tokens) is about 9 H100-hours and $27 at $3 an hour and 400 TFLOPS achieved (band $16 to $50). Two seeds and the ablation, with set-up, the learning-rate probe and transfer, come to about 29 H100-hours and $86 (band $51 to $158; at the dear end $100 buys one run). The PyTorch backend and the 1910 shelf are work, not H100-hours, and are not in the $100.
- **The gates before the $100 is spent:** the PyTorch backend matches MLX at 9m; the guessers meet their Ogden goals; the 21m leads its size at chaining; the 50m passes the eternal return; the quantum chain's forecast over 9m to 100m reaches the bar by 350m with P at least 0.9; a 1910 shelf of about 1.5B unique English tokens, and Justin's S5 decision on reading outside Ogden.
- **The known risks:** the precursors are thin in English before 1910 (Balmer, Planck and Haas wrote in German); the harmonics rung teaches Stoney's rival too; facts in the weights are harder to chain than facts in the prompt; a bet is not a derivation; S5's Ogden-only policy refuses the dated shelf as it stands; the price band.

## Shapes: the parts-of-speech taxonomy at every reading; Ogden v0.1 called back (7 November, 20:20, Justin: "ogdens v0.1 must include the parts of speech classification you proposed ... so we'll need to call the ships back and send them out with that included")

- **The judge books** (Justin, 20:15: "I think judge books makes sense"): every held-out deck a voyage is scored on and no voyage may read. Today the Forgotten Tomes judge Ogden; the hole cards are the older judges.
- **The taxonomy** (`backend/thorp/viable/s5_policy/taxonomy.py`, `shapes-v1`, S5): two tiers. The fine tier is Universal Dependencies' parts of speech (NOUN, PROPN, NUM, PRON, VERB, AUX, ADJ, ADV, ADP, DET, CCONJ, SCONJ, PART, INTJ, PUNCT; SYM and X folded into OTHER) plus Thorp's BREAK (a line or paragraph break). The coarse tier is Ogden's own kinds: THING, QUALITY, OPERATION, plus MARK. PUNCT and BREAK count as guesses ("Jesus, wept" needs the comma).
- **The tagger** (`s2_coordination/tagger.py`, S2): spaCy 3.8.7 with `en_core_web_sm` 3.8.0, pinned (the backend refuses other versions), its tagger only. An instrument, outside the period rule. The Ogden corpus was tagged once (45.0M tagged tokens, 242,187 distinct words, 13 minutes): each word's commonest tag (the lexicon) and the shape bars (tag tables of 1, 2 and 3 tags of context), under `data/tags/`.
- **The `shapes` evaluator** (`backend/thorp/evaluators/shapes.py`, every reading, every class): at 2,048 fixed points of the judge books (two windows a book, up to 64 points each), the top guess's part of speech (`shapes/upos_top1`), its Ogden kind (`shapes/ogden_top1`), the keyed word (`shapes/keyed_top1`, the 2-, 3- and 4-word goals), the probability on each kind of word (`shapes/mass/<TAG>`) and a confusion table. 1.4 s a reading after the first.
- **Prechecks added:** `shapes.recorded` (S5), `tagger.pinned` and `tagger.lexicon` (S2), `audit.tagger_agrees` (S3*: on words whose kind context cannot change, 99.7%). Ogden v0.1: 20 of 20 required pass.
- **First reading, the first Ogden 300k's final checkpoint** (sailed before the evaluator): the right part of speech 35.5%, the right Ogden kind 58.3%, the right keyed word 21.5%. It over-expects working words and marks (punctuation 19% of its probability against 14% of the text, determiners 14% against 9%, names 10% against 6%) and under-expects content (nouns 9% against 17%, verbs 3% against 10%, adjectives 2% against 6%): the frame of a sentence before what fills it.
- **The recall (20:20):** the 1m's two voyages were stopped (the queue's terminal tab closed; both marked `recalled` in the ledger), and all six specs gained `shapes` in their light and full readings (new run ids). The two finished 300ks stay in the ledger as Ogden v0.1's first pair, without shapes; the six re-sail behind a hold.
- **The Nevermore needs layers in order** (the record, 20:30): in the four-cards census's three-layer 300k (`300k-l3`, one seed), previous-word lit in layer 1 and the Nevermore's head appeared in layer 2 (0.05 to 0.23 at step 2,100, with the copy gain 0.38 to 1.47 and previous-word 0.33 to 0.59 in the same window: the phase change of an induction head), lit at 0.34 by step 5,600, and fell to 0.18 in post-training. In every two-layer 300k, previous-word formed in the last layer, leaving no later layer for the copying head. The 1m has 4 layers and the 9m 6; an earlier 1m still read only 0.13, so layers are necessary, not sufficient.

## The deck and the Navigator's Wager (7 November, 21:00 to 21:30, Justin: "collecting cards to get to next levels"; "keep the deck a certain size to keep within a human's context window"; "predicting where the cards fall based on pulling a specific lever"; "I should be able to choose the lever, bet and then queue an experiment")

- **The deck** (`backend/thorp/viable/s5_policy/deck.py`, S5): 16 cards in four constellations of four (about four chunks fit in working memory): ♠ Copying (previous-word, duplicate-word, induction, the Nevermore), ♥ Position (the sink, the delimiter, words back, the counter), ♦ Comparing (number-spotter, order-word, the comparator, the scales), ♣ Carrying (the rare word, premise-marker, the bridge, the mover). Stages as Pokemon evolve: a Basic is one head in one layer; a Stage 1 is played only above a played Basic of its constellation in an earlier layer; a Face is traded in when its cards are held in order and its ability (what it does: "a machine is what it does") passes: the Nevermore's is a copy gain of 1.0 nats, the mover's a one-hop lift of +0.1, the scales' an order gain of +0.1. Cards without a reading are uncharted (the counter, number-spotter, order-word, premise-marker). Rarity across 104 finished voyages: the delimiter lit in 42 of 74, the rare word 24, the comparator 13, the sink 5, previous-word 3 (one head), induction / the Nevermore 1 of 107, the rest 0.
- **The faces:** 16 images in the dashboard's noir ink style (fal, `fal-ai/flux/dev`, `backend/thorp/dash/art/generate_art.py`, now writing to `frontend/public/art/`); the five existing cards kept, eleven made.
- **The Navigator's Wager** (`backend/thorp/viable/s4_intelligence/wager.py`, S4): a round is one lever pulled on a control voyage of Ogden v0.1 (depth, heads, the copy tack, learning rate x2 or /2, learned positions, one or two passes, seed; never the vocabulary, which would break the shared tokeniser). The Navigator calls how the pull moves each card (appears, vanishes, earlier, later by more than a quarter of the tokens, same) and, for a bonus, its layer; five chips; the House calls that nothing moves. Right +3, right layer +2 more, wrong -2; ties to the House; a bet placed after the pull sailed is practice. Where a card falls is read from the ledger with the evolution rules. Rounds and bets in Postgres (`game.rounds`, `game.bets`, Alembic `13d6214bc39b`). The Bet tab on each class page deals a round, takes the bets, queues the pull voyage (written, through the S3 gate, at the end of the standing queue, with a confirmation) and settles it as the readings arrive.
- **The first lesson (depth, seed 1, 21:28):** on Ogden text a third layer did not draw the Nevermore (0.006, copy gain 0.06, as in two layers). Previous-word moved to layer 2, the last layer again (the census's three-layer keel had it in layer 1, under the copying head); the comparator appeared (layer 1, by 4M tokens); the rare word and the delimiter vanished. The census voyage that drew the Nevermore also had the induction tack: the copy-tack round decides between them.

## Pre-registered before it sails: does the Ogden 21m draw ♣? (7 November, 21:55, Justin: "so this would be our first optimization claim no?")

- **What kind of claim it can be:** a discovery on Ogden text at one size, not an optimisation claim. There is no 21m control (no other corpus, no second seed), so whatever it draws cannot be credited to Ogden or to any lever; it can only be "the first Thorp to draw it". Optimisation claims come from lever rounds with controls and two seeds (the Wager).
- **The voyage:** `fu-ogden-v0-1@21m-pre-s1` (10.5M parameters, 8 layers, 320 wide; the shared 2,048-piece tokeniser; Ogden v0.1, four passes, 269M tokens; one seed), read every 64 steps.
- **The predictions, fixed now:**
  1. ♣ **the mover** is played (its reading at least 0.2 and the logic deck's one-hop lift at least +0.1, its trade-in): **not expected** (no Thorp has drawn it; Pythia has it from 31m, but after about 1,100 times the reading).
  2. ♣ **the bridge** reads at least 0.5: not expected, and weak evidence if it does (the reading is untrusted: lit on 2 of 5 outside chainers).
  3. ♠ **previous-word forms below the last layer** (its layer at most 6 of 0 to 7) and **induction above it**: expected, given 8 layers. The **Nevermore** is traded in (copy gain at least 1.0): possible.
  4. **Chaining two facts** (chaining lift above +0.03 and the cause gain above +0.1): **not expected**; even if seen, one seed is a hint, and a second seed sails at once to test it.
  5. **Prediction:** judge-book bits per byte clearly below the keel's 1.85 (around 1.55 to 1.7), and still falling past 34M tokens, where the keel flattened.
- **What would be a finding:** any of 1, 2 or 4 holding is a first for Thorp and gets a second seed before it is called a result; 3 holding while 1 and 4 fail says the ♠ suit comes with depth on Ogden text while ♣ needs more than 21m on 269M tokens.

## The deck charted: number-spotter, order-word, premise-marker and the counter (7 November, 22:15, Justin: "add support for premise marker, order word, number spotter, the counter ... the next voyage can inherit them")

- **The `pips` evaluator** (`backend/thorp/evaluators/pips.py`), on the judge books (four windows of up to 256 pieces from each of the 16 Forgotten Tomes, the same for every class and reading). Three Basics are read as the delimiter is: a head's attention on one class of word pieces over that class's share of the keys it can see (1 indifferent; the mark 3.0): **number-spotter** (numerals and number words), **order-word** (more, less, before, after, than, first, last, next, most, least, bigger ... worse), **premise-marker** (if, when, because, since, unless, so, therefore, hence, thus, then). One Face by its ability: **the counter**, a ridge regression from the residual stream after each layer to each piece's word position in its sentence, fitted on every other window and scored on the rest, the best layer's R² (the mark 0.5). The deck now charts all 16 cards.
- **First readings** (final checkpoints; the marks 3.0 and 0.5): the Ogden keel 0.68 / 1.30 / 1.07 / R² 0.22; the three-layer Ogden keel 0.87 / 1.13 / 1.21 / 0.20; the census's Nevermore keel 1.20 / 1.08 / 0.92 / 0.32; the two-fact pilot (1m) 1.31 / 1.24 / **2.30** / **0.59**: the 1m holds the counter, and its premise-marker is two thirds of the way to its mark. The readings rise with size, as a measure should.
- **Inherited:** the five Ogden v0.1 voyages not yet sailed (the second copy-tack keel, the 1m and 9m pairs) read `pips` at every reading (new run ids; prechecks 20 of 20). The 21m was at sea already; its checkpoints can be scored afterwards with `thorp eval`.

## Pretraining in phases: the metre scaffold on the keel (7 November, 23:15, Justin: "pretraining is broken up into phases, the insertion of the metre topics and then normal training on content"; "schedule it after the 21m run")

- **The scaffold** (`optim.scaffold`, `backend/thorp/train.py`): a second loss pulls chosen heads' attention toward a metre card's pattern, a rule over the token ids, so no teacher is needed. **previous-word** attends to the piece before. **induction** attends to the pieces that followed earlier copies of this piece, shared evenly among them. The pull is a cross-entropy from the pattern to the head's attention, averaged over the scaffolded heads, added at this step's weight. The model reads Ogden text throughout; only the pull changes. **Three phases of one voyage:** **metre** (full weight, to `hold`), **fade** (eased linearly to zero by `fade`), **content** (the language-model loss alone). `fade: null` never releases it. Recorded every step: `scaffold/weight`, `scaffold/loss`, `scaffold/phase`. The Watch panel draws the phases under the stage bar, with the pull and its weight on the loss chart. The picked heads' weights come from `GPT.forward_attending` (the ordinary forward pass, with those heads' attention computed out beside it). Only rule-made metre cards can be pinned so far (previous-word, induction); the other eight metre cards follow if this works.
- **Why the fade, not a hard switch:** a head pinned while nothing above it learns to read it is held from the page, and the language-model loss lets it drift once released. Reading text all along lets the layers above come to depend on it, and the fade is the test of "by heart".
- **Placement:** the layout the 21m found for itself (previous-word moved to its layer 1 at steps 1,280 to 1,600; induction rising in layer 3 above it), squeezed into the keel's 2 x 2 heads: previous-word on layer 0 head 0, induction on layer 1 head 0. The other head in each layer is free.
- **The arms (Ogden v0.1, the 300k keel, 8,216 steps, two seeds each), queued right after the 21m:** `fu-ogden-v0-1~metre@300k-pre-s1/s2` (full to 5% = step 411, faded by 25% = step 2,054) and `fu-ogden-v0-1~metre-held@300k-pre-s1/s2` (never released: the ceiling). Both read `pips` as well. **The control is home:** `fu-ogden-v0-1@300k-pre-s1/s2` (Nevermore look 0.004 / 0.003, copy gain 0.085 / 0.062, previous-word 0.36 / 0.33 in layer 1, the last; judge-book bpb 1.853 / 1.860).
- **Predictions, fixed now:**
  1. **During metre**, both arms hold previous-word in layer 0 (at least 0.5) and the induction look in layer 1 (at least 0.3): expected; if not, the pull is too weak, and the arm says nothing about the idea.
  2. **By heart:** at the end, the fade arm still has previous-word in layer 0 at 0.5 or more, and the induction look at 0.3 or more in layer 1, on both seeds: **the claim**. Expected for previous-word, a coin toss for induction.
  3. **The Nevermore traded in** (natural copy gain at least 1.0) on the fade arm, on both seeds, where the control reads 0.06 to 0.09: hoped for, not expected. Above 0.5 on both seeds already counts as the ♠ chain doing work.
  4. **The cost:** judge-book bpb during metre is worse than the control's at the same tokens; by the end the fade arm is within 0.02 of the control (1.85) or better. If it ends more than 0.02 worse on both seeds, the scaffold costs language and the claim must say so.
  5. **The ceiling:** the held arm's copy gain is at least the fade arm's. If the fade arm keeps most of it (at least three quarters), the cards are by heart; if far less, they were held from the page.
- **What it settles:** 2 and 3 holding on both seeds is Thorp's first optimisation claim: a lever (pretraining in phases) that draws a card the keel never draws unaided, with a control and two seeds. 1 holding while 2 fails says rule-made metre can be pinned but not kept at this size, and the next lever is a longer fade or a 21m teacher's own heads.

## The night of 7 to 8 November: the 300k, then the 9m, then the 21m v0.2 (23:30, Justin: "once 21m finishes, run the 300k metre run and then take those learnings to the 9m run and if the 9m can drastically compete with the 21m then roll out the lessons from the 9m to the 21m and train that so hopefully when I wake up we have a much improved v0.2 of a 21m thorp ogden model")

Fixed before any of it sails. Claude runs the night; the queue (`ships/specs/queue.txt`) holds at two points (`specs/night-9m.hold`, `specs/night-21m.hold`; each lets go on its own after 45 minutes, and the plain 9m sails next). Time allows one seed of each: the keel arms' second seeds, the plain 9m and the re-scores sail after the 21m v0.2.

1. **The 21m v0.1 comes home** (`fu-ogden-v0-1@21m-pre-s1`): the bar to beat.
2. **The keel, seed 1** (`~metre`, `~metre-held`), against the plain keel (home). **The 9m sails with metre unless** both arms end worse than the plain keel by more than 0.05 judge-book bpb **and** neither holds previous-word in layer 0 (at least 0.5) at the end: then the scaffold hurt and held nothing, and the plain 9m sails instead.
   - **The 9m's schedule from the keel:** if the fade arm keeps previous-word (at least 0.5) and induction (at least 0.3, one head or two) at the end, the fade schedule (full to 5%, faded by 25%); if only the held arm keeps them, a longer pull (full to 10%, faded by 60%); if neither keeps induction but the held arm's copy gain beats the plain keel's, the held schedule at half weight.
   - **The 9m's heads:** the 21m's own layout, scaled to 6 layers: previous-word in layer 1 and induction in layer 3 (the 21m: layers 1 and 3 of 8), with **two** heads pinned for induction (the 21m split it across two heads of layer 3).
3. **The 9m with metre** (`~metre@9m`, 3.05M parameters, a third of the 21m). **It drastically competes with the 21m** if, at the end, at least two of these three hold:
   - **copying:** its natural copy gain is at least the 21m v0.1's final copy gain;
   - **language:** its judge-book bpb is within 0.08 of the 21m v0.1's final (a 3.4x size gap is usually worth 0.1 to 0.15);
   - **against Qwen3 0.6B** (Scoring > Versus): its median share of Qwen's level, over the ranked readings, is within 10 points of the 21m v0.1's.
4. **The 21m v0.2** sails only if the 9m competes: the 21m with the 9m's metre schedule and the 21m's own layout (previous-word in layer 1, induction on two heads of layer 3), the same corpus, seed and steps as v0.1, so the comparison with v0.1 is one lever. Its name: `fu-ogden-v0-1~metre@21m-pre-s1`, the Ogden 21m v0.2. **v0.2 is "much improved"** if it beats v0.1 on copy gain and on the Versus median share, with bpb no worse than v0.1's by more than 0.02. If the 9m does not compete, the 21m v0.2 does not sail, and the plain 9m (the 9m's control) sails in its place.
5. **One seed is a hint.** Whatever the night shows is reported as such; second seeds follow.

### The night, 01:25: the keel's seed 1 is home, and the 9m sails with the longer pull

| | plain keel | fade (to 5%, out by 25%) | held (never released) |
|---|---|---|---|
| judge-book bpb | 1.853 | **1.821** | 1.870 |
| previous-word | 0.36 (layer 1, the last) | **0.76 (layer 0)** | 1.00 (layer 0) |
| induction, one head (two) | 0.004 | 0.14 (0.14), down from 0.55 during the metre | **0.60 (0.61)** |
| natural copy gain | 0.085 | **0.75** | **1.04: the Nevermore traded in** |
| period test lift | -0.14 | **+0.58** | +0.18 |

- **Against the predictions** (pre-registered 23:15): 1 held (both arms put the chain where it was pinned). 2 half held: the fade arm kept previous-word by heart (0.76 in layer 0) but let induction go (0.55 to 0.14 over the fade and after). 3: the Nevermore traded in on the **held** arm, not the fade arm; the fade arm's copy gain (0.75) is past 0.5, so the ♠ chain does work there, 9x the plain keel's. 4: the fade arm ended **better** than the plain keel (1.821 against 1.853); the held arm 0.017 worse. 5: the fade arm kept 72% of the held arm's copy gain, just under the three quarters.
- **One seed:** the second seeds sail after the night's line.
- **The 9m's schedule, by the rule:** only the held arm kept induction, so the longer pull (full to 10%, faded by 60%), previous-word on layer 1 and induction on two heads of layer 3. Sailed at 01:19 (`fu-ogden-v0-1~metre@9m-pre-s1`).
- **The 21m v0.1's bars** (home at 00:37): bpb 1.397, natural copy gain 0.639, induction 0.154 one head and **0.298 two heads** (peaked 0.335 at step 4,480; just under the mark at the end), Versus median share of Qwen3 0.6B 0.598 (24 ranked readings).
- **Fixed on the way:** `score_checkpoints.py` read the ledger's spec, which leaves out the fitout label, and the S3 gate refused it; it now takes the labels back from the voyage's spec file.

### The night, 02:45: the 9m with metre is home; it competes, two of three; the 21m v0.2 sails

| | 9m with metre (3.05M) | 21m v0.1 (10.5M) |
|---|---|---|
| judge-book bpb | 1.547 | **1.397** |
| natural copy gain | **0.737** | 0.639 |
| induction, one head / two heads (layer 3) | **0.42 / 0.84** | 0.15 / 0.30 |
| previous-word (layer 1) | **1.00** | 0.71 |
| the comparator (two heads) | **0.80** | 0.58 |
| period test lift / refrain test lift | **0.43 / 1.27** | 0.30 / 1.08 |
| the counter (R²) / premise-marker | 0.30 / 1.92 | not read |
| one-hop lift | 0.14 | **0.25** |
| part of speech / keyed word right | 43% / 25% | **48% / 30%** |
| Versus median share of Qwen3 0.6B | 0.500 | 0.598 |

- **The rule (pre-registered 23:30):** copying holds (0.737 against 0.639); language does not (0.150 behind, the mark 0.08); Versus holds by 0.2 of a point (0.098 behind, the mark 0.10). Two of three: **it competes, by the letter**, and narrowly on the third. Read plainly: a model a third the size out-copies the 21m and holds its circuits far more cleanly, while the 21m still knows the language better (bpb, parts of speech, keyed words) and follows one premise better.
- **The longer pull kept induction by heart at 9m:** two heads 1.21 through the metre, 0.84 at the end (the fade ended at step 4,930), well past the mark; the keel's short fade had lost it.
- **The 21m v0.2** (`fu-ogden-v0-1~metre@21m-pre-s1`): v0.1's spec with only `optim.scaffold` added (full to 10%, out by 60%; previous-word layer 1 head 0, induction layer 3 heads 0 and 1); the same data, model and seed. Sailed 02:43.
- **No control yet for the 9m:** the plain 9m sails after the v0.2, so what metre did at 9m (against the 9m itself) is known by mid-morning.

### The night, 05:30: the 21m v0.2 is home: better at copying and on the Versus share, not "much improved" by the rule

| | 21m v0.1 | **21m v0.2 (metre)** | 9m with metre |
|---|---|---|---|
| judge-book bpb | **1.397** | 1.428 | 1.547 |
| natural copy gain | 0.639 | **0.774** | 0.737 |
| induction, one head / two heads (layer 3) | 0.15 / 0.30 | **0.51 / 1.01** | 0.42 / 0.84 |
| previous-word (layer 1) | 0.71 | **1.00** | 1.00 |
| the comparator / the mover (two heads) | 0.58 / **0.10** | **0.95** / 0.05 | 0.80 / 0.08 |
| words back / the rare word / the delimiter | 0.30 / 0.32 / 4.3 | **0.42 / 0.47 / 5.1** | 0.35 / 0.28 / 3.6 |
| the counter (R²) / premise / number / order | not read | **0.39 / 2.07 / 1.56 / 2.19** | 0.30 / 1.92 / 1.14 / 1.46 |
| period / refrain / again (lift) | 0.30 / 1.08 / 1.04 | 0.40 / **1.34 / 1.08** | **0.43** / 1.27 / 0.91 |
| one-hop lift | **0.25** | 0.16 | 0.14 |
| part of speech / Ogden kind / keyed word | **48% / 70% / 30%** | 46% / 68% / 29% | 43% / 65% / 25% |
| Versus median share of Qwen3 0.6B | 0.598 | **0.631** | 0.500 |

- **The rule (pre-registered 23:30): "much improved" if it beats v0.1 on copy gain and the Versus median share, with bpb no worse than v0.1's by more than 0.02.** Copy gain holds (0.774 against 0.639), the Versus share holds (0.631 against 0.598), **bpb does not: 1.428 against 1.397, 0.031 worse**, 0.011 past the allowance. So v0.2 is **better at what metre targets and better against Qwen, but not "much improved"**: it paid for its circuits in language.
- **The cost grew in the content phase:** v0.2 was 0.010 behind v0.1 at step 1,280 and 0.031 behind at the end. On the keel the fade arm ended *ahead* of the plain keel (1.821 against 1.853); the keel's pull was short (out by 25%), the 21m's long (out by 60%). The long pull holds the cards by heart (induction two heads 1.23 in the metre, 1.01 at the end) and costs the language; the short pull costs nothing and let induction go at the keel. The next lever is between them: the long pull at a lower weight, or a pull that ends by 40%.
- **Cards by the deck's rules:** v0.2 plays induction on one head (0.51) where v0.1 did not; the Nevermore is not traded in (copy gain 0.77, the mark 1.0); the mover is weaker (0.05 against 0.10). On the leaderboard's necessary cards v0.2 holds 3 of 4 (v0.1: 2, its two-head induction ending at 0.298), and so does the 9m with metre at 3M parameters; neither shows on the board yet, which takes one ship per class.
- **One seed each.** The plain 9m (the 9m's control) sails now; then the keel's second seeds.

## The full deck on the 9m: every metre and riddle card (8 November, 07:50, Justin: "faces are all duende, can we get all metre and riddles for 9m")

- **The scaffold, extended** (`backend/thorp/train.py`, `backend/thorp/riddles.py`): rules for the other eight metre cards (duplicate-word: earlier copies of this piece; the sink: the first piece; the delimiter, number-spotter, order-word, premise-marker: the earlier pieces of the card's class, the same classes their readings use; words back: two pieces back; the rare word: every earlier piece weighted by its rarity on the shelf). The two riddles, pulled on composed items beside the reading, never the tests' own: **the comparator** (other frames, years none of the reading's years or decoys: the pinned head's attention from the last piece onto the year to exceed) and **the bridge** (padded order items in invented words and the logic tutor's comparison words, "Each" for the deck's "Every": the logit lens at the chosen layer preferring the middle term's first piece to the second middle term's). A **floor** keeps the pull at a set weight after the fade; the pull is **read on 8 windows of 512 pieces** (the full batch would not fit twelve heads' patterns in memory).
- **The voyage** (`fu-ogden-v0-1~deck@9m-pre-s1`, after the 1m in the queue): the 9m with metre's spec with the layout the evolutions ask: layer 0 the sink, the delimiter, number-spotter and order-word; layer 1 previous-word, duplicate-word, the rare word and premise-marker; layer 2 words back; layer 3 induction (two heads) and the comparator; layer 4 the bridge (logit lens). Full weight to 10%, down to a floor of 0.2 by 60%.
- **Predictions, fixed now:**
  1. **All ten metre cards played at the end** (the deck's looks and evolutions, `wager.falls`): expected, with the floor holding them.
  2. **The comparator played** (two heads at least 0.3, above number-spotter): expected; it is near already (0.80 unpinned).
  3. **The bridge played** (at least 0.5 nats on the logic deck's padded rows, a different item set from its pull): a coin toss; its pull and its reading share the shape of the items but not their words or frames.
  4. **The cost:** judge-book bpb worse than the plain 9m's 1.501, by more than the 9m with metre's 0.046: expected; under 1.60 is the line past which the deck is too dear.
  5. **The Faces** are not scaffolded. If one trades in (the Nevermore or the Counter), it is earned.
- **Read alongside it, from the night:** the plain 9m (the control) came home at bpb **1.501** and copy gain 0.47 with 3 cards; the 9m with metre at 1.547 and 0.74 with 4: **metre cost the 9m 0.046 bpb and bought it 0.27 nats of copying.** The keel's second seeds replicate the first: the fade arm at 1.823 (seed 1: 1.821; the plain keel 1.853 and 1.860) and copy gain 0.75 on both.

## Ogden v0.2: v0.1's rule over the whole library, and the logic tutor's passages (8 November, 08:10, Justin: "we can have an ogden v0.2 but the type of content has to be approved by me"; "let's go with A and strike the Bible and then C1 as you suggest")

- **Why:** the 21m's readings were flat or falling by the end of v0.1 (67M unique tokens read four times), and the Mover, the Face most likely waiting on reading, has its look and its one-hop lift already at the keel and lacks only the Bridge. More unique text, and text that practises carrying a premise, are the levers; the content was Justin's to approve.
- **A, approved: the same rule, the whole library.** v0.1 scored only the two stores' 883 books. The library's other 1,217 English recipe books (no judge book; none of the 468 control texts with shuffled words) were scored the same way: 715 at 68% or more, 688 kept as new titles (one copy a title, none already scored), **48.0M words**. Struck by Justin: **The Bible, King James Version**. Left out by the period gate's rule: *Botchan* and *The Social Contract* (translators dead in 1959). Kept, flagged for Justin (only the Bible was struck): two official reports and a manual of games (*Second Shetland Truck System Report*, *Foster's Complete Hoyle*), and religious-titled books such as *The Confessions of Saint Augustine* and *Martin Luther's Large Catechism*.
- **C1, approved: the logic tutor's passages, template writer only**, at **7.5%** of the reading (the middle of the 5 to 10% proposed): 245,881 passages, 7.0M words, in 100 local books (`tutor-v02-<n>`). Two facts and the conclusion that needs both, in order, cause, membership and proportion, in strict Basic English with invented words; fixed frames, no model's text; every passage passes the tutor's own checks (no 50m-rung word, no run of six words shared with any test). Held out of all teaching, as the tutor was built: the counterfactual relation and three-fact chains.
- **Not approved, held:** B (a 64% bar), C2 (dialogue and plays), C3 (school readers and catechisms), D (knowledge shelves).
- **Kept fixed:** **v0.1's shared 2,048-piece tokeniser** (trained on v0.1's books), so every v0.2 ship can be set beside every v0.1 ship; the judge books held out; cleaning unwrap-v2; four passes.
- **The fitout** (`ships/fitouts/ogden-v0-2.json`, `lists/ogden-v0.2.csv`: 1,380 rows, about 92M words): built by `backend/scripts/make_ogden_v02.py` (deterministic: the scores, the cut, the tutor's seed). It reads beyond the stores with `data.opt_in` set to Justin's approval. The plain 9m on v0.2 (`fu-ogden-v0-2@9m-pre-s1`) is written, not queued: the control every v0.2 9m is read against, and against the plain 9m on v0.1 (what the text alone buys).

## The Smallest Bridge, pre-registered (8 November, 08:25, Justin: "a plan for an expedition using numerous size ships in order to get the bridge card and the mover and counter at the lowest size possible, the different ship classes would need to sail in order and take their learnings to follow up journeys"; 08:20: "get the rare word and premise maker into the model as soon as possible, then try simulate the bridge and then move to see if we can get the nevermore, counter and mover"; "yes, pre-register it")

- **The aim:** the smallest Thorp that plays **the Bridge, the Mover and the Counter**, by the deck's own rules and marks (`wager.falls`): the Bridge at 0.5 nats above the rare word and premise-marker; the Mover's look at 0.2 with the one-hop lift at 0.1, above the Bridge; the Counter's R² at 0.5, above words back. Faces are never pinned. A card counts at a class only on **two seeds**.
- **Each voyage in three stages** (`optim.scaffold`, a start `from` per head and riddle, added for this): **1, the Basics from the first step** (the rare word and premise-marker, with the sink, the delimiter, number-spotter, order-word, previous-word and duplicate-word); **2, from 5% of the voyage, the Stage 1 cards** (induction, words back) **and the riddles** (the comparator and the Bridge on composed items), once the Basics are held; **3, the Faces earned**, the pulls eased to a floor of 0.2 by 60%. On Ogden v0.2 the tutor's passages (7.5% of the reading) give the Bridge its exercise in the text as well.
- **The legs, in order; each carries the last one's recipe:**

| Leg | Ship | Fitout | What it settles |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 9m, all at once (`fu-ogden-v0-1~deck@9m-pre-s1`, at sea since 08:14) | v0.1 | can the Bridge be taught at all; what staging must beat |
| 1 | **9m, staged** (`fu-ogden-v0-2~staged@9m-pre-s1`) | v0.2 | the three stages at 9m; against leg 0 (staging) and the plain 9m on v0.2 (`fu-ogden-v0-2@9m-pre-s1`, the control) |
| 2 | 1m, staged | v0.2 | the 9m's recipe at a third of the size, its layout on 4 layers |
| 3a / 3b | three-layer keel: the Counter keel (the delimiter and the sink in layer 0, words back in layer 1) / the Mover keel (the rare word and premise-marker in layer 0, the Bridge in layer 1) | v0.2 | the theoretical floor (each Face costs 3 layers); two keels, two heads a layer being room for one chain |
| 4 | the keel, two layers (only for a Face leg 3 earned) | v0.2 | can it squeeze in, as the Nevermore did |
| ✓ | the smallest success, seed 2 | | the claim |

- **The rules between legs:** after leg 0, the Bridge's own method: played, carry on; rising but under 0.5, double its weight and raise its floor to 0.5, pulled at two layers; flat, the composed pull is the wrong method and the Bridge is taught by the text alone (the tutor's share raised). A ship that plays what the one above it played sends the recipe down; **a ship that fails where the bigger one succeeded sails again with the bigger ship as teacher** (its Bridge layer's logit lens and its Mover head's attention as the targets; the trainer for it is built then); a ship that fails with a teacher marks the floor, and the last success is the answer. The Counter stalling on Ogden text at every size is Justin's to decide (a varied shelf outside Ogden, or the floor accepted).
- **Predictions, fixed now:** the Bridge's smallest class **1m**; the Mover **1m** (its look and one-hop are already at the keel; it waits on the Bridge); the Counter **9m** on Ogden text (0.35 to 0.41 unpinned so far: the trend), lower only with more varied text; the Nevermore at every class it is tried (the keel has it held). Leg 1 beats leg 0 on the Bridge (staging is the lever) and costs less language (the floor is the same, the pulls start later).
- **Leg 1 is queued** behind a wait for Ogden v0.2's prechecks (its lexicon is being tagged), then v0.2's plain 9m; the expedition is on the Expeditions page (`fleet.EXPEDITIONS['smallest_bridge']`); leg 0 keeps its run id (only its label changed).
- **Ogden v0.2 ready (08:53):** 20 of 20 required prechecks pass. The lexicon (111M tagged tokens, 42 minutes) is built; the period check passes once the prechecks read each row's own book (they had matched *The Tale of Peter Rabbit* by title to another edition, and not checked the 100 tutor books and 3 archive books at all). The shapes evaluator keeps reading v0.1's lexicon for every fitout, so shapes readings stay comparable across v0.1 and v0.2. Leg 1 sails after leg 0 and the Pythia and Qwen scoring.

The deck's rules count 14 cards played at some reading (`wager.falls`); held at the end: the ten metre cards, the comparator, and the Nevermore near its mark.

The composed items were order alone; the deck's padded rows are a quarter each order, cause, membership and proportion. The pull carried at first and then narrowed to order. Leg 0's rule ("rising but under 0.5: pull harder") does not fit a solved pull, so the rule is amended, before leg 1 sails, on this evidence:
- **Leg 1b, added (09:50):** leg 1 with only the Bridge's composed items changed, to **all four relations**, built from the logic tutor's skeletons (two chains of one relation, the Bridge the middle of one, the distractor the middle of the other, then the conclusion's opening; the tutor's frames and words, never the deck's): `riddles.bridge_items_all`, `kinds: all`. Queued straight after leg 1 (`fu-ogden-v0-2~staged-all@9m-pre-s1`), before v0.2's plain 9m. **Prediction:** the Bridge at or above 0.5 at the end of leg 1b; leg 1 (order only) loses it as leg 0 did.

## The first 50m, and its reason for being: the chaining gain (8 November, 12:30 to 12:45, Justin: "the priority is to set sail on our first 50m with the goal of beating Qwen 3 4B on 15 of the 16 cards. So measure qwen3 4B, then the 21m run and then final signoff from me before launching our first 50m"; "making the chaining gain the 50m's raison d'être"; "let's go spy on qwen's weights to see how the deck impacts its chaining gains"; "we won't rent GPU for the 50m, we'll build support for nvidia GPUs while the ships are sailing, but the 50m's first voyage will be on this Mac")

- **The order:** (1) Qwen3 4B measured on all 16 cards and the cleaned judge books (queued after leg 1); (2) leg 1b; (3) **the 21m**: the full deck staged on Ogden v0.2, the Bridge in all four relations (if leg 1b holds it), the copy tack, and a release stage at the end (the pull off for the last 10%, so every card is judged by heart, as Qwen's are), sailing straight after leg 1b, ahead of v0.2's control and the second seeds (the queue holds for it: `specs/deck-21m.hold`); (4) **Justin's sign-off**; (5) **the first 50m** (38.8M parameters, 12 layers x 8 heads), on this Mac, one seed. NVIDIA support is built meanwhile in its own session, on its own branch.
- **The scorecard (to be fixed in the 21m's pre-registration):** a card is beaten when its reading at the end, after the release, is above Qwen3 4B's on the same reading; 15 cards, the Scales left out (no model read up to 4B holds it; its 8-item reading cannot place it). The Mover needs its look and its one-hop lift both above Qwen's (0.47, 0.30). Qwen3 4B's Bridge reads low (0.10): counted, with that caution. **The sign-off gate:** the 21m beats Qwen3 4B on at least 12 of 15, and the cards it misses are ones the evidence says size closes (the Mover, the Counter, copy gain).
- **The 50m's reason for being: the chaining gain** (the logic deck's: both premises against the stronger alone, on invented words, so knowledge cannot supply it), not the leaderboard's chaining lift (which needs the period science Ogden keeps out). Qwen3 4B reads +0.19 on the 8-item deck; Thorp at best +0.04; the mark is +0.10. Once launched, her job is to find which suits carry reasoning (knockouts) and push them (Wager rounds, the logic tutor's post-training with counterfactuals and three-fact chains held out).
- **The 40-item logic deck** (`lists/tricks/logic-40.csv`, seed 1860: 40 items each of order, cause, membership and proportion, the 8 hand-written counterfactuals; `backend/scripts/make_logic_deck.py 40 1860 logic-40.csv`), read beside the 8-item deck, never instead of it: evaluator `logic40` (metrics `tricks/logic40/...`), and `backend/scripts/read_logic.py` reads both decks with bands. *A slip on the way:* regenerating the 8-item deck with a larger word pool changed it for about a minute (to 12:32:32) before it was restored byte for byte; leg 1's readings at steps 16,384 and 16,448 may have read the changed deck (their one-hop lifts, 0.257 and 0.275, are in their range).
- **Spying on Qwen's weights** (`thorp/knockout_deck.py`): every card read by heads (the ten metre cards, the comparator, the Mover), its 16 strongest heads in Qwen3 4B silenced; each suit's together; the Bridge's best layer whole; against random heads of the same count (and a random layer), the chaining gain read on the 40-item deck with paired bootstrap bands. A card **carries** chaining when its drop beats the largest random drop of its size and its band stays above zero. Yesterday's knockout (four cards, 8 heads, the 8-item deck) found none necessary but could not have: one random draw moved the cause gain by -0.26. Queued after the 21m, with the 40-item deck on eight more reference models (where the gain, and the Scales, begin).

## The 21m before the 50m, pre-registered (8 November, 13:05; Justin 12:30: "measure qwen3 4B, then the 21m run and then final signoff from me before launching our first 50m")

- **The voyage** (`ships/specs/fitouts/fu-ogden-v0-2~deck@21m-pre-s1.yaml`, run `6819ee98`): The Smallest Bridge's leg 1b recipe on the 21m (8 layers x 8 heads), its layout unchanged (layer 0 the sink, the delimiter, number-spotter, order-word; layer 1 previous-word, duplicate-word, the rare word, premise-marker; layer 2 words back; layer 3 induction on two heads and the comparator; layer 4 the Bridge by logit lens, composed in all four relations), so layers 5 to 7 are left to the Faces. The Basics from the first step, the Stage 1 cards and the riddles from 5%, full weight to 10%, a floor of 0.2 by 60%; **the copy tack** (induction, alpha 3); the 21m's learning rate (8e-4, 300 warm-up steps); Ogden v0.2, four passes (20,840 steps); the 40-item logic deck read beside the 8-item one (`logic40`).
- **The release stage, built** (`optim.scaffold.release`, `backend/thorp/train.py`): from that share of the voyage the pull's weight is 0, past any floor (phase 3, `release`, in `scaffold/phase`). Here **0.9**: the last 2,084 steps read by heart, as Qwen's cards are. The copy tack stays on to the end: it weights the text's own loss and pins no head. Tested (`test_the_release`); a 12-step smoke of this spec and a 6-step one with every card and riddle started both trained (11.2 GB, about 1.15 s a step beside leg 1): **about 8 hours at sea.**
- **The scorecard, fixed now:** 15 cards (the Scales left out), each read at the voyage's last reading, after the release, by the deck's own readings (`wager.falls`), against Qwen3 4B's on the same reading (`data/compare/deck-mlx-community--Qwen3-4B-4bit.json`, measured after leg 1, before this sails). A card is beaten when the 21m's reading is above Qwen's; the Mover when its look and its one-hop lift are both above. Qwen3 4B's Bridge is counted with the caution that it reads low.
- **The sign-off gate (Justin's):** at least **12 of 15** beaten, and the misses only among the cards the evidence says size closes: the Mover, the Counter, copy gain (the Nevermore). Short of it, the 50m does not sail on this recipe without Justin's word.
- **Predictions, fixed now:**
  1. **The release costs little:** of the 12 pulled cards (ten metre cards, the comparator, the Bridge), at least 10 still played at the last reading, 100 to 2,000 steps after the pull came off. Expected: they were at the floor's weight from 60%, held largely by heart already.
  2. **The pinned Basics beat Qwen:** the sink, words back, the rare word, duplicate-word and the class Basics read above Qwen3 4B (leg 0's 9m already read 0.60 / 1.04 / 0.73 / 0.71 against outside models' 0.3 to 0.8). Expected.
  3. **The Bridge played at the end** (0.5 nats above its rare word and premise-marker) and above Qwen's 0.10: expected if leg 1b holds it; a coin toss otherwise.
  4. **The Nevermore:** copy gain at or above 1.0 with the copy tack (the 9m full deck read 0.96 without it); still under Qwen's (a 4B model's copy gain is near 4): a miss size closes.
  5. **The Counter and the Mover missed** (R² under 0.5; the Mover's look under 0.47): the misses the gate allows.
  6. **The count:** 12 or more beaten, a coin toss.
  7. **The language:** judge-book bpb under leg 1's 9m on v0.2 at the end, and under 1.43 (the 21m v0.2 on v0.1 text; a different corpus, so only a guide).
  8. **The chaining gain** on the 40-item deck under the +0.10 mark: the 50m's job, not the 21m's.
- **Before it sails:** leg 1b's Bridge read against leg 1's. If leg 1b's order-only rival holds the Bridge better (leg 1 at or above 0.5 at the end and leg 1b not), the spec goes back to the order-only items (`kinds` dropped) and this entry says so; the 21m is then inserted above the `deck-21m.hold` line and the hold lifted.

## The table stops at a blank line (8 November, 13:05, Justin: "Yes, change it")

- **What happened at the table** (the same ten lines, temperature 0.7, seeds 11 to 20, nothing kept): no Ogden ship ended its own turn (the 21m 0 of 10, the 21m with metre 0, the 9m 0). Each answer ran on to the length limit, past its first paragraph into figure captions and lists of names, and the table sent that text back as history, so the window filled with the ship's own prose and it copied it: the 21m was writing "[Illustration: Fig. 292.—The ship is a fair ship.]" by turn 4 and "ship ship ship" by turn 10, the metre 21m "PAGRESS—PAGRESS" from turn 2. Asked fresh (no history), the plain 21m's first lines are reply-shaped ("I have no name.", "I cannot tell you."), and 0.25 of each question's content words are in its answer against 0.00 shuffled (the metre 21m 0.03, the 9m 0.07; one seed, a crude reading, not Table Talk's check).
- **The change** (`thorp.table.reply`, `stop_at_blank=True` by default): an answer also ends at its first blank line (stopped `blank`), blank lines before its first words not counted; the next "Q:" still stops it first. `stop_at_blank=False` gives the old behaviour. The dashboard says "stopped at a blank line".
