- **After it** (the same ten lines on the plain 21m): 9 of 10 answers are cut at a blank line, each a sentence or a few lines, and the tenth runs to the length; the prompt at turn 10 is 450 pieces, not about 820. It still drifts onto a subject it wrote before ("ship", then "Paradise-Land" repeated in the last answer), and it starts writing "A.:" lines of its own, copying the turn format from the window.
- **Not turn-taking.** A blank-line stop is the table cutting, not the ship handing back the turn; Table Talk's turn-taking check (80% opening the next "Q:") counts only `turn`.

## The crowns, pre-registered (8 November, 13:20, Justin: "Who is the Queen of England depends on things ... what time period, what version of england, who is asking, someone from the monarchy or someone from the peasantry ... How do we make sure table talk includes those nuances"; "Yes, pre-register")

- **The question.** Does a model's answer to a disputed question move with the asker's standpoint? Who is the Queen of England depends on the year, the kingdom, the side and the station of whoever asks; answering needs the standpoint and the record chained, two facts into a third, which is the 50m's job.
- **Why an invented kingdom.** The real crowns (Stephen and the Empress Matilda in 1141, Jane and Mary in July 1553, William and Mary or James in 1689, George I or James III in 1716, and in 1930 no queen regnant: George V king, Queen Mary his consort, the kingdom Great Britain and then the United Kingdom since 1707) need history the Ogden library keeps out; a failure there could not tell not chaining from not knowing. They wait for the 100m and a library that holds the histories, and are the fair round for a bout with Qwen.
- **The deck** (`backend/scripts/make_crowns_deck.py` → `lists/tricks/crowns.csv`, seed 1141, the year Stephen was taken at Lincoln): 40 items, ten of each kind of standpoint, every word invented (the logic deck's `nonce_words`). The record names both claimants (copying cannot decide), half the items name the answer's claim last (recency cannot decide), and the asker's standpoint is in a second premise; the frame is "So to Pim, the king of Brell is".
  - *side*: "In Bilol the Borpan crowned Skixan, and the Drarpan crowned Brilkol. Felol is one of the Borpan." (Skixan, against Brilkol)
  - *time*: "In Filkol, Tonan was king before the ferp, and Rulet was king after the ferp. Drar lived in Filkol after the ferp." (Rulet)
  - *place*: "In Telket, in Zaskol they hold that Drolkol is king, and in Skunan they hold that Vitet is king. Nirp lives in Zaskol." (Drolkol)
  - *station*: "In Larp, in the fields they hold that Gandet is king, and at the court they hold that Gluxan is king. Drutan works at the court." (Gluxan)
- **Variants:** both, first, second, swapped, padded (another person of the rival's standpoint, named twice), one_hop, as the logic deck; **flip** (the asker's standpoint turned, the answer with it); **ask_disputed** and **ask_settled** ("Q: Who is the king of Brell?" after the disputed record and after a settled one with the same names, "That depends on whom you ask." against a flat answer).
- **Its own deck, not the logic deck's fifth relation:** adding it to `logic.csv` would move the logic deck's gain, the 21m's sign-off and every reading so far. Evaluator `crowns` (`thorp.evaluators.logic.evaluate_crowns`), trick `crowns_test` (goal class the 50m), metrics `tricks/crowns_test/...`: `gain` and `gain_crowns-<kind>`, `score`, the variants' lifts, and two readings, `moves` (the smaller of the lift and the flip's lift) and `ask` (the ask-back lift, disputed less settled). `score_deck` gained the three new variants' lifts; the logic decks have none, so their metrics are unchanged. Tests in `backend/tests/test_crowns.py`.
- **The marks, fixed before any reading:** performed when `gain` ≥ 0.10 and `score` ≥ two-thirds (the logic deck's rule); the answer **moves with the standpoint** when `moves` > 0. `ask` is reported with no mark: it is a Table Talk reading, expected near zero on any ship without the dialogue stage.
- **Expectation, stated now:** the crowns have the shape of membership (a person in a group, the group's choice), which no reference model chains on the 8-item logic deck (Qwen3 4B −0.09; it chains only cause and effect). The crowns may be beyond every reference model up to 4B; if none reaches the mark, the trick is reported as beyond the ladder, as the Scales was, and the 50m is not judged on it.
- **For Table Talk:** once a ship with the dialogue stage performs the crowns, a fifth check at the table: the framed question asked in turns, does the answer move with the scene line.
- **Not yet read on any model, and queued nowhere.**

### The Smallest Bridge, 13:20: leg 1 is home (the staged 9m on Ogden v0.2): **the Bridge held to the end**

- **The Bridge 0.569 at the end, at or above its 0.5 mark in all 10 of the last 10 readings,** with the pull at its floor (0.2): the first Thorp to hold the Bridge at the end of a voyage (leg 0, all at once on v0.1, rose to 1.37 and fell to 0.11). Its riddle's items were order alone, as leg 0's; what differs is the staging (the riddles from 5%, once the Basics were held) and Ogden v0.2 (the tutor's passages, 7.5% of the reading). Which of the two held it is for leg 1b and v0.2's plain 9m to tell apart.
- **At the end:** 13 cards played (the ten metre cards, the comparator, the Bridge, the Nevermore near its mark: copy gain 0.97, induction 1.19 on two heads); the Mover not (its look 0.06; the one-hop lift 0.29, its ability, passes); the Counter not (R² 0.33). Judge-book bpb **1.439** (the 9m with metre on v0.1: 1.547; the plain 9m on v0.1: 1.501). The logic deck's chaining gain -0.055 (the 8-item deck).

## The first 50m is a Table Talk ship, sailed on Vast.ai (8 November, 13:24, Justin: "I'd like to run my first 50m ... on vast.ai but it's goals are more table talk goals than beating Qwen 4B. I'd like it to still record all the logic deck and cards ... I want the first true Thorp model whereby I can have a conversation"; on the 21m's gate: "No, just record it")

- **This replaces the 12:45 plan** ("The first 50m, and its reason for being"), which put the first 50m on this Mac, aimed at the chaining gain and Qwen3 4B's cards. Its aim is now Table Talk's: the first Thorp you can converse with. It sails on a GPU hired through the charter (Vast.ai), once the NVIDIA path is proved.
- **Still recorded, not the verdict:** the logic deck and the 40-item deck (the chaining gain against +0.10), the 16 cards against Qwen3 4B's readings, and the crowns.
- **The 21m's sign-off gate is recorded, not a gate.** `fu-ogden-v0-2~deck@21m-pre-s1` sails as pre-registered, and its reading against Qwen3 4B (12 of 15) is written up as planned. The 50m's reading stage borrows its recipe but does not wait on its verdict. The 21m's entry says that short of the gate the 50m does not sail "without Justin's word": this is that word, for the Table Talk 50m.
- **Its checks** are Table Talk's (7 November): it opens the next "Q:" itself at least 80% of the time; its answers stay on topic beyond a shuffled pairing; its Forgotten Tomes score is no worse than the reading-only checkpoint (the boundary between its two stages) by more than 0.05 bits per byte; and Justin's own conversations at the table.
- **The order, and what is still to build:** `ships/plans/first-50m-vast.md`. The NVIDIA path is proved on Vast first: the parity test on a real GPU, the charter's shakedown, and a 9m twin within 0.02 bits per byte of its MLX voyage. Meanwhile, on the Mac: the dialogue library (its bar Justin's to set), the turn converter, the two check evaluators, and the 50m spec, pre-registered before it sails.

## The charter: voyages on GPUs hired from Vast.ai (8 November, Justin: "i'm going to use vast.ai can you build the code via the VSM system in order to leverage vast.ai")

- **A voyage may sail on a hired GPU.** It runs the same spec on the torch backend (`THORP_BACKEND=torch` on the box, so its run id is the spec's own). `thorp charter quote|sail SPEC`, `thorp charter audit`, `thorp charter terms`; the how-to is in `backend/CUDA.md`.
- **Laid out by system:**
  - S1, the voyage and the Vast.ai client: `viable/s1_operations/charter.py`, `vast.py`.
  - S2, the berths and the ledger: one hire at a time, the ship's papers (a small ledger, never the 1.2 GB one), the rows brought home: `s2_coordination/charter.py`.
  - S3, the quote and the spend log: `s3_control/charter.py`, `data/charter-log.jsonl`.
  - S3\*, the audit: an orphaned instance found and released, a ghost hire closed: `s3star_audit/charter.py`.
  - S4, the market read: the cheapest voyage, not the cheapest hour; each class's speed is measured once it has sailed: `s4_intelligence/market.py`.
  - S5, the terms and the policy `charter`, enforced, governing only voyages that ask for a hire: `s5_policy/charter.py`.
- **The terms, adopted as proposed** (8 November, Justin: "Adopt as proposed"; a change is a new entry here, set in `data/charter.json`):
  - **GPU ceilings:** RTX 4090 $0.50/h, RTX 5090 $0.75, H100 SXM $2.60, H100 PCIe $2.30.
  - **Host:** datacenter-verified only, reliability at least 0.98, a line of at least 200 Mb/s down and 100 Mb/s up, CUDA at least 12.8, 80 GB disk.
  - **Money:** **$10 a voyage**, **$30 a week** (Monday to Sunday, UTC), credit covering the quote 1.25 times over.
  - **Limits:** one ship out at a time; a ship is brought home and released after its quoted hours × 1.5.
- **The key** is read from `VAST_API_KEY` or `data/secrets/vast-api-key` (data/ is never in git, and `secrets/` is never carried aboard). It is sent only to console.vast.ai.
- **Not yet run against the real API:**
  - Built and tested against an in-memory market and a folder as the box, where the torch voyage really sails on the CPU, from the API docs as read on 8 November.
  - **Two points to check with the first key:** the GPU name's spelling ("RTX 4090" or "RTX_4090"; the client tries both) and the credit field of `/users/current/`.

## The parity run, pre-registered (8 November, 13:25, Justin: "Plan the parity run")

- **Why:** the first gate before the $100 is spent ("the PyTorch backend matches MLX at 9m"), and the first voyage on CUDA at all: the torch backend has been checked on the CPU build only. No 50m sails on a hired GPU before it passes.
- **The merge, on its own branch** (`parity-torch`, a worktree beside the live checkout, so the standing queue's code is untouched while the 21m waits to sail): `torch-backend` (the torch loop, the charter) merged onto `thorp-2026-11-07` (the release stage, the 21m). Two fixes on the way: **the torch loop's phase list lacked `release`**, so a released voyage on CUDA would have crashed at 90% (fixed, and its scaffolded test now ends in the release); **the quote priced FLOPs and setup only**, nothing for the evaluators or the scaffold's passes, so a 9m quoted 0.40 h would have been brought home by its deadline (the quote x 1.5 = 0.6 h) before it docked. Until a class has sailed a preset, the quote is now the FLOP guess or **this Mac's own wall-clock rate for the preset, evals included, whichever is slower** (`market.mac_rate`); once a class has sailed, its measured rate rules as before. The terms are unchanged. 170 tests pass (with `THORP_DATA` and `THORP_LEDGER` pointed at the live data: the worktree has none of its own).
- **Two voyages, one after the other on one hired GPU class** (one ship out, the terms' rule), both on Ogden v0.1 so the MLX twins are already home:

| Voyage | MLX twin (home) | What it checks |
|---|---|---|
| P1 `fu-ogden-v0-1~torch@9m-pre-s1` | the plain 9m: bpb 1.501, copy gain 0.47, 3 cards | the loop, the evaluators, the charter, end to end |
| P2 `fu-ogden-v0-1~deck-torch@9m-pre-s1` | leg 0, the full deck: bpb 1.550, all ten metre cards and the comparator at the end, the Bridge played from step 821 to ~3,500 | the scaffold, the staged starts and both riddles on CUDA |

- **The quotes** (no key yet, so at the terms' ceilings): each 8,216 steps, about **1.8 h and $0.88 on an RTX 4090** (H100 SXM $4.60, no faster on this guess), so both for under $2, inside $10 a voyage and $30 a week. The 4090 is the class to hire: a 9m cannot fill an H100.
- **Pass marks, fixed now.** A torch voyage starts from other random weights than its MLX twin, so it is a new seed and is judged against the seed spread (the keel's seeds differed by 0.002 to 0.007 bpb; the 9m's second MLX seed, `fu-ogden-v0-1@9m-pre-s2`, is queued and widens the band when it docks):
  1. judge-book bpb within **0.015** of the twin's;
  2. copy gain within **0.15** of the twin's;
  3. the same cards played at the end, give or take **one** (by `wager.falls`);
  4. P2 only: all ten metre cards and the comparator held at the end; the Bridge played at some reading (its MLX twin's was played and lost);
  5. the charter itself: the GPU name's spelling and the credit field read right, checkpoints and ledger rows home, the instance released, the spend log matching Vast.ai's bill.
  All five pass: torch is at parity at 9m. A miss on 1 to 4 is a bug to find before any 50m sails on CUDA; a miss on 5 alone is the charter's to fix.
- **Its other product: the 4090's measured rate on the 9m,** which the market uses from then on, and the first real measure of what a hired GPU buys over this Mac.
- **Before it sails:** a Vast.ai API key (Justin's to put in `data/secrets/vast-api-key` or `VAST_API_KEY`), an SSH key on the account, Justin's go-ahead to spend, and the branch merged into the checkout the queue runs from (after the 21m sails, so its code does not change under it).

## The coordinator: one queue, two lanes, this Mac and Vast.ai (8 November, 13:30, Justin: "we'll need to build the VSM coordinator that then coordinates between vast and then running on this mac")

- **Why:** the charter could sail a voyage on a hired GPU, but nothing coordinated it with the Mac. Written into the standing queue as a `cmd:` line, it held the Mac in port until the ship docked; run from another terminal, nothing kept the order between the two (a voyage seeded from one still at sea, the same run on both, a score of a voyage not yet home).
- **S2, `backend/thorp/viable/s2_coordination/lanes.py`** (run by `backend/scripts/queue.sh`, which now execs it; the log is `data/runs/queue.out` as before):
  - **One queue, two lanes.** `vast: <spec>` lines, and any spec with `model.backend: torch`, sail on the **Vast lane** (`thorp charter sail`, inside S5's terms: one ship out, $10 a voyage, $30 a week). Every other line sails on the **Mac lane** as before. The Mac keeps sailing while a hired ship is out.
  - **Order kept.** Each lane takes its own lines in order and waits at its first line (nothing jumps the queue). A voyage waits while a run it reads from (its seed, teacher or heads' donor) is queued above it or at sea in either lane. The same run never sails on both lanes. **`wait: <voyage name>`** holds the Mac lane until that voyage docks: the way to score on the Mac what a hired ship brought home.
  - **No key, no loss:** without a Vast.ai key the Vast lane's lines stay in the queue, untouched.
  - **What is out** is in `data/lanes.json`. The S2 lint reads `vast:` and `wait:` lines, and a chartered run at sea (S3's spend log has it hired, not released) is no longer reported as "lost at sea" for not holding this Mac's helm.
  - Unchanged: `queue-done.txt`'s format (a `vast:` line is written as queued), the queue file editable while it runs (its edits now under a lock), the helm taken by each voyage itself, and the wait while a voyage started outside the queue is at sea (Mac lane only).
- **Tested** (`tests/test_lanes.py`, 9 tests): the routing; the Mac sailing beside a hired ship; Vast lines waiting without a key; a seed in the other lane waited for; `wait:`; no run on both lanes; a lane waiting at its first line; `lanes.json`; a real `cmd:` run from the root. 180 tests pass on `parity-torch`. Read against the live queue (13:30), it routes every line to the Mac lane and idles the Vast lane (no key).
- **To bring it in:** merge `parity-torch` into the checkout the queue runs from **once the 21m is at sea** (so no code changes under the voyage choosing its spec), then restart the queue (`sh backend/scripts/queue.sh >> data/runs/queue.out 2>&1`, `caffeinate -w` on its pid). **Only one coordinator runs** (it holds `data/lanes.lock` and refuses a second), and queue.sh stays its parent process, so `pgrep -f queue.sh` (the First Light steering task's health check, which restarts a missing queue) still finds it. The running queue.sh has its loop in memory and would read a `vast:` line as a spec that does not exist, so no `vast:` line goes in before the restart. The parity run is then two lines: `vast: fitouts/fu-ogden-v0-1~torch@9m-pre-s1` and `vast: fitouts/fu-ogden-v0-1~deck-torch@9m-pre-s1`.

## One store: Postgres, by the viable system (8 November, proposed 13:40, approved 13:42, Justin: "Approve, start phase 1"; asked at 13:35: "throughout the codebase i'm noticing recording of data in a variety of places whereas postgres should store all data and relationships about our data model as per VSM")

- **Why:** Thorp's record is spread over three SQLite files (the ledger, 1.2 GB, 123 runs, 7.1 million metric rows; the library's manifest, 2,897 books; a hired box's ledger), some twenty JSON and JSONL files in `data/`, a dozen more under `data/compare/`, `data/tokenisers/`, `data/table/` and `data/acquisition/`, three CSVs the library writes, and the standing queue as text. Nothing ties a gate decision to the run it let sail, a crowning to the readings behind it, or a hire to the voyage it carried, except names that happen to match. Postgres (Thorp's own cluster, `localhost:5433`, database `thorp`) already holds the fitouts and prechecks (`fitout`), the Navigator's Wager (`game`) and, in hand in the main checkout and not yet committed, the sizes, ships and voyages (`ship`, Alembic `b8d83576f281`). This move makes it the one store.
- **One schema per system.** `s1`, `s2`, `s3`, `s3star`, `s4`, `s5`. The three schemas already in use move into them by `ALTER TABLE … SET SCHEMA` (no rows copied): `ship` and its tables into `s1`, `fitout` into `s3`, `game` into `s4`. Every table names the run, ship, fitout or book it is about by foreign key, not by a string that has to match.
- **S1, operations** (what is done: the library, the voyages and what they read):
  - *The library:* `books` (from `manifest.sqlite`), `book_measures` (from `data/trigrams.csv`: repeat rate, variety, name repetition per book), `lists` and `list_resolutions` (a `ships/lists/*.csv` list, each title and the book it resolved to, or none; from `data/resolution.csv` and `data/unresolved.csv`), `stores` and `store_books` (from `data/stores.json`), `intakes` and `intake_verdicts` (from `data/acquisition/intake.json`, `intakes/*.json`, `log.jsonl`), `tokenisers` and `tokeniser_books` (from each `data/tokenisers/<sha>/settings.json`, `books.json`, `stats.json`).
  - *The fleet:* `sizes`, `ships`, `voyages` (the other session's tables), with `data/ships.json`'s names, subtitles and archive marks folded into `ships`, and `crownings` (from `data/thorp-versions.json`: a ship crowned the class's version n, its chaining lift, when).
  - *The ledger:* `runs` (one per voyage; `voyages` becomes a view over it, as in the SQLite ledger), `metrics` (run, step, name, value, time), `evals` (run, step, set, book, nats, bits per byte), `checkpoints` (run, step, path, sha1, backend; the `checkpoint` artifacts), and `readings` (run, step, kind, the reading as JSONB: the cards, logic, stars, shapes, soundings and the other seventeen artifact kinds). `table_talk` (from `data/table/*.jsonl`: the ship, the question, the answer, when).
- **S2, coordination** (what keeps the operations from colliding): `queue_lines` (position, the line, its kind: spec, `vast:`, `wait:`, `cmd:`, comment; who added it, when), `queue_history` (from `queue-done.txt`: the line, its lane, started, finished, how it ended, the run it made), `holds` (a `.hold` spec), `lanes` (from `data/lanes.json`: each lane's line out, its process, since), `helm` (from `data/helm.json`).
- **S3, control** (resources and rules applied): `gate_decisions` and `gate_policy_decisions` (from `data/gate-log.jsonl`: when, the voyage, where, the verdict; each policy's how and why, linked to the run it let sail), the fitout model as it is (`fitouts`, `stages`, `precheck_items`, `fitout_prechecks`, `precheck_results`, its views), with `data/fitouts/*.json`'s aims and book lists folded into `fitouts`, and `hires` (from `data/charter-log.jsonl`: the run, the Vast.ai offer and GPU, the hourly price, hired, released, cost; the spend).
- **S3\*, audit:** `audits` (each orphan or ghost found, the instance, the run it was labelled with, what was done, what it cost). Written today into the spend log; given its own table.
- **S4, intelligence** (the outside and the future): `market_reads` (from `data/charter-market.jsonl`), `outside_models` and `outside_readings` (from `data/compare/*.json` and `chain-grades.json`: a model, what was read, when, the clean and context, the metrics as JSONB), `loop_fits` (from `data/loop/fit.json`), the Wager's `rounds`, `bets`, `players`.
- **S5, policy:** `policies` (from `data/policies.json`, each with when and in whose words it was adopted), `policy_exceptions` (from `data/policy-exceptions.json`, one row per policy and run, with the reason), `charter_terms` (the defaults and `data/charter.json`'s overrides, each change kept), `sampler_terms` (from `data/loop/sampler.json`, every steering edit kept).
- **What stays a file, and why:** checkpoints and their `state.json` (the weights and the step they belong to must be written together, atomically, beside each other; Postgres holds where they are and which run and step), tokeniser models, shards, texts, lexicons and the spaCy tags (large binary or text read straight into training; Postgres holds their path, sha1 and what made them), the hand-written lists in `ships/lists/` and the specs in `ships/specs/` (written and reviewed in git; their use is recorded, and a run keeps its spec in `runs`), logs, and the process locks (`helm.lock`, `lanes.lock`, the berth locks: an `flock` dies with its process, which is the point of them).
- **A hired box keeps its own ledger as a ship's log.** It cannot reach this Mac's Postgres on `localhost`, so it writes the SQLite ledger it carries, and S2's papers fold it into Postgres when it docks, as today into `ledger.sqlite`. A transit document, not a store.
- **No silent fallback.** One module (`thorp/store.py`) owns the engine from `THORP_PG`. If Postgres is down, readers and writers raise; the S3 gate refuses to sail (it cannot record its decision, so it does not make one); the dashboard says the store is down rather than showing files. When a phase is cut over, its files are renamed `*.imported` and nothing reads them.
- **In phases, each with its tests (`cd backend && uv run pytest -q`), against a test database:**
  1. *The small state:* S5's policies, exceptions and terms; S3's gate log, spend and audits; S2's helm and lanes; S4's market, outside readings and fits; S1's stores, intakes, tokenisers, crownings and table talk. Backfilled by an import (`thorp store import`) that can be run again without doubling anything. The running queue has the old code in memory and writes the files until it restarts, so this phase cuts over at a queue restart, with a second import of anything written since the first.
  2. *The library* (`manifest.sqlite`, the CSVs), when no intake is running.
  3. *The queue:* `queue_lines` becomes the queue; `thorp queue show` prints it as `queue.txt` reads now, and `thorp queue add / move / drop` and the dashboard edit it. Cut over in port, at a time agreed with Justin.
  4. *The ledger,* backfill and cutover: `ledger.py` keeps its API and gains a Postgres writer; new runs write both stores from the next restart in port; the old runs are copied from a read-only copy of `ledger.sqlite` taken in port; every run's row counts and sums are checked against SQLite; the readers (`train.py`, the evaluators, the dashboard, expeditions, leaderboard, the Wager) move over; the SQLite ledger is kept as an archive outside the repository. At a time agreed with Justin; never while a voyage is at sea.
- **Decided with the approval (13:42), each as first written:** the schemas are named for the systems; the queue goes into Postgres and is no longer edited by hand; the readings move as JSONB rows, normalised later; a hired box keeps a SQLite ship's log, folded in at docking. The questions as they were put:
  1. Schemas named for the systems (`s1` … `s5`, moving `ship`, `fitout` and `game` into them), or keep the topical schemas and note the system in each table's comment.
  2. The queue in Postgres ends editing `queue.txt` by hand: lines are added, moved and dropped through `thorp queue` and the dashboard. Yes, or keep `queue.txt` as the queue and record only its history in Postgres.
  3. The readings kept as JSONB rows in one table for now (the move is a copy, nothing reinterpreted), with the cards and the logic deck given their own columns in a later step; or normalised now.
  4. A hired box's SQLite ship's log, folded in at docking, as above.

## One store, phase 1: S2 to S5's small state in Postgres (8 November, 13:57; Justin 13:42: "Approve, start phase 1")

- **What moved** (Alembic `6d2adf2121f4`, after the ships' `b8d83576f281`; models in `backend/thorp/viable/records.py`; one schema per system, `s1` to `s5` and `s3star`, made by the migration):
  - **S2:** `s2.helm` (who holds this Mac's helm; the lock stays an `flock`, which dies with its process), `s2.lanes` (what each lane has out). Were `helm.json`, `lanes.json`.
  - **S3:** `s3.gate_decisions` with `s3.gate_policy_decisions` (every decision, each policy's part: kept, excepted, advisory, bargain, refused, and why; where it was asked; whether made here, aboard a box, or imported), `s3.spend_events` (hired, released, the cost; the rest of the line as JSONB). Were `gate-log.jsonl`, `charter-log.jsonl`.
  - **S3\*:** `s3star.audits`: each orphan or ghost the charter audit finds and what it did. New: the audit's findings were only lines in the spend log.
  - **S4:** `s4.market_reads` (every Vast.ai price read). Was `charter-market.jsonl`.
  - **S5:** `s5.policy_switches` (S5's switch over a policy's default, in whose words), `s5.policy_exceptions` with `s5.policy_exception_matches` (the runs and patterns, the reason, and anything else written, such as `follow_ups`), `s5.charter_terms` (every change kept, the latest in force). Were `policies.json`, `policy-exceptions.json`, `charter.json`. The policies themselves (statement, check, default) stay in `vsm.POLICIES`: they are checks, not data.
  - S5's decisions are now made with `thorp s5 switch POLICY on|off --words "…"` and `thorp s5 term TERM VALUE --words "…"`, not by editing a file.
- **One store, no fallback** (`backend/thorp/store.py`): one engine from `THORP_PG`. If Postgres cannot be reached, `StoreDown` is raised. The gate cannot record its decision, so it makes none and the voyage does not sail (before, a decision that could not be written was dropped silently). The dashboard answers 503 with `"store": "down"` and the page says so at the top (`/api/store`). After the cutover a file written by old code is read by nothing. The coordinator alone carries on when it cannot record what is out, saying so in the queue's log every pass, so a blip in Postgres never orphans a voyage at sea.
- **A hired box sails under papers:** it cannot reach this Mac's Postgres. The charter writes S5's switches, exceptions and charter terms into `data/charter/<run>.papers.json`, carried aboard, and launches the box with `THORP_PAPERS` set to it. Aboard, the gate reads its policies from the papers and writes any decision beside them, and the charter brings that log home into `s3.gate_decisions` (each line once) every time it brings the box home. Today the box's gate makes no decision of its own, because S2's papers tell it S3 passed the run at home; the path is there for when it does.
- **The dashboard and the gate read the same store:** the Governance page's decisions, switches and exceptions come through `vsm` (`gate_log`, `enforced`, `_exceptions`), which now read Postgres, as the gate does.
- **Tested** (`tests/test_store.py`, 9 tests, and the charter's, lanes', acquisition's and vsm's tests moved onto the store): the import brings every file in once and again writes nothing; a file changed after the import is brought in; retired files are read by nothing; with the store down the gate, S5's terms and the spend log raise; each decision is kept with each policy's part; a box under papers never asks the store, and its decisions come home once; the helm is recorded while held and cleared after; S5's decisions in words through the CLI; the dashboard says the store is down. The full suite: 195 pass, and 4 fail as they do on `main` in a fresh worktree (they read the real `data/`, which a worktree has none of). Tests run against their own database (`THORP_PG_TEST`; `thorp_test` by default, migrated to head once a session).
- **Read against the real files** (copies, into a test database, 13:55): 84 gate decisions, 2 exceptions, 1 switch, 1 spend event, 20 market reads; every one read back as the files have it; imported twice, nothing doubled.
- **Not in phase 1, still to come:** S4's readings of outside models (`data/compare/*.json`, `chain-grades.json`), S1's stores (`stores.json`), intakes, tokenisers and crownings (`thorp-versions.json`), the loop's fit and sampler, table talk; and moving the `fitout`, `game` and `ship` schemas into `s3`, `s4` and `s1` (decided at 13:42; done in one migration with the version table, at a cutover). Then the library, the queue and the ledger, as planned.
- **The cutover, at a queue restart in port, with Justin:**
  1. In port: no voyage at sea on either lane (`pgrep -fl "thorp run"`; `thorp charter audit` shows no hire out), and the dashboard stopped.
  2. Merge `postgres-one-store` into the checkout the queue runs from.
  3. `cd backend && uv run alembic upgrade head` (adds the six schemas and their tables; changes nothing that is there).
  4. `uv run thorp store import --retire` (each file brought in, then renamed `*.imported`).
  5. Restart the queue (`sh backend/scripts/queue.sh >> data/runs/queue.out 2>&1`) and the dashboard, after building the frontend (`cd frontend && npm run build`) for the store-down banner.
  6. `uv run thorp store ping`; the Governance page shows the gate's decisions. Run `thorp store import --retire` once more after any process started before the merge has exited, since it may write a file again and the import brings it in.

## One store, phase 1b: S1's and S4's records, and the systems own their schemas (8 November, 14:15; Justin, once phase 1 was written: "Yes, do phase 1b")

- **What moved** (Alembic `09d8a21e0199`; models in `backend/thorp/viable/records.py`):
  - **S4:** `s4.outside_readings`, every reading of an outside model (the decks, the knockouts, the logic deck, the book lists scored on them, the chain grades), each kept under the name its file had (`deck-EleutherAI--pythia-14m`, `chain-grades`), with its kind and model. Was `data/compare/*.json`. Read by `thorp/viable/s4_intelligence/readings.py` (`get`, `glob`, `put`, `chain_grades`): the leaderboard, the labs, Smoking Qwen, the card check, the outside deck, the knockouts, `grade_chains.py`, `read_logic.py`, `score_external.py` and ten readers in the dashboard. A number JSON cannot hold (NaN) is kept as null: no reading.
  - **S1:** `s1.crownings` (each version of a size, the ship crowned, its lift, when; was `thorp-versions.json`), `s1.stores` (the ship's stores as frozen, the stores rule reads their hashes from here; was `stores.json`), `s1.intakes` and `s1.intake_verdicts` (each intake's record and each book's verdict; were `acquisition/intakes/*.json` and `acquisition/log.jsonl`), `s1.table_talk` (each question put to a ship at the table and its answer, now with the run and step it was asked of; was `table/<voyage>.jsonl`), and the register of tokenisers, `s1.tokenisers` and `s1.tokeniser_books` (which books, which settings, the per-book stats; written when a tokeniser is trained).
  - **S2:** `s2.intake_holder` (who is taking books in; the lock stays an `flock`). Was `acquisition/intake.json`.
- **What stays a file, and why:** a tokeniser's own `settings.json`, `books.json` and `stats.json` stay beside its `spm.model`. They are part of the artifact a voyage reads, here and aboard a hired box, and its id is their hash. Postgres holds the register that ties a tokeniser to its books. The loop (`data/loop/`) stays as it is for now: it has been stopped since 7 November (its STOP file), and its sampler is edited by hand by a scheduled steering task outside the repository (`thorp-loop-steer`), with its runs, features and steering log beside it. It moves when that task is changed with it, which is Justin's call.
- **The systems own their schemas** (Alembic `a3f1c0de5b1b`, decided at 13:42): `fitout`'s tables and views moved into `s3`, `game`'s into `s4`, `ship`'s into `s1`, by `ALTER … SET SCHEMA`, so no rows are copied and the sequences and views follow their tables. Alembic's version table follows into `s3` at the next Alembic command (a migration cannot move the table its own revision is written to) and `fitout`, then empty, is dropped. `models.SCHEMA`, `GAME_SCHEMA` and `SHIP_SCHEMA` are now `s3`, `s4` and `s1`, and every SQL string and docstring that named `fitout.`, `game.` or `ship.` names the system. **For the other sessions:** after this merges, write `s1.ships`, `s1.sizes`, `s1.voyages`, `s3.fitouts`, `s3.readiness`, `s4.rounds` and so on, not the old names. The old migrations make their own schemas now and the fitout one keeps its views' SQL as it was, so a new database builds from nothing.
- **A box's papers also carry the frozen stores**, since aboard the gate's stores rule cannot ask Postgres.
- **The import** (`thorp store import`) brings each 1b file in too, and running it again writes nothing new. It counts whole lines, so two alike lines in one second (two books refused at once with no book id) are kept as two. It never retires the tokenisers' files. With `--retire` it renames `compare/`, `table/`, `thorp-versions.json`, `stores.json`, `acquisition/intakes/` and `acquisition/log.jsonl` to `*.imported` (the intake lock beside them stays).
- **Tested** (`tests/test_store.py`, 14 tests now): the 1b import once and as it was; the cutover retiring all but the tokenisers; a reading named as its file was, with NaN kept as null; a crowning kept; the intake holder recorded while held. The two tests of the stores rule now freeze the real store lists into the test database rather than read `data/stores.json`. The full suite: 202 pass; 2 fail as they do on `main` in a worktree (they read the real ledger and deck from `data/`). Migrations checked three ways: a new database built from nothing, then down to the Wager and up again; a copy of the live database's shape (version table in `fitout`, at `b8d83576f281`, with rows) upgraded, its rows and views found in `s3` and `s4` and its version table in `s3`; and `alembic check`, which finds nothing the models and the database disagree on.
- **Read against the real files** (copies, into a test database, 14:10): 18 readings, 17 models' chain grades, 6 crownings, 2 stores, 4 intakes, 3,591 verdicts in order, 3 voyages' table talk and 19 tokenisers, each read back as the files have it.
- **The cutover, for phases 1 and 1b together, at a queue restart in port, with Justin:**
  1. In port: no voyage at sea on either lane (`pgrep -fl "thorp run"`), no hire out (`thorp charter audit`; at 14:10 the spend log still had the parity run's hire of 03:52 UTC open), no intake taking books in (at 13:29 `in-20261108-132904` was), and the dashboard stopped.
  2. Merge `postgres-one-store` into the checkout the queue runs from.
  3. `cd backend && uv run alembic upgrade head`, then any Alembic command again (`uv run alembic current`), so the version table follows into `s3`.
  4. `uv run thorp store import --retire`.
  5. Build the frontend (`cd frontend && npm run build`), and restart the queue and the dashboard.
  6. `uv run thorp store ping`; the Governance, Leaderboard and Lab pages read as before. Once every process started before the merge has exited, run `thorp store import --retire` once more, in case one of them wrote a file again.
- **Still to come:** the loop (with its steering task), then the library (`manifest.sqlite`, `resolution.csv`, `unresolved.csv`, `trigrams.csv`), the queue, and the ledger.

## The purser: S3's allocation of the backlog between local and foreign waters (8 November, 14:20, **adopted 14:25 as proposed, Justin: "Accept all five"**; Justin: "Is the coordinator created to optimise our daily spend to get through the backlog of runs as efficiently as possible as per Beer's VSM"; "Design it first")

- **Why it is S3, not S2.** The coordinator (S2, `lanes.py`) keeps the two lanes from colliding: order, dependencies, one run in one place. It chooses nothing: a line sails abroad only when it is written `vast:`. Deciding where each voyage should sail, to clear the backlog soonest for the money, is the **resource bargain**, Beer's System 3: it allocates the operations' resources (the Mac's hours, the charter's dollars) under S5's terms ($10 a voyage, $30 a week, one ship out), on S4's intelligence (the market's prices, each GPU class's measured rate). Its decisions are written down where S3\* can audit them.
- **What it decides, and what it does not.** For each voyage in the queue whose expedition allows foreign waters: **local or foreign, and which GPU class.** Never the order: the queue's order is Justin's priority, and the purser keeps it (a line never sails before one written above it in the same waters). Never a `cmd:`, hold or `wait:` line (the outside-model readings run on MLX here). Never a voyage pinned by its line (`mac:` or `vast:`) or by `model.backend`.
- **How, every time a lane comes into port** (greedy list scheduling, explainable line by line): walk the queue in order; for each eligible voyage estimate
  - *local:* when the Mac lane is free, plus its time here (`waters.py`: the last voyage of the same corpus and size, else the size's mean, else the Mac's tokens a second for the size);
  - *foreign:* when the Vast lane is free (one ship out), plus set-up, plus its time on each class (the class's measured tokens a second for this size; for a size it has not sailed, scaled from a size it has by the FLOP ratio; never assumed faster than the Mac until measured), and its cost at today's market price;
  - **sail it abroad** when it would come home sooner by at least the margin, its cost keeps the voyage cap, and the week's spend with it stays within the **day's allowance** (what is left of the week's budget spread over the days left, so Monday cannot spend Sunday's money); otherwise local.
- **Who may sail abroad: each expedition says** (an S5 setting per expedition, `local` by default). A voyage is compared with others (a control, a twin seed, the legs of an expedition), and a torch voyage starts from other random weights, so a comparison is kept in one set of waters. Proposed: Table Talk `foreign` (the 50m, its seeds and ablations), every other expedition `local` until Justin says. On today's queue that routes nothing: leg 1b, v0.2's control and the second seeds are each read against Mac voyages. **The purser earns its keep from the 50m on.**
- **Before it routes anything:** the parity run passes (torch at parity at 9m); a class has a measured rate for at least one size; and a week **advisory** first, where it writes its call and the reason beside each line ("foreign: home 6.2 h sooner for $2.10, inside today's $4.30") and Justin's `vast:` lines still decide. Then S5 switches it to routing.
- **Recorded:** each call (the line, the waters chosen, both estimates, the cost, the allowance, the reason) in Postgres, `s3.allocations`, beside `s3.hires` in the one-store model, so S3\* can set the estimate against what the voyage took and the market against the bill. Shown on the Queue tab's plan, a column of its own.
- **Justin's decisions:**
  1. **The aim:** the backlog cleared soonest within the budget (proposed), or the least spent to clear it by a date, or weighted by expedition priority.
  2. **Which expeditions may sail abroad** (proposed: Table Talk only, for now).
  3. **The day's allowance:** the week's budget spread evenly over the days left (proposed), or free to spend early.
  4. **The margin to hire:** home at least 1 hour sooner and at least 25% sooner (proposed).
  5. **Advisory for a week first** (proposed), or routing from the start.
- **Decided (14:25, Justin: "Accept all five"):** the backlog cleared soonest within the budget; Table Talk the only expedition allowed abroad for now; the week's budget spread evenly over the days left; a hire only when it brings a voyage home at least 1 hour and at least 25% sooner; advisory for a week before it routes. **To build after the one-store cutover** (its calls go in `s3.allocations`, a table of that model): S3's module, the expedition setting in S5, and the plan's column on the Queue tab.

## The 350m's goals: the most understood 350M, and the best knowledge-free reasoner at its size (8 November, 14:22, Justin: "i like being the most understood 350m and the best knowledge free reasoner at its size, can we use those as our goals for 350m and compare where we are today vs Gemma 3 270m and SmolM2 360m")

- **The most understood 350M:** every ability it has is named as a card, located in its heads, and shown necessary or not by a knockout. Measured by the 16 cards (`thorp/outside_deck.py` reads the same cards in any model), and by knockouts (`thorp/knockout_deck.py`).
- **The best knowledge-free reasoner at its size:** the chaining gain on invented words, where knowledge cannot supply the answer: the logic deck (8 and 40 items) and the crowns, read with bands (`backend/scripts/read_logic.py`, which reads the crowns as well from today).
- **The rivals measured first:** Gemma 3 270M (`mlx-community/gemma-3-270m-bf16`, the base model, about 0.54 GB, under Google's Gemma terms) and SmolLM2-360M (`HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-360M`, the base model, about 0.72 GB, Apache 2.0). Both are queued on the Mac next, ahead of Qwen3 4B's cards: their logic decks, crowns and 16 cards, then the crowns at the last checkpoint of six Ogden voyages.
- **Size counted by the core, not the word table:** Gemma 3 270M is about 170M parameters of word table (262,144 pieces) and about 100M of transformer, so by its core it is a rival for Thorp's 100m, not its 350m. SmolLM2-360M (about 315M of core) and Pythia-410m (302M of core) are the true 350M matches; Thorp's 350m on the 2,048-piece tokeniser is about 302M of core and 2M of word table.
- **The quantum bet** (the $100 bet's 350m) is not changed by this entry.

## The dialogue candidates scored (8 November, 14:22, Justin: "Yes, score them")

- **The intake:** 1,510 books admitted and fetched, 157 already in the library, 226 refused (an author or translator with no death year), 1 failed. 1,667 scored (`backend/scripts/dialogue_candidates.py score` → `lists/dialogue-ogden-scores.csv`), as Ogden v0.2's books were.
- **At the 68% bar:** 485 books, 24.9M words: 394 plays (19.6M words, of 1,297 scored, median 66%), 39 books of table talk (2.6M, median 69%), 34 philosophical dialogues (1.7M, median 69%; Plato's *Lysis*, *Euthydemus*, *Parmenides* and *Protagoras* at 74 to 75%), 18 catechisms (1.1M, of 251, median 65%).
- **At lower bars:** 65%, 1,041 books and 47.6M words; 62%, 1,500 books and 68.6M words.
- **Justin's to set:** the dialogue stage's bar. At 68% the stage alone is about 27% of Ogden v0.2's 92M words, several times the 10% the 50m's plan gives it.

## Thorp's schedule: S2's jobs in Postgres, judged by S5's choice of model (8 November, 14:30; Justin, sent by another session: "thorps schedules tasks shouldn't be in claude code, but rather part of our VSM model and in postgres"; "Thorps most capable model should judge it, claude initially but eventually a thorp model")

- **Why:** Thorp's watches and steering ran as Claude Code scheduled tasks (`~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/thorp-*`), outside the repository and the store: nothing in Thorp knew what was scheduled, what fired or what it found.
- **The model** (Alembic `42266ae2e729`; `backend/thorp/viable/records.py`):
  - `s2.schedule_jobs`: one row per job: its key, title, the system that owns it, a cron (five fields, this Mac's local time) or one time, enabled, and either a prompt (put to the judge) or a command, its limits (`max_minutes`, 60; `catch_up_minutes`, 30; `cwd`), where it came from, and in whose words it was set.
  - `s2.schedule_firings`: every firing: the time it was due, when it started and ended, the judge (or `command`), how it ended (ok, failed, timeout, missed), the exit code, its last 2,000 characters, and the path of all it said (`data/runs/schedule/<job>-<firing>.out`). A due time fires once, however many ticks see it (unique on job and due time).
  - `s5.judges`: "Thorp's most capable model", every choice kept, the latest in force: a name and the command the prompt is given to on stdin. A Thorp model takes over by a new row (`thorp schedule judge thorp:<ship> '[...]' --words "…"`), not a change of code.
- **The runner** (`backend/thorp/viable/s2_coordination/schedule.py`, `thorp schedule tick|list|run|import-claude|enable|judge`): each minute `tick` fires what is due, each firing in its own detached process (`thorp schedule run FIRING`) so a long judge never holds the next tick. A due time is owed only after the job was enabled, and only while fresh (within `catch_up_minutes`). One later than that, or one that comes while the job's last firing is still running, is logged as missed, never fired late. A one-time job switches itself off once it has fired. With no judge chosen, a judged firing fails and says so. The launchd agent that runs the tick is written (`backend/scripts/launchd/io.thorp.schedule.plist`, every 60 seconds) and not installed.
- **Imported** (`thorp schedule import-claude LISTING.json`, from the scheduled-tasks listing): the seven `thorp-*` tasks, each prompt word for word from its SKILL.md (checked: every one equal to its file's body), each switched off, with the state it had in Claude Code noted in `source`. First Light is S2 (`10 */2 * * *`, the only one on in Claude Code); the loop's steering is S2 (`20 */3 * * *`, paused there); the four night watches are S2 and the spelling report S4 (one-time, all fired). `land-search-recheck` is not Thorp's and is left alone. Nothing in Claude Code was changed or deleted.
- **Tested** (`tests/test_schedule.py`, 11 tests): cron read as cron reads it (lists, steps, Sunday as 0 or 7, day-of-month or day-of-week); a due time fires once; nothing owed before enabling; a stale time missed, not fired; a job still running misses its next time; a one-time job fires once and is off; a disabled job never fires; the judge chosen in S5 gets the prompt word for word, and a second judge takes over with no change of code; no judge, no firing; a command job and a timeout; a judge that cannot start fails the firing; the import word for word, switched off, Thorp's only. The full suite: 213 pass, 2 fail as on `main` in a worktree.
- **For Justin to decide before anything fires** (proposed; nothing is installed or switched):
  1. **The judge's command.** No `claude` CLI is on this Mac's PATH; the only one is the desktop app's, at a versioned path (`~/Library/Application Support/Claude/claude-code-vm/2.1.317/claude`) that an update would move. Proposed: install Claude Code's CLI, then `thorp schedule judge claude '["claude", "-p", "--allowedTools", …]'`.
  2. **What an unattended judge may do.** Headless `claude -p` asks before running a command or editing a file, and unattended nobody answers. So First Light's watch (pgrep, `uv run`, fixing a bug, restarting the queue) needs a choice: a narrow `--allowedTools` list (proposed), `--permission-mode acceptEdits`, or no tools at all (it reads and reports only).
  3. **First Light's prompt is out of date.** It restarts the queue with `nohup sh scripts/queue.sh` (now `backend/scripts/queue.sh`, run under the S2 coordinator, whose session asked that nothing else restart it), and it steers a voyage whose round 2 may be done. Proposed: fire it once by hand from Thorp (`thorp schedule run`) after the one-store cutover, with its restart step struck; then switch it on in Thorp and off in Claude Code in the same minute, so it never fires twice and never not at all.
  4. **Installing the launchd agent** (a standing job on this Mac), at that switch-over.
  5. **When it lands:** with the one-store cutover at about 17:45 (Justin: "Yes, with cutover"). The cutover merges `thorp-schedule`, which carries `postgres-one-store`: one more migration, every job switched off, no judge chosen, the launchd agent not installed. Items 1 to 4 wait for Justin.

## A voyage abroad has no store: what it reads comes in its papers (8 November, 14:37; found by the session directing the expedition on the parity voyage)

- **What happened:** on the hired box (parity voyage `ce07ce94`, RTX 4090), cards, nevermore, stars and pips (and discovery) failed at every reading with "connection to 127.0.0.1:5433 refused", while training and the score went on. `thorp/fleet.py` reads the ladder of sizes from Postgres as it is imported (`PROXY`, `NURSERY`, `PARAGRAPH`, from `s1.sizes`), every one of those evaluators imports `fleet`, and a failed import is tried again, and fails again, at the next reading. The fake box in the charter's tests never showed it: it ran in a process that had already imported `fleet` at home, and could still reach the test database.
- **The fix, on this branch:**
  - The papers now carry the sizes, beside S5's switches, exceptions and terms and S1's stores, and `ship_store` reads them aboard.
  - Aboard, registering a ship or a voyage says plainly that it is done at home. The charter now does it at home when the box docks (`_register_home`), and says so, or says why not.
- **The tests now make a box as a box is:** the fake box cannot reach Postgres and caches nothing from home, a fresh process under papers must import `fleet` and the evaluators with no store (and without the papers it fails, as the box did), and a voyage aboard with stars, nevermore, cards, pips and discovery must come home with no `eval_error`. 215 pass.
- **Until the cutover lands, every voyage on Vast.ai loses those readings** (`main` has no papers). The parity voyage's lost readings are being rescored on the Mac from its checkpoints. Holding the other `vast:` lines until the cutover is the queue's call.

## The copy test, pre-registered (8 November, 15:05, Justin: "if the qwen3 4b is better at language and copying but it misses certain cards then we can put weights on cards that improve language and copying, no?"; "yeah do it")

- **Why:** Qwen3 4B reads a copy gain of 3.60, and our best 9m 0.97 (leg 1). It is not size: **Pythia-14m, smaller than our 21m, reads 4.21**, and its induction heads (1.25) look no better than the full-deck 9m's (1.06). Our heads attend as a copier does, but the model does not copy: a star lit with no trick. So the lever is the loss that rewards copying (the copy tack), not more attention pulled. The cards have cost language so far (+0.046 to +0.049 bpb at 9m) and bought none, so the test reads both.
- **The voyages:** leg 1b's recipe exactly (Ogden v0.2, the full deck staged, the Bridge in all four relations), the induction-weighted copy tack added (`copy_weight: {kind: induction}`: the guesses that followed an earlier copy of this piece, the ones a Nevermore can get right, weigh 1 + alpha):
  - `fu-ogden-v0-2~staged-all-copy3@9m-pre-s1`, alpha 3 (the 21m's own setting);
  - `fu-ogden-v0-2~staged-all-copy8@9m-pre-s1`, alpha 8.
  - The control is leg 1b itself (alpha 0), at sea today. Queued straight after the 21m, on this Mac (local waters): about 3.5 h each. If the 21m goes back to the order-only Bridge after leg 1b, these two follow it.
  - **Moved ahead of the 21m** (15:10, Justin: "getting the first 50m ship trained is the top of the agenda globally"): the 50m's reading stage needs the copy alpha and not the 21m's verdict, so the copy test sails straight after leg 1b (and the switch to Postgres), its answer about 02:00 on 9 November, and the 21m after it.
- **Predictions, fixed now** (at the end of the voyage):
  1. **Copy gain rises with alpha:** leg 1b about 1.0; alpha 3 at 1.2 to 1.6; alpha 8 at 1.5 to 2.2. Expected.
  2. **Still far from Pythia-14m's 4.2:** no arm passes 2.5. Its rest is reading (Pythia read about 300B tokens of repetitive web text; we read 170M four times).
  3. **The cost:** judge-book bpb worse than leg 1b's by 0.005 to 0.03 at alpha 3 and 0.03 to 0.08 at alpha 8. A coin toss whether alpha 3 costs anything (the guesses it weights are the easy ones).
  4. **The cards held:** the ten metre cards and the comparator still played; the Bridge unmoved by the tack.
- **The rule, fixed now:** the arm carried into the 50m's reading stage is the one with the highest copy gain whose judge-book bpb is no more than **0.03** worse than leg 1b's; neither passes, the 50m keeps alpha 3 (the 21m's). One seed each: a gap in copy gain under 0.15 is not called.

## Expeditions carry their rules: what each smaller ship settles for the bigger ones (8 November, 15:20, **proposed**; Justin: "an expedition needs a plan whereby the smaller ships provide info to the larger ships, so that an expedition can encode those rules for the final ships goals"; "I want to start the 50m in the next few hours")

- **The idea:** an expedition is not only a list of voyages. It is a set of **rules**, each a question a smaller ship settles for a bigger one: *what it decides* (a setting in the bigger ship's spec), *who settles it* (the voyage or voyages, and the comparison), *the rule* (how the result picks the setting, fixed before they sail), and **the fallback with its deadline** (the setting the bigger ship takes if the answer is not in when it sails). With a fallback, a big ship never waits on a small one: it sails with what is known, and what comes later goes to its next seed or the next size up. The final ship's spec is then **compiled from the rules' answers**, and every setting in it can say which ship settled it.
- **Where it lives:** Postgres, with the one-store model (`s4.rules`: the expedition, the setting, the voyages that settle it, the rule, the fallback, the answer, when and by what reading it was answered). The Queue tab's plan shows each small ship's row with the rule it settles and the big ship waiting on it. Built after the switch to Postgres.
- **The first instance, the first 50m (Table Talk, foreign waters):**

| setting in the 50m | settled by (local waters) | rule | fallback (if not in at launch) | in by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the Bridge's composed items | leg 1b against leg 1 (the 9m, staged, on Ogden v0.2) | all four relations unless leg 1 (order only) holds the Bridge at the end and leg 1b does not | all four relations | about 18:30 today |
| the copy tack's alpha | the copy test (alpha 0, 3, 8 at 9m) | the most copy gain within 0.03 bpb of alpha 0 | 3 (the 21m's) | about 03:30 on 9 November: **after launch, so the 50m takes 3** |
| the torch backend at parity | P1 (Vast) against the 9m's two MLX seeds | judge-book bpb inside the seeds' spread (or 0.015 with one seed); copy gain and cards once rescored | none: it must pass | P1's bpb has passed (1.511 against 1.501); the rest about 19:05 and 20:30 |
| the scaffold on CUDA | P2 (the full-deck 9m twin on Vast) | its ten metre cards and the comparator held, as leg 0's | the first 640 steps of the 50m itself: the pull's loss falling, every reading recorded | about 19:40 if P2 sails first |
| the release share | the 21m (its cards after the release) | keep 0.9 unless the 21m loses more than 2 of 12 pulled cards after its release | 0.9 | Monday: **after launch** |

- **So the 50m's reading stage can sail at about 19:00 to 19:45:** the Bridge from leg 1b, alpha 3, release 0.9, on the box with the Postgres switch's papers. What arrives later (the copy alpha, the 21m's release) is carried to its second seed and to the 100m.

## The parity run, P1 home and re-scored: three marks of four read, one missed by the letter (8 November, 15:25, amended 15:30; the session watching the queue and the charter)

- **What happened:** `fu-ogden-v0-1~torch@9m-pre-s1` (run `ce07ce94fd2f`) sailed on an RTX 4090 (instance 59661418, $0.342/h) and came home: 8,216 steps, 269.2M tokens, 30.1 minutes of training, released 14:52 (Mac time), **$0.323 against a $0.61 quote** (the quote priced the Mac's rate for the preset, evals included; the 4090 measured **648,539 tokens a second** training, about 149k over the whole voyage with its evals, against the Mac's 52,668). A first hire (instance 59661208) was lost within a minute ($0.005): the week's spend is $0.328 of $30. `charter audit` after: no instance, no orphan, credit $24.59.
- **Against the five marks (the parity run, pre-registered):**
  1. **Judge-book bpb: pass.** 1.5112 against the twin's 1.5014: 0.0098 apart, inside 0.015.
  2. **Copy gain within 0.15 of the twin's: pass.** `nevermore/natural_copy_gain` at step 8,216: P1 0.389, the twin 0.466 (0.077 apart). The other copy readings agree in direction: `copy_gain` 0.270 against 0.286, shared induction score 0.100 against 0.129, the counter's `probe_r2` 0.296 against 0.329. P1 is a little lower on every one.
  3. **The same cards at the end, give or take one: missed by the letter.** By `wager.falls` on the readings at step 8,216 (same evaluators on both, re-scored on the Mac): the twin plays 5 (delimiter, order-word, premise-marker, previous-word, words-back), P1 plays 3 (delimiter, premise-marker, previous-word): two apart. The two it did not play are both at their marks: **words-back** needs 0.30 on offset 2 or 3 and P1's offset-2 shared score is 0.297; **order-word** needs 2.0 and P1 reads 1.57 against the twin's 2.108 (itself just over). Ever played at any reading: 7 against 5, also two apart. (The pre-registration's "3 cards" for the twin was read before the pips and the order words were scored; it is 5 now.) A card on its threshold flips with the seed, so this is the case the seed spread is for: the second MLX seed `fu-ogden-v0-1@9m-pre-s2` (queued, local waters) gives the band. Until it lands, **mark 3 is a miss by the letter and open on the merits.**
  4. **P2 only.**
  5. **The charter: mostly pass.** The GPU name is read right in the ledger (`NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090`) and so is the credit field; 13 checkpoints and the rows are home (134,109 metric rows, 208 evals, 804 artifacts); the run is `finished`; the instance is released and the spend log ($0.3229 = 0.943 h x $0.342) matches the hours. **Not checked: the spend log against Vast.ai's bill** (no balance was logged before the hire; the credit has held at $24.59 since the release): Justin to set the instance's invoice beside $0.3229.
  - Also equal: the logic deck's score (0.425 both); its gain -0.013 against -0.009.
- **The re-score is done** (Justin, 15:20: "Yes, run now"): `score_checkpoints.py fu-ogden-v0-1~torch@9m-pre-s1 stars nevermore cards pips discovery`, all 13 checkpoints, about 6 minutes, run beside leg 1b with the helm lock switched off for that one process (no repo code changed; log `data/runs/score-p1.out`). **Verdict so far: marks 1, 2 and 5 (bar the bill) pass; mark 3 misses by one card and waits on the MLX seed band; mark 4 is P2's. Torch is not yet recorded as at parity.**
- **Three charter faults the run showed (none fixed yet; the first two were already approved for after P1):**
  - the box has no Postgres, so its `stars`, `nevermore`, `cards`, `pips` and `discovery` readings failed (the foreign-waters flag);
  - the upload is 2.05 GB, 1.65 GB of it book texts the box does not need (it took most of the 22 minutes between the hire and the launch);
  - `Box.launch` leaves its ssh call open for the whole voyage (`nohup ... &` over ssh keeps the channel), so the charter never reached its poll loop until the voyage ended: no mid-run pulls, and no overrun deadline. It docked correctly because the voyage was short. Fix: `ssh -n` / `setsid ... < /dev/null`, with a timeout on the launch.

## The first 50m's reading stage, pre-registered (8 November, 15:35, Justin: "I want to start the 50m in the next few hours"; "You write it")

- **The voyage** (`ships/specs/fitouts/fu-ogden-v0-2~table-talk-reading@50m-pre-s1.yaml`, run `c353647d`, foreign waters): the 21m's spec (`fu-ogden-v0-2~deck@21m-pre-s1`) at the 50m (12 layers x 8 heads, 38.8M parameters) on the torch backend. Ogden v0.2 read four times (20,840 steps, 683M tokens, about 18 a parameter); the full deck staged in leg 1b's layout (layers 0 to 4, leaving 5 to 11 to the Faces), the Bridge in all four relations, the copy tack (induction, alpha 3), the pull released from 90%; learning rate 6e-4 (the 21m's 8e-4 eased for size), 400 warm-up steps; full readings every 1,024 steps and light every 256, checkpoints every 1,024 (the plan's B5: at the 21m's rate the readings would fill the hired hours); the crowns read beside the logic decks. Its settings are the rules' answers or fallbacks ("Expeditions carry their rules"): the Bridge from leg 1b (if leg 1 holds it and leg 1b does not, the spec goes back to the order-only items before it sails), alpha 3 (the copy test reports after launch), release 0.9.
- **The dialogue stage follows** from this voyage's last checkpoint, as its own seeded voyage (`-post1-`), once the dialogue library, the turn converter and Table Talk's two checks are built. This checkpoint is the reading-only twin: the dialogue stage may cost the Forgotten Tomes no more than 0.05 bits per byte against it.
- **Sails** straight after the Postgres switch (about 18:50), so the box carries its papers and keeps every reading, **on an RTX 4090** (`--gpu "RTX 4090"`): it is the only class with a measured rate; the 5090 and H100 still quote on the bare FLOP guess, and the cheapest of those (the 5090, 1.2 h) would be brought home at 1.8 h, long before it docked.
- **The quote, and a fix to it:** the market read P1's rate as 648k tokens a second, its training steps alone; with its readings P1 sailed at 126k. A measured rate is now the slower of the two, and a size a class has not sailed is scaled from one it has by the FLOP ratio (`market.scaled`; `tests/test_market_rates.py`). On a 4090: **14.7 h, $5.01** at most, slow on purpose (a 9m left 41% of the 4090 idle); expected **3 to 5 h, about $1 to $2**; inside $10 a voyage and the week's $30 ($0.33 spent).
- **Predictions, fixed now** (at its end, after the release):
  1. **Judge-book bpb under 1.40**, below every Thorp so far (the 21m on v0.1: 1.397; leg 1: 1.439). Expected.
  2. **The cards:** at least the 13 leg 1 played (the ten metre cards, the comparator, the Bridge, the Nevermore near its mark). Expected.
  3. **Copy gain** above leg 1's 0.97 and under 2.5. Expected.
  4. **The Mover** (its look at 0.2 with the one-hop lift at 0.1): a coin toss; the first size with layers to spare for it.
  5. **The chaining gain** on the 40-item deck still under the +0.10 mark: expected; the reading stage is not where reasoning is taught.

## P2 skipped; the first 50m's reading stage to sail on Vast.ai (8 November, 15:35, Justin: "Skip P2, sail 50m")

- **Decided:** the parity run's second voyage, P2 (`fu-ogden-v0-1~deck-torch@9m-pre-s1`, the scaffold, staged starts and both riddles on CUDA), is **not sailed**. This departs from "The parity run, pre-registered", whose gate said no 50m sails on a hired GPU before the parity run passes. Torch is therefore **not recorded as at parity**: P1 passes marks 1, 2 and 5 (bar Vast's bill) and misses mark 3 by one card (3 against the twin's 5, both missing cards on their thresholds); mark 4 was P2's and is never read.
- **In its place:** the 50m's own first steps are the check. Watch the first 640 steps for the pull's loss falling and every reading recorded (stars, nevermore, cards, pips, discovery, the logic decks, the crowns), and compare the first readings with the 21m's and leg 1b's at the same step. The second MLX seed (`fu-ogden-v0-1@9m-pre-s2`, local waters) still widens the band for mark 3 when it docks, and the 50m's cards are read against it.
- **What sails:** `fu-ogden-v0-2~table-talk-reading@50m-pre-s1` (run c353647d, 861e9abf), the reading stage only, on an RTX 4090 (`--gpu "RTX 4090"`; the 5090 and H100 have never sailed and quote on the bare FLOP guess). Quote at most $5.01, 14.7 h, a 22 h deadline; expected 3 to 5 h and $1 to $2, inside the $10 a voyage and $30 a week.
- **When:** after the Postgres cutover (store-cutover, about 18:30 to 18:50), so the box has its papers and keeps its card readings (on main today they fail on the box), and with no hire out while the cutover runs. Before it sails: Drake's leg 1b Bridge verdict (drop `kinds: all` from the Bridge riddle only if leg 1 holds the Bridge and leg 1b does not), and a fresh `charter quote`.
- **Stopping rule:** if the first 640 steps show the pull's loss not falling, or readings missing, bring it home (`charter audit --release-orphans` if the charter is not running) and report before anything else sails. The dialogue stage is a later voyage from its last checkpoint.

## The first 50m's reading stage, first attempts: two lost hires, one crash on the box (8 November, 15:50; Justin: "Skip P2, sail 50m", "Fix launch bug, sail now")

- **Three hires, none trained:** (1) instance 59670112 (offer 48448055, $0.342/h) went `offline` before it came up and was released in 48 seconds, $0.004 (P1's first hire did the same); (2) instance 59670269 (offer 26474970, $0.402/h, quote $5.89) came up, took the upload (about 14 minutes) and launched, then **crashed 3 minutes into training**, released at 15:46, $0.113. The week's spend is $0.445 of $30.
- **Cause of the crash:** a hired box has no Postgres. The scaffold's `metre_flags` (train_torch.py) imports the evaluators at the start of training; `evaluators.stars` → `nevermore` → `fleet` → `registry.LADDER` → `ship_store.sizes()` reads Postgres at import (`psycopg.OperationalError: 127.0.0.1:5433 refused`). P1's plain 9m imported them lazily inside each reading, so the failure there only cost its stars, nevermore, cards, pips and discovery. **Any scaffolded voyage on Vast fails at step 0 until the box carries its sizes** (the "foreign waters" fix; Drake's box-papers commit 0fa42ef3 on `thorp-schedule`, which the one-store cutover merges). The 50m's reading stage waits for it. I should have ruled this out before hiring: the note that readings would fail on the box was true for P1 and I carried it over to a scaffolded voyage without checking the import order.
- **Fixed in `charter.py` before the sail (and tested, `test_the_launch_returns_at_once_not_at_the_end_of_the_voyage`):** `Box.launch` kept its ssh open for the whole voyage (the `cd X && cmd > /dev/null &` form backgrounds the whole list as a subshell that still holds ssh's stdout), so P1's charter never polled or pulled mid-run and its overrun deadline never ran. It is now one backgrounded command with all three streams redirected, and the launch ssh has a 120 s timeout. A failed mid-run pull no longer releases the box; the last pull is tried three times; a box that fails ten polls in a row is brought home as "lost contact". The new launch returned at once on the real box, and the crash was seen by the charter within a minute.
- **Decided (Justin, 15:55):** ask Drake to run the Postgres cutover now (the Vast lane is idle) instead of waiting for leg 1b, then sail the 50m's reading stage with the box-papers fix on main.

## The 1m's goal: solving De Morgan (8 November, 16:45, Justin: "ok so the goals for the 1m is not passing three word trigrams. It's solving De Morgan")

- **The goal, set in Postgres** (`s1.sizes`, the 1m, the pilot, through the new `ship_store.set_goals`): **all nine chapters of the maths book** (Scoring > Maths; Augustus De Morgan's *Elements of Arithmetic*, his Book I in his order, 72 sums in plain words, each against the rival of a learner's mistake). A chapter is learnt at six of its eight sums, the answer among the ship's ten guesses and above its rival; the ship has the book up to the first chapter it has not. Replaced: the four necessary cards, cause and effect and chaining two facts as the 1m's judges, and its Ask set of two-word contexts. Kept: the Forgotten Tomes beside, so the sums are not bought with the reading, and the pilot's other job, scouting data questions fast for the bigger ships.
- **Metrics:** chapters learnt (of nine), sums right (of 72), the Forgotten Tomes; *to come*, sums first (the answer as its first guess: the stricter bar).
- **Where it stands:** no 1m has read the book yet; the best 21m has 19 of 72 and not chapter 1. The library writes each sum in words a handful of times at most (158 "N and N make", 36 "N times N" in 1.8 GB), so **the goal needs a maths shelf in the 1m's reading** (a tutor in De Morgan's order, the book's sums held out): Justin's content to approve. Expected, before it sails: counting at the 300k to 1m, the facts (chapters 3 to 7) and one-step stories at the 1m, two-step stories the hardest (the chaining every Thorp so far misses).

## Each size's primary objective: the 300k the bigram table, the 1m De Morgan (8 November, 16:50, Justin: "the 300k must say clearly it's primary objective is to be the best at 2 word bigram table and the 1m's primary objective is to pass De Morgan")

- **Set in Postgres** (`s1.sizes`, each size's role, through `ship_store.set_goals`), worded "Primary objective: ..." so the page can show it as one: the ship page's heading and the **Goals tab's first box, now "the primary objective"** (it had printed the same line, "as many tricks as it can learn", for every size). The tricks stay, under it, as what comes beyond the objective.
- **The 300k: the best at the two-word bigram table.** Its next word guessed as well as Ogden's keyed table of word pairs guesses it (853 x 853: the 850 words, `<name>`, `<number>`, `<thing>`; 7 November, "Ogden v0.1"), or better. Read by `shapes/keyed_top1` (its top guess the right keyed word at the judge books' points) against **the table's own 30.3%** (every word keyed); *to come*, the Ogden-word test pre-registered on 7 November (top 1 and 5 against the table's 16.5% and 43.3%). **Where it stands:** the best 300k at **21.0%** (`fu-ogden-v0-1~metre@300k-pre-s1`), about 69% of the way; the 1m 23.7%, the 9m 26.5%, the 21m 29.5%. Its old judges (the four cards, cause and effect, chaining) are kept beside as what it carries up, not as the objective.
- **The 1m: pass De Morgan** (the entry above, worded as its primary objective).

## Prune the 300k and the 1m to their top five ships (8 November, Justin: "we need to clean up all the versions of 300k and 1m, can we just prune it down to the top 5 for both of them")

- **Status: applied** (8 November, Justin: "Yes, retire", "Merge, then retire"). The branch was merged to main, the live store migrated, and `scripts/ship_prune.py --apply` retired 47 of the 52 300k ships and 29 of the 34 1m ships. Kept: the five Ogden v0.1 300k ships (keyed_top1 0.2019 to 0.2100) and, for the 1m, Ogden v0.1 (0.2375), Two facts round 2 (pilot with rope), First Light pathfinder, vocabulary 16,384 and learning rate 4e-3 (the last four by Forgotten Tomes bpb, having no keyed reading). The Ogden v0.1 controls are all kept. All 123 voyages remain in `s1.voyages`.
- **Archive, never delete.** `s1.ships` gains `status` ("in service" or "retired") and `retired_note` (Alembic `c7e4a1d90b52`; `archived` keeps its old meaning, the First Fleet). A retired ship stays in Postgres with its voyages; the ledger, checkpoints, specs and these entries are untouched. Each size's **Fleet tab** hides retired ships and folds them under "Retired ships"; `scripts/ship_prune.py` shows the ranking, `--apply` retires, `--restore` undoes it.
- **Ranking:** each ship by its best voyage's last reading of `shapes/keyed_top1`, higher the better. The 300k's primary objective is that; the 1m's is De Morgan, which no voyage records yet, so **the 1m is ranked by `shapes/keyed_top1` too until it does**. Only one 1m ship has a keyed reading, so the other four places go by the Forgotten Tomes bits per byte (lower is better), as does every ship without a reading. Ogden v0.1 ships are the controls for current work and stay in service wherever they rank.

## The 300k is Pan, the 1m Augustus (8 November, 17:00, Justin: "the 300k should be called Pan and the 1m should be named Augustus"; "the fal images in the ui need to reflect pan and augustus and not the boats": "pan playing cards with the universe and augustus teaching maths")

- **The names** in `s1.sizes` (through `ship_store.set_goals(name=...)`): 300k **Pan** (was the keel, before it the seedling), 1m **Augustus** (was the pilot, before it the proxy): Augustus for De Morgan, whose book is its objective. A size's name is its page's address (`#/sizes/pan`, `#/sizes/augustus`); `CLASS_RENAMES` (server.py and the dashboard) maps every older name straight to the new one, so `#/sizes/keel` opens Pan's page. The code that knew a size by its old name follows the alias (`_class_names`: the subjects' own tricks, the Ask question sets).
- **Their art** (`generate_art.py`, `pan`, `augustus`, made with fal in the cards' noir): Pan alone at a card table under the open sky, playing his cards against a spiral galaxy; Augustus a Victorian master teaching sums at a blackboard by gaslight. The other sizes keep their ships.

## Pan names the parts of speech it looks for (8 November, 17:10, Justin: "the 300k is to also identify parts of speech in its guesses so that we can map its guesses to what it thinks the correct parts of speech it's looking for")

- **Every guess on the page is tagged** (`/api/table/guesses`: each guess's `tag` and `coarse`), and the panel says **what the ship is looking for**: its ten guesses' probability summed by part of speech, in the fine tags (NOUN, VERB, ADJ, ADV, PUNCT, BREAK...) and Ogden's coarse tier (a thing, a quality, an operation, a mark). Ask's run-all table has a column of it. The lexicon and the rule are the shapes evaluator's (a word's commonest tag in the Ogden corpus; a line break BREAK), so the page and the voyages' readings agree. Example, a Pan ship after "The old man walked into the": NOUN 87% (door, window, tree), ADV 10% (back), ADJ 2%.
- **Pan's goals** (`s1.sizes`) carry it, second after the bigram table: **parts of speech**, its top guess the right part of speech at the judge books' points (`shapes/upos_top1`). Where it stands: the best Pan ship 37.2% (`fu-ogden-v0-1@300k-pre-s1`), the 9m 44%, the 21m 48%.

## Phaleron's fourteen hours: Pan and Augustus as near their objectives as they can get (8 November, 17:25, Justin: "can we also clear the queue for local waters. Let's name it after a famous bay, perhaps that the greeks used to train ships in and then the local waters needs a plan to run for the next 14 hours to get Pan and Augustus as close as possible to achieving their goals")

- **Local waters are Phaleron** (the plan page, `waters.py`): Athens' first harbour, where its early fleet lay and sailed from before Themistocles built Piraeus beside it for the triremes of Salamis. A sheltered bay for the youngest ships.
- **The queue cleared** (17:20): eight lines, kept in their order in `ships/specs/queue-removed-2026-11-08.txt` (leg 1b's resume, the 21m's hold, the copy test's two arms, the knockout, the Pythia logic readings, v0.2's plain 9m, the 1m's second seed). The MLX 9m second seed, at sea since 16:03, docks first: it is the parity run's seed spread.
- **Pan, round 1 (queued):** Pan's ten voyages so far all read 0.200 to 0.210 on its objective (`shapes/keyed_top1`; the bigram table 0.303), whatever the lever (the metre scaffold, three layers, the copy tack): the seeds' spread, ±0.008. So round 1 tries other levers, one seed each, on Ogden v0.1 against the plain Pan (0.208, 0.200):

| voyage | lever | about |
|---|---|---|
| `~pan-lr6` | learning rate 0.006 | 15 min |
| `~pan-lr15` | learning rate 0.0015 | 15 min |
| `~pan-ctx128` | context 128 pieces (a bigram needs one word back) | 15 min |
| `~pan-l1w96` | one layer 96 wide, not two 64 wide (a new shape, `300k-l1w96`, about the same weights) | 15 min |
| `~pan-p8` | eight passes, not four | 30 min |
| `fu-ogden-v0-2~pan-v02` | Ogden v0.2, two and a half times the unique text | 40 min |
| `~pan-teach5` | **the 21m as its teacher** (`fu-ogden-v0-1@21m-pre-s1`, 0.295 on the table; alpha 0.5, temperature 2; the same tokeniser) | 2.3 h (the 21m runs on every step) |

  The 21m as a teacher at alpha 0.9 is written (`~pan-teach9`) but not queued: at 2.3 hours a taught voyage, one arm is what fourteen hours allow.
- **Predictions, fixed now:** the teacher moves Pan most, to **0.23 to 0.26** (it hands Pan the 21m's word-pair odds directly), still short of the table; one layer 96 wide a coin toss for a point; the learning rates, eight passes and the short context within the seeds' spread; Ogden v0.2 up a point at most (more pairs, but the judge points are the same). **No arm reaches 0.303 in round 1.**
- **Round 2 (about 2.5 h, queued when round 1 is read):** every lever that beats the plain Pan by more than 0.01 combined in one ship, two seeds, read on the bigram table and the parts of speech.
- **Augustus (proposed, about 5 h, needs Justin's approval of its reading):** nothing in the library teaches sums often enough (158 "N and N make" in 1.8 GB), so no lever without a maths shelf can move De Morgan. The plan: (1) a **maths tutor** in Basic English in De Morgan's order (counting up and down to thirty, the addition and times tables, sharing, halves and doubles, stories of one and two steps), every sum of the maths book and every run of six of its words held out, written by a deterministic generator as the logic tutor's passages are; (2) a **`maths` evaluator**, the maths book read during voyages (chapters learnt, sums right, sums first), so Augustus is measured as it sails; (3) Augustus on Ogden v0.1 with the tutor at **2%, 5%, 10% and 20%** of the reading, about 40 minutes each, against the plain Augustus (`fu-ogden-v0-1@1m-pre-s1`, and its second seed put back as the control); (4) the best share on a second seed. Expected: counting and the facts (chapters 1 to 7) at 5% or more; one-step stories at 10% or more; two-step stories a coin toss at any share. The Forgotten Tomes beside, so the sums are not bought with the reading.



## Fitouts compose their books: weights and filters, with sources tracked (8 November, Justin: "our fitouts or the part of it that controls what the books are should be able to have weights, so I should be able to weight by language. Obviously that's 100% English now but in future will include other languages. Then it should weight by ogdenish as we already do, then also choose a time period, both a start and an end date, then it should have filters for content type.
