Novels, poems, textbooks, etc."; "also we need to have sources, things like the library and the clergy for example will have different biases. We need to track this early and transparently"; "start off with the UI for library so that I can sign that off ... structured by filters and cards for all the kind/date/language/collection (things like renaissance and mayflower collections)")

**Status: the Library page's branches and variations approved by Justin (8 November: "i love it"; "Looks right"); the rules for the year, kind and voice, and merging and migrating, still await his word.** Pre-registered. Nothing here changes Ogden v0.1, Ogden v0.2 or any fitout that sails, and nothing is applied to the live database** (the Alembic revision `d0ba1dfea619` is on the branch `library-selection`, not run on `thorp`). Built and shown so far: the Library page's Browse section, the Fitouts tab's Selection panel, the composing command, all tried against a scratch database.

- **The design.** A fitout's stage declares its books as a *selection* (`thorp/selection.py`, stored in `s3.stages.compose`) instead of pointing at a hand-made list. `thorp select compose <fitout>` reads the selection and the library's *facts* (`thorp/library_facts.py`, one row a book, `s3.library_facts`) and writes a fixed list, `lists/composed/<fitout>-<stage>.csv`, as a recipe reads it; the fitout's own shelf is never touched. Every composing is a row in `s3.compositions`: the selection and its hash, the facts' fingerprint, the list and its sha1, the counts, the funnel of rules (books left after each), and the gate's library prechecks (period, decks, stores) read on the list. Deterministic: the same facts and selection write the same bytes.
- **What a selection can say.** (1) **Language weights**, shares of the words (`{en: 1.0}` today); a language named with no book to fill it is an error, never a silent zero. (2) **Ogden-ness**, a bar (the score `lists/ogden-*-scores.csv` already holds; a book on the bar is scored again from its text, because the files round to four places) and the rank a trim keeps. It is not yet a sampling weight. (3) **A period**, `start` and `end` years, inclusive. (4) **Filters** by kind, voice, source and bookshelf, include and exclude; and **weights** by kind, voice or source, as shares of the words, trimmed lowest-ranked first to the largest total that fills them all. Also: `as_of` (the library as it stood that day), `dedupe` (one copy a title), `carry` (a list kept whole), `strike` (named books, with the reason), `append` (a list added after, as the tutor's passages are), a word budget.
- **Always on, whatever the selection says** (the gate's rules, not choices): `recipe` books only, none read by the decks, no shuffled control, no credited name dead after 1955 (S5 `period`).
- **Which date: the writer's year, estimated, with its basis kept.** No catalogue we hold carries a publication or first-edition year (Gutenberg's "Issued" is the day the file was released), so a *publication* or *first-edition* period cannot be applied honestly yet. What it carries is the credited names' birth and death years, which is what the gate already reads. A book's year is, in order: **published** (the year recorded when archive.org books were fetched: 16 books); **floruit**, the writer's birth year + 40, no later than their death (the classical age of a writer's prime: 3,298 books); **late**, death - 25 where only the death is known (16); else **undated** (322, mostly Britannica articles, *Punch* and *Notes and Queries* volumes, volumes by "Various", and ancient dramatists with no dates). "The writer" is the translator if one is credited (it is their English), else the first credited author, else the first credited name with dates. **A book with no date** has no year: a period with bounds leaves it out unless it says `undated: include`; with no bounds it is read as usual. The gate's death-year rule is separate and always on. A true publication year (Wikidata, Open Library) can be added later as a `year` override (`lists/library-overrides.csv`), and the basis then says so.
- **Kinds** (one to a book, the first rule that matches, with the rule kept): periodical, sermon, dialogue, textbook, play, poem, stories, novel, essay, biography, travel, reference, history, philosophy, religion, science, practical, other; "derived" is Thorp's own texts. The rules read the catalogue's subjects, then its bookshelves ("Category: Novels"), then its LoC class, then the title; the order is the judgement (a sermon on a play is a sermon). Sixteen archive.org books with no subjects are set by hand in `lists/library-overrides.csv`.
- **Sources, tracked early and in the open** (Justin: the library and the clergy have different biases). Two columns, both shown on every count and every book, each with its rule: **source**, the collection a text was fetched from (gutenberg 3,636 books; archive.org 16; `local` is Thorp's own derived texts, shown separately); and **voice**, who speaks: *clergy* (the Church's voice: a clerical title after the surname in the credit, sermons, catechisms, prayer books), *press*, *official*, *program* (the logic tutor and the shuffled controls), *lay* (any other named writer). **Said plainly, the voice undercounts:** it is read from the credit and subjects, so a priest who wrote a novel under his own name is missed, and Olcott's *Buddhist Catechism* is counted with the Church. A selection may filter or weight by either, and the Fitouts tab shows each fitout's share by voice and source beside its share by kind.
- **The Library starts with branches** (Justin, later the same day: "it has to start with branches of the library and those branches will have different purposes. So currently we have Ogden with variations on it. So I'd like to be able to open the Ogden branch and then explore what's in that branch"). A **branch** is a purpose books are gathered for; it holds **variations**, each a set of books by a list, by a selection (composed, no list written) or all of the library, with what it was derived from and whether it is a draft. Opening the Library shows the branches; opening one shows its variations (books, words, and what each adds to and drops from the one it came from); picking some, or none for all of them together, scopes the same cards and filters to those books. Stored in `s3.library_branches` and `s3.library_variations` (revision `93d94cd08802`), written by `thorp select branches` and never over what Justin changes by hand. **The defaults, mine to propose and his to reshape:** *Ogden* (v0.1 592 books; v0.2 1,380, +788 from v0.1; v0.2 declared, a draft, the same books; "Ogden-ness 68%+, now", a selection of 1,305, +26 and -101 against v0.2, which is what a v0.3 could draw on); *The whole library* (3,652); *Decks and yardsticks* (hole cards, hole cards II, the Forgotten Tomes, truths, held Greek, Islamic and Renaissance); *Content experiment* (Greek, Islamic, Renaissance, about, random 1 to 3); *Data levers and pools* (the 100/200/400/800 levers, the speller's fiction and non-fiction, "Mayflower"'s shelf, the mixed pool, English-original and shuffled fiction, verse, the loop pool); *The stores and early shelves* (the two stores, the nursery, the legal, reasoning and cause shelves, dialogue, Britannica, the precursors).
- **Collections** are the named shelves and decks a book belongs to (Renaissance, Greek, Islamic, the nursery, the legal shelf, the stores, the Forgotten Tomes and the other decks, the two Ogden corpora...), a book in several. Inside a branch every collection is a card, dim where the view holds none of its books (the Forgotten Tomes among Ogden's: none read, which is the held-out check made visible). **The Library page's old Fitouts table is retired** (Justin: "retire this section"); a fitout's weights, filters, rules and counts now show under the variation it reads (pick Ogden v0.2 in the Ogden branch). The class pages' own Fitouts tab and the fitout builder are unchanged.
- **The counts, per kind, shown before they are relied on** (the 3,652 library books, 242.1M words): play 1,021 (44.3M), novel 578 (46.7M), periodical 354 (7.5M), poem 277 (17.5M), stories 277 (17.2M), history 201 (22.6M), essay 162 (9.0M), reference 150 (29.5M), textbook 146 (7.8M), biography 129 (11.6M), philosophy 128 (10.3M), science 80 (7.9M), other 53 (3.3M), travel 36 (3.7M), dialogue 30 (1.4M), practical 23 (1.0M), sermon 5 (0.7M), religion 2 (0.2M). By language: English 3,647 (241.6M), Chinese 2, Portuguese 2, German 1. By period: before 1500 6; 1500-1599 27; 1600-1699 287; 1700-1799 195; 1800-1849 331; 1850-1899 1,611 (101.2M); 1900-1949 873 (46.1M); undated 322 (30.1M). By voice: lay 3,271, press 354, clergy 26, official 1. Of the 1,306 books at Ogden-ness 68% or more: novel 466, stories 222, poem 148, textbook 88, essay 83, biography 68, history 61, philosophy 60. **The library holds almost no sermons (5) and no religion (2): it is what was fetched for the Ogden work, not Gutenberg (79,712 titles).** A selection can only weight what is fetched.
- **Ogden v0.2 reproduced from its declared selection** (`ships/fitouts/ogden-v0-2-declared.json`, a draft that never sails; `thorp select compose ogden-v0-2-declared --against lists/ogden-v0.2.csv`): v0.1's list carried, the library as of 7 November, scored by the v0.2 scoring, Ogden-ness 68% or more, one copy a title (the first id read as text, as the maker sorted), the Bible struck, the tutor's passages appended. **1,380 rows, the same books in the same order: the list is byte for byte `lists/ogden-v0.2.csv` (sha1 df4394ec2806ff2344aa3834312db9d3e6919069).** The gate's prechecks read on it: period, decks, stores all pass.
- **Ogden v0.1, by rule alone** (scored by the v0.1 scoring, 68% or more): 592 books, 589 of them v0.1's. The three differences: the rule picks two editions of a title that the stores kept under another id (*Little Black Mingo*, *Mrs. Tiggy-Winkle*) and *Popular Rhymes and Nursery Tales* (on the bar; v0.1 left it out), and misses *Treatise on Light* (below). Not forced; v0.1 stays as listed.
- **Found on the way, not fixed:** *Treatise on Light* (pg14725, Huygens) is in Ogden v0.1 and v0.2, and the manifest sets it as a `truth` book (the truths deck's held-out treatises). The S5 `decks` policy reads only the hole-card decks, so the gate does not see it. The composing carries it as listed and names it in the notes; whether it should read the truths deck's books is S5's call (Justin).
- **To sign off, in order:** (1) ~~the Library page: its branches and their variations as proposed, and the cards and filters inside a branch~~ (approved, 8 November); (2) the rules for the year, kind and voice above; (3) merging the migration and running it on `thorp`, then `thorp select facts`. After that, a new fitout may declare a selection; **no fitout that sails is changed without Justin's word**, and the first use is the planned Ogden v0.3 (more English, a wider period or other kinds), his to approve.

## The Ships tab becomes Harbours: Phaleron and the Market Harbour (8 November, Justin: "the ship page needs to be revised based on harbours. Internal waters is Phaleron after the first greek harbour and then the outer waters should be the first great trading harbour so then on the ships pages, it's actually called harbours and then when you open it, it's two tiles for the two harbours of today and then you click in the harbour to view its ships. So Phaleron has Pan, Augustus, the 9m and the 21m) and then the Market Harbour has the 50m up")

- **The tab is Harbours** (`#/harbours`; `#/ships` still opens it). It shows two tiles: **Phaleron**, the internal waters (this Mac mini, the Mac lane: Pan 300k, Augustus 1m, the speller 9m, the stylist 21m), and **the Market Harbour**, the outer waters (GPUs hired from Vast.ai, the Vast lane: the paragraph 50m, the explorer 100m, the trader 350m). A tile opens to its ships, the cards the Ships page had; "← harbours" goes back, and so do the ship and size pages' back links.
- **The queue page's "Foreign waters" is now "The Market Harbour"**, the same name for the same lane (`waters.py`, `WATERS['vast']`).
- **Which ship is in which harbour** is its size's, held in `waters.HARBOURS` for now (a size not named is in the outer harbour). It wants to be a column of `s1.sizes` ("these things shouldn't be hardcoded, we have postgres for a reason"); that is one more small migration, not made here. **The name of the Market Harbour is Justin's words ("the first great trading harbour"), not a place; a historical name can replace it in one line.**
- **Not changed:** a ship's own pages, the sizes' pages, the queue's lanes.

- **The Library page's fitout builder is retired** (Justin, 8 November: "remove fitout builder section on the library page"). Fitouts are now composed from declared selections (`thorp select compose`) and read under the variation they feed; the builder's save and queue endpoints (`/api/fitouts/save`, `/api/fitouts/queue`) and `thorp/fitouts.py` stay for now, so a saved fitout still builds and queues from the command line. Its page code in `dashboard.js` is unused and can go in a tidy-up.

- **The harbour's rule is its depth** (Justin, 8 November: "clicking on Phaleron or the other one would open a harbour page which then has all the assigned ships to that harbour and then the harbour rules is how deep it is, so that determines what ships can be in that harbour"). A harbour's **depth** is the most parameters (the whole ship, tables included) a ship may carry and lie in it; a ship lies in the **shallowest harbour deep enough** for it, so the sizes sort themselves and no preset is named. **Phaleron is 20M deep** (proposed, Justin's to set): the 300k, 1m, 9m and 21m (15.4M) fit, the 50m (46.7M) does not. **The Market Harbour has no bottom**: the 50m, 100m and 350m. The harbours' tiles show their depth; a harbour's page opens on its rules (the depth against every size's draught, on a log scale) and then its ships. `waters.HARBOURS` holds the depths for now, to be a harbours table.

- **The Market Harbour is Zayton** (Justin, 8 November: "Zayton"): Quanzhou, the harbour Marco Polo and Ibn Battuta both called the greatest in the world, where the Silk Road met the sea. The outer waters, the Vast lane and the deeper harbour (the 50m up) carry that name on the tiles, the harbour's address (`#/harbours/zayton`), its art and the queue page; "the Market Harbour" above is what it was called until then.

## The 9m's goal: Euclid (8 November, 17:55, Justin: "i think Euclid is the 9m for sure")

- **Decided:** the 9m's primary objective is **Euclid**, as the 1m's is De Morgan (1m, "The 1m's goal: solving De Morgan") and the 300k's the bigram table. The ladder this makes, each rung a period textbook the ship is taught from and judged on held-out problems: **300k Pan** the two-word table; **1m Augustus** De Morgan's arithmetic; **9m** Euclid's geometry (proof chains); the 21m chaining two facts; the 50m the first eureka (the eternal return; a Table Talk ship for now); the 350m quantum. The rungs beyond the 9m on the way to quantum (mechanics, fields and heat, the atom of 1910 to 1913) are a suggestion, not decided.
- **Why Euclid:** each proposition rests on earlier ones, so the rung is the chaining the 21m and the 50m are after, in a closed domain with a fixed curriculum. Size-matched rivals (Pythia-70m and -160m, Gemma 3 270M, SmolLM2-360M) can be measured on the same deck. The 9m already plays more of the 16 cards than they do (11 to 12 against 5 to 10 for Pythia 14m to 160m, 6 to 9 for Gemma and SmolLM2), but chaining on the 8- and 40-item logic decks is about zero on the 9m so far (the 40-item deck reads +0.069 for Gemma and +0.141 for Qwen3 4B): the rung has to be earned.
- **What a "win" means (to be pre-registered, not yet set):** a big model recalls Euclid, so the test must use **relabelled or invented terms** (points, lines and figures renamed) so knowledge cannot carry the answer; the held-out problems the ship's own reading never sees; the rival a learner's wrong step, as De Morgan's sums are scored; and a bar against size-matched models, not Qwen. Whether the frontier models are beaten on the relabelled version is a result to report, not a claim.
- **What exists:** Playfair's *Elements of Geometry* (the first six books of Euclid) is in Ogden v0.2; Proclus on the first book is on the reasoning shelf. Gutenberg's own Euclid (21076) has no plain-text file. **Not built:** a geometry tutor shelf in Euclid's order (as the 1m's maths tutor, proposed), the deck (propositions, their prerequisites, relabelled variants, wrong-step rivals), its evaluator and its marks.
- **Not done here:** the 9m's goal is not yet set in Postgres (`s1.sizes`, `ship_store.set_goals`), so the 9m's page still shows its old objective. Justin's to say when, with the deck's design and the tutor shelf, which need his approval as the 1m's did.

## The sources beyond Gutenberg: the gather pipeline (8 November, Justin: "can you use our VSM system and build a data fetching pipeline to get as many of the sources mentioned below into our library")

**Status: built and measured on a pilot; the two policy questions below are Justin's, not made here.** The sources named were the Internet Archive, HathiTrust, Google Books, the British Library, Harvard's Institutional Books, EEBO and Chronicling America. What can lawfully be fetched is fetched through the same gate as every other book; the rest are named as walls rather than worked around.

- **S4 is one catalogue, not a table in a docstring.** `thorp/sources/catalog.py` holds every source with what it holds, its size and licence, its status and, where there is one, its **wall**: *admitted* (open, and an adapter can fetch it now), *gated* (after an agreement or a token), *walled* (no lawful bulk full text) and *awaits S5* (the unsigned newspapers). `thorp gather --list` prints it and `thorp gather --plan N` sizes a pilot. Acquisition, the gather operation and the dashboard read the same list, so a status and an adapter cannot drift apart.
- **S1 is a gather, not a second fetcher.** `thorp/gather.py` (`thorp gather SOURCE`) reads a bulk source one record at a time and runs each through **acquisition's S3 gate**, not a copy of it: *english*, *dead-by-1955*, *printed-before-1930*, *not-a-deck-book* before the text is written; *cleans*, *not-duplicate*, *not-a-deck-text*, *not-same-edition*, *provenance* after. A refused record is not written. An admitted one is written to `data/texts/<source>/` and the manifest, and its verdict logged in `s1.intakes` and `s1.intake_verdicts`, exactly as an intake's is. A gather takes the same S2 intake lock, so it cannot write the manifest beside an intake; it never takes the GPU's helm.
- **The adapters** (`thorp/sources/bulk.py`): Common Pile's pre-1929 books (124,898 HathiTrust volumes, 27 gzip shards) and its Library of Congress books (129,052 volumes, 50 shards); the British Library's parquet (pages joined into volumes by `record_id`); AmericanStories' yearly tar.gz (pages joined into issues by LCCN and date); an Internet Archive collection by advanced-search query, through the existing per-item OCR fetch; Harvard's gated parquet; and a `folder` for licensed texts (EEBO-TCP, a HathiTrust or Google export) with a metadata CSV. The adapters only map a record's fields; they decide nothing.
- **The wall, said plainly.** **HathiTrust** has no open bulk full text (Extracted Features are page-level counts, not text); its scans reach Thorp through Common Pile's pre-1929 set. **Google Books** has no full-text API (metadata and snippets only); its public-domain scans are what Common Pile and Institutional Books redistribute. **EEBO/EEBO-TCP** is licensed by ProQuest and the Text Creation Partnership; there is no lawful bulk route without a subscription, and the `folder` adapter is the door if Justin has one. **Chronicling America's** direct OCR endpoints now redirect to loc.gov behind Cloudflare and return 403; AmericanStories is the same pages, already segmented. **Institutional Books** is gated: the adapter exists and refuses with its terms until `HF_TOKEN` is set after the agreement; the non-commercial, no-redistribution terms are Institutional's to grant, not Thorp's to assume. **Trove** waits on both the NLA's bulk terms and the unsigned question.
- **The decks are protected by text, and editions are checked.** The plan's warning was that a scan of a Forgotten Tomes book may arrive under another name. A new adopted rule, *not-a-deck-text* (serves S5 `decks`, after the fetch), compares 5-word shingles: a candidate sharing 35% or more of its shingles with a deck book is refused, with the deck book named. The check is capped at 2,000 evenly sampled shingles a book, so it stays cheap in bulk. A proposed rule, *not-same-edition*, records a title and first author already in the library; it refuses nothing until Justin adopts it.
- **The authority lookup** (`thorp/sources/authority.py`, `--authority`): the Library of Congress set's authors are often names without dates, so the death rule cannot speak and the book is refused. The resolver asks Wikidata for the person and reads the life dates from the search result's description, one request at a time, cached in `data/catalogue/authority.json`; a description with no death year, a name that is not a person, or a label that does not name the person returns nothing. The resolved dates sit on the candidate and the gate's own rule decides. It turned six of a 50-book Library of Congress sample from undated into dated; it is opt-in because it is slower than the fetch.
- **Where the scans live, and what is not changed.** They enter as `recipe` books (so a selection can use them) but the Library opens a new **scanned corpus** branch: every scanned book, before 1858 for the Origin shelf, the British Library, Common Pile, the newspapers. The scanned branch opens at 5,984 books, 340.3M words: 2,914 before 1858 (115.6M words), 1,985 British Library books, 3,958 Common Pile books and the 17 newspaper issues. Their `kind` is mostly "other" because the scanned catalogues carry no subjects; the period, source and language filters are sound. The rule that replaces Ogden for this branch is not an Ogden bar: it filters by source, the book's own imprint year (before 1858 for the Origin shelf), English, the gate's death rule and, for Institutional Books, its OCR scores; the vocabulary and style are the scans' own. **Ogden's lists, every fitout that sails and the Ogden-only policy are untouched.** Whether the Ogden branch is lifted for scans, and whether the *signed* rule is adopted for unsigned and corporate work, are the two S5 decisions this pipeline leaves to Justin; the second is why the AmericanStories adapter needs `--allow-unsigned` and why the great mass of undated corporate authors in both Common Pile sets is still refused.
- **The pass, measured (8 November).** Common Pile pre-1929: 5,000 records read, 3,184 new books admitted (3,322 in the library with the pilot's 138), 1,678 refused, 1,442.0 MB (about 400.6M tokens). Library of Congress: 2,825 records read before the pass was stopped (the opt-in authority lookup is slow), 603 admitted (636 here), 2,191 refused, 249.9 MB (69.4M tokens). British Library: 2,000 read, 1,985 admitted, 15 refused, 237.2 MB (65.9M tokens). AmericanStories 1770: 17 issues, 17 admitted, 0.3 MB. Internet Archive, a collection query with `year:[1850 TO 1929] AND language:eng`: 20 items, 8 new books (24 here), 15.3 MB. **All scanned sources: 5,984 books, 1,944.8 MB, about 540.2M tokens**; the whole library is 10,288 books, 579.5M English words, 340.3M of them dated by the book's own imprint. The refusals are the rule working: authors dead after 1955 (Untermeyer 1970, Frost 1963), books printed after 1929, fifteen French volumes in the British Library sample, and the corporate or undated authors ("Presbyterian Church", "Ohio", "American Art Association") the death rule cannot date. The audit after the pass found **no scanned book breaking the death rule**, 30 duplicate text groups (the older Gutenberg ones), no missing files and no drift in the sample; it counted 17 newspapers breaking the proposed *admitted-source* rule (their source awaits S5) and 300 unsigned recipes breaking the proposed *signed* rule, both recorded rather than hidden.
- **The tests** (`backend/tests/test_gather.py`): a stand-in bulk source through the gate (admitted, refused, known, dry run), the unsigned and walled refusals, the deck shingle rule, the Common Pile and Library of Congress field mapping, the British Library page join, the AmericanStories issue join, the licensed folder, the authority lookup and the catalogue's "every wall has a reason" check. The full suite passes (256 tests).

## Four positions at the 1m: Augustus, Plato, Francis and Diderot (8 November, Justin: "the 9m is then looking to have knowledge if pan is to be the best bettor, then augustus is to understand logic and mathematics and to help other models above it ... I think it's probably 4 bets. 1. Logic and maths 2. Metaphysics 3. Empirical knowledge 4. Generalised knowledge. So we have to have a position at the 1m base. So we need 4 ships, Augustus plus 3 more"; "do it and register the ships in the postgres db and then generate fal images and add them to the phaleron harbour")

- **Why:** in 1930, the most advanced account of how to acquire knowledge was logical empiricism (Schlick, Carnap, Neurath: knowledge from observation checked by experience, and from logic and mathematics, which are true by their form), with Fisher's experimental statistics, Bridgman's operationalism and the quantum physicists' stress on measurement beside it; the Circle called metaphysics meaningless. A period model should be asked which of these positions its reading builds, and the 1m is the first size where a reading can be varied cheaply.
- **The line of production:** **Pan** (300k, the best bettor, the bigram table) is the base. **Each of the four positions is grown from Pan's best version** to the 1m and reads a different thing, the same tokens and passes, on the same shared tokeniser and Ogden v0.2's base, so the reading is the only lever. **Each 1m then seeds a 9m** in the same position (the fork Justin described: the 9m class starts the specialisms). Today's Augustus was not grown from Pan; whether it is sailed again that way is a decision for the first leg, below.
- **The four ships** (registered in `s1.ships`, expedition `four_positions`; status in service, no voyages yet):

| Ship | Position | Reads (proposal) | Tested by (proposal) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Augustus** | logic and mathematics | the logic tutor's and the maths tutor's passages, De Morgan's order | the maths book (9 chapters, sums against the rival of the learner's mistake) and the 40-item logic deck: his goal as it stands |
| **Plato** | metaphysics | the philosophy and dialogue books at Ogden-ness 68% or more (72 and 15 books, 5.7M words: Plato, Berkeley, Hume, Spinoza, Descartes) and a metaphysics primer in Basic English: kinds and categories, same and different, cause and ground | the primer's held-out items, each against its rival |
| **Francis** (after Bacon) | empirical knowledge: observation checked by experience | the natural science, travel and how-to books at 68% or more (33, 22 and 15 books: thin) and an empirical primer in Basic English: what happens from what is seen, what a general claim forbids, what to look at to check it | the primer's held-out items; the world questions of the Ask page |
| **Diderot** | generalised knowledge: the control | Ogden v0.2's mix as it is, no specialism | language on the judge books, and all four tests |

- **Not approved, to be shown to Justin before anything is built** ("the type of content has to be approved by me"): the two primers (a metaphysics primer and an empirical primer, written by deterministic generators as the logic tutor's passages and the maths book are, every item and every run of six words held out of the reading), the share of each ship's reading that is its position's (the specialist books and primer are a thin slice of Ogden: philosophy 4.5%, science 1.8% of the words at 68% or more), and the choice between primers and a lower Ogden bar for these ships. **The Ogden corpus alone cannot carry metaphysics or empirical science**: Faraday, Lyell, Davy and Harvey are far below 68%, and the truths deck holds some of them out.
- **Measured as a matrix:** every ship on every ship's test (four by four), so the question "does a specialist help the models above it, or only itself" has an answer. **A position counts at a size only on two seeds.**
- **Predictions, fixed now:** (1) each specialist beats Diderot on its own test; (2) Diderot is within 0.02 bits per byte of the best specialist on the judge books (a thin slice costs little language); (3) Augustus's reading lifts Plato's items more than Francis's (they lean on order and on kinds, which logic practises), and Francis's lift is the smallest at this size; (4) the metaphysics test is the least reliable (the smallest effect on the widest spread of seeds), and may need a larger class to read.
- **What each leg settles, in order:** leg 1, the primers written and approved; leg 2, the four 1m ships grown from Pan, one seed each, on this Mac (Phaleron); leg 3, second seeds and the matrix; leg 4, the nine-meter forks, one per position that held at two seeds. **Nothing is queued.** The first voyage waits for the primers' approval.
- **Where the ships lie:** all four are 1.4M parameters, so in **Phaleron** (depth 20M); the Phaleron page shows them as "fitting out" until they have sailed. Art for Plato, Francis and Diderot made with fal in the dashboard's noir style.

- **The two primers are drafted, for Justin's approval** (8 November, Justin: "Draft them"): `ships/plans/four-positions-primers-draft.md`. A primer has the maths book's shape: chapters in a teaching order, each sentence stopping where one word decides it, its answer and its rival (the learner's mistake), in strict Basic English (the 29 sample items all pass `logic_tutor.not_basic`, Ogden-ness 100%), written by a deterministic generator, every test item and six-word run held out of the reading. Plato's seven chapters (kinds, things and qualities, same and like, seems and is, one and many, cause and reason, has to be and may be) follow Aristotle, Hume, Berkeley, Plato and Spinoza; Francis's eight (same cause and same effect, one change at a time, what is always there, more and more, one case against, how much to say, what to look at, seen and given) follow Hume, Mill and Bacon. **Found:** Aristotle's *Categories* is in the library's catalogue only in E. M. Edghill's translation, and Edghill died in 1964, so the period gate keeps it out. Five questions for Justin are at the end of the draft (the chapters, the common-sense choice, a 10% share with a matched 10% of random books for Diderot, an older translation, the Ogden bar). Nothing is written to a list or queued.
- **The chapters of both primers are approved** (Justin, 8 November: "Approve the chapters"): Plato's seven and Francis's eight, as drafted. **Still open, so nothing is built:** the common-sense choice (keep "seems and is" as tested, or drop it), the 10% share (with Diderot's matched 10% of random books), an older translation of Aristotle's *Categories* (the only one in Gutenberg fails the period gate), and the Ogden bar for the specialist books. The generators are written after those four are answered.

## The catch-up rule: the backlog sails while a decision waits, if it still sails and still decides (8 November, 18:55, Justin: "there will always be a backlog of things that can run while waiting for me to make a decision, so another rule should be if justin's not available then catch up on the backlog experiments if they're still valid")

- **The rule (S5).** When a lane would otherwise idle, and no line committed to the current window is waiting on it, the lane sails the highest-ranked **valid** backlog item that fits the gap. The window's committed lines come first; the backlog is the lowest of the four ranks (committed lines, then evidence for an open question, then valid backlog, then stop). Nothing in the backlog sails on its own: the rule is offered only to a lane that would idle, and the queue's order stays Justin's.
- **Still sails** is S3's answer, read and recorded, never re-decided: the fitout still sails (S5 `ogden`), the text's cleaning is current, the voyage belongs to an expedition. `vsm.gate_refusals` is the gate's read-only twin, so an audit writes no gate rows for voyages that never sail (and an advisory does not count as a refusal — the first audit struck two valid items by mistaking one for the other).
- **Still decides** is the other half, and it must be named: an item that names no decision is struck, because nothing can check what nobody wrote down. An item whose **control has not docked** is *blocked*, not struck: it still sails and still decides, it cannot be read yet. Only a person closes a decision (or the item sailing); `strike` keeps the reason, so the list stays finite instead of becoming a museum.
- **Order, and the gap.** Valid items by expected gain an hour (highest first), then shortest first, and only what is **known** to fit the gap: an unknown length is not offered to one. Gain an hour is empty on every item today (the wager is the instrument that fills it), so the order falls back to shortest first, which is the right default while a decision waits.
- **Built:** `s4.backlog` (migration `b7c2f41a9d03`), `thorp/viable/s4_intelligence/backlog.py` (`seed`, `audit`, `list`, `rank`, `add`, `strike`; `thorp backlog ...`), `vsm.gate_refusals` (the gate's read-only twin), and `lanes.append` (a line at the end of the queue, under the queue's own lock, with the reason beside it). Tried by `tests/test_backlog.py` (the two tests and the order, without a store).
- **The first audit**, on the five voyages of `queue-removed-2026-11-08.txt` (its `cmd:` lines are not items): **2 valid**, Augustus's second seed (`fu-ogden-v0-1@1m-pre-s2`, 37 min) and the plain 9m on Ogden v0.2 (`fu-ogden-v0-2@9m-pre-s1`, 211 min, the Smallest Bridge leg 1's control); **3 blocked** on that control (`staged-all` and its two copy arms). So the two lines that were still valid are exactly the two the Phaleron clearing dropped while the plan was still using them — the second seed was the control for Augustus's four maths arms.
- **Tonight:** the second seed is caught up into Phaleron's queue behind the four Pan arms, where the window's own plan needs it. The 9m control stays on the backlog: it is another expedition's work, 3.5 hours of it, and the window's committed lines are Pan and Augustus.
- **Not built, on purpose.** (1) The idle hook in `lanes.py` (a lane that would idle asks the backlog, and records what it took and why): it wants the queue's own decision record, and the window's precedence is the part that can go wrong. (2) **The questions table with defaults and deadlines**, so "Justin's not available" is a fact rather than a judgement. That one waits on Justin: which questions may be answered by a default (reversible and cheap — the four maths shares, say) and which are identity-level and must wait. Until he says, unavailability is a `.hold` file or a `wait:` line, as before.
- **Found on the way:** `uv run thorp ...` from the repository root is broken — the venv's editable install still maps `thorp` to the pre-split top-level directory, so `thorp schedule tick` (as this file and launchd write it) dies on `No module named 'thorp.cli'`. `uv run --project backend thorp ...` works, and so does the queue, which runs a module (`python -m ...`).
- **The recommendations taken, and the generators written** (Justin, 8 November: "i'll take your recs"): the common-sense answers stay and **"seems and is" is scored by itself** beside the rest, so the one contestable chapter cannot decide a verdict; the Ogden bar for the specialist books stays 68%; an older translation of Aristotle's *Categories* is still to be looked for (the primers follow the idea only). **`thorp/primers.py`** writes both primers (`backend/scripts/make_primers.py`, `tests/test_primers.py`): Plato's 124 cases in seven chapters and Francis's 100 in eight, every sentence strict Basic English (a case that fails the check is dropped, never loosened); every third case of a chapter is the test's: **35 held-out items for Plato and 33 for Francis** (`lists/tricks/plato-primer.csv`, `francis-primer.csv`), and a chapter's reading never uses that chapter's held-out subject words nor a whole test sentence. The reading is passages of an opening with two names and three lesson sentences, in 40 local books a primer (`primer-plato-<n>`, `primer-francis-<n>`; shelves `lists/primer-plato.csv`, `primer-francis.csv`).
- **A finding that changes one recommendation: the share is 2%, not 10%.** Each primer has only about 100 distinct lesson sentences. 34,000 passages (1.9M words for Plato, 2.5M for Francis, about 2% of Ogden v0.2's reading) already say each sentence hundreds of times; at 10% (9M words, read four times) a ship would read each one tens of thousands of times, which would swamp its language and teach it the test's frames by heart. So **2% is written, and the share is a lever, not a fixed 10%**: the first leg reads 2%, and a second tries 5% only if 2% moves the position's test. Diderot's matched slice is 2% of random other books. Held-out subjects make the test a check on carrying a frame to new nouns, not on the sentences read. **Nothing is queued; no fitout is composed.**
- **The share is approved at 2%** (Justin, 8 November: "Yes, 2%"): the first leg of each position reads 2% of its reading as its own (the primers as written, 1.9M words for Plato and 2.5M for Francis; the logic and maths tutors for Augustus; Diderot a matched 2% of random other books). 5% is tried only if 2% moves that position's test. The primers and their shares are now approved content; what remains before a first voyage is composing each ship's fitout (Ogden v0.2's base with its 2% slice, through the selection, the gate's prechecks) and the pre-registration of leg 2, which waits for Justin.

## The card census's shelf matrix sails: the ogden and prechecks rules excepted for 69 runs (8 November, 18:52, Justin: "relax the ogden rule for those experiments and queue them")

- **The decision, and how it is made.** The 69 card-census arms that were refused only by the ogden switch and by their fitout's prechecks now sail on their own fitouts (`keel-cards-cause`, `pilot-cards-cause`). The switch itself is unchanged: this is written as two **exceptions** in S5 (`s5.policy_exceptions`, for `ogden` and for `prechecks`), named run by run, with the reason and Justin's words kept beside them. Every voyage that is not named still has to pass both. `vsm.except_policy` and `thorp s5 except POLICY VALUE --reason ... --words ...` (a name, a glob, or `@file.json`) make an exception a recorded row, never a code change.
- **Why not convert them to the Ogden fitout.** I measured before doing either: none of the 102 refused specs reads an Ogden list. Their recipes are `reasoning-shelf.csv`, `cause-shelf.csv` and the pre-shelf control — the corpus **is** the census's variable. Pointing them at `ogden-v0-1` would have made them pass and test nothing, and their controls would still read the shelf. Justin's word is the trade: the census's question survives, the Ogden-only rule bends for these 69 runs and no others.
- **What the exception does not cover, and cannot.** 33 of the 102 stay refused: they name a fitout that no longer exists (`fitout`), or read v1 text and are not in the 7 November `clean` exception (158 runs, "let the queue finish"). An exception cannot give a voyage a fitout: `_prechecked` needs one to check, so those want repair, not relaxation.
- **The prechecks are excepted because there are none.** `fitout_store.readiness('keel-cards-cause')` is None — the fitout has no rows in `s3.fitouts` and no prechecks at all (it is hand-curated in `fleet.FITOUTS`). So the exception sails an unchecked fitout at Justin's word; the risk is stated rather than hidden, and the first arm's failure (if any) is seconds long, not hours.
- **Order.** A control before its arms: the two `fo-pilot-cards-cause-pre-s1/s2` controls are moved to the head of the block, and each lever's `pre`, `post1` (reasoning shelf) and `post2` (cause shelf) follow in that order. S2's lint (`vsm.lint_queue`) reads zero findings on the queue after the move; before it, it caught the two post arms seeded from controls that sat below them.
- **The cost, stated.** 69 runs, about **14.5 h** of this Mac at the class mean. The census's own stop rule is 38 runs, so this overruns it by roughly 30: Justin's word is the amendment. If the stop is to stand, the tail of the block should be struck rather than run.
- **The queue as it now stands** (78 lines, one coordinator): Pan's `teach5`, Augustus's second seed, the four maths shares (`maths5`, `maths10`, `maths2`, `maths20` — queued by another hand while this was being written), the 69 census runs, and the three primer voyages (`plato2`, `francis2`, `diderot2`). About twenty hours, which carries the Mac to roughly midday tomorrow.
- **Found:** the queue is being edited by more than one hand — the maths arms and the primers arrived mid-edit. `uv run --project backend` failed twice with a network 504 for the spaCy wheel while re-syncing, so these commands were run as `PYTHONPATH=backend .venv/bin/python -m thorp.cli ...`; the running coordinator is unaffected.

### The four positions at the 1m, leg 2 queued (8 November, Justin: "queue em, the vsm should pick them up")

- **Queued, one seed each, at the end of the standing queue (Phaleron, the Mac lane):** `fu-ogden-v0-1-plato2@1m-pre-s1` (metaphysics), `fu-ogden-v0-1-francis2@1m-pre-s1` (empirical knowledge), `fu-ogden-v0-1-diderot2@1m-pre-s1` (generalised knowledge, the control). **Augustus's arm is the one already queued by Phaleron's plan, `fu-ogden-v0-1-maths2@1m-pre-s1`** (Ogden v0.1 + De Morgan's tutor at 2%), so the four positions are Augustus (maths2), Plato, Francis and Diderot, and the plain Augustus (`fu-ogden-v0-1@1m-pre-s1`, seeds 1 and 2) is the control for all four. About 40 minutes each.
- **Two changes from the pre-registration above, made to match the maths arm already queued:** the base is **Ogden v0.1** (592 books, 37M words), not v0.2, and each ship is **trained from scratch at the 1m, not grown from Pan's best version**: Pan's round 1 is still sailing, so there is no best Pan yet. Growing the four from Pan's best is a later leg, once it is known. Everything else is as pre-registered: the 2% slice (0.82M, 0.80M and 0.77M words: Plato's first 22 primer books, Francis's first 16, Diderot's 57 books drawn equal by kind from other library books, none of v0.1's, as of 7 November, up to 60,000 words each), four passes, the shared 2,048-piece tokeniser, the 1m's learning rate, the same eval cadence.
- **Composed from declared selections** (`ships/fitouts/ogden-v0-1-<slug>.json`, `thorp select compose`): v0.1's list carried alone and the slice appended up to its words; Diderot's slice is its own selection (`plans/diderot-slice.json`, weights by kind). Each composing is a row in `s3.compositions`, the gate's library prechecks (period, decks, stores) pass on each list, and **each fitout's 20 required prechecks pass** (the tagger's lexicon for each is v0.1's counts with the new books tagged on top, exactly the lexicon a full build would write; `tagger.extend_lexicon`, tested).
- **Every ship reads three sets of items:** the maths book (72 sums), Plato's primer (35 held-out items) and Francis's (33), by the new `plato` and `francis` evaluators (`thorp/evaluators/primers.py`), at every eval, and Plato's "seems and is" chapter is also reported by itself (`plato/right_not_seems` is the share without it). The plain Augustus and Augustus's maths arms were queued before these evaluators and do not read them: **they are scored on Plato's and Francis's items afterwards**, from their last checkpoints (`thorp eval --evaluators plato francis`), to complete the four-by-four.
- **The gate passed all three** (S5 `ogden`, `prechecks`, `stores` with each fitout's opt-in, `period`, `decks`, `pre-registered` through the label "Fitout: Ogden v0.1 + ..." under the Ogden expedition) and S2 put them in Phaleron's plan. The ships are re-keyed in `s1.ships` to their voyages' ship keys, so each voyage docks to Augustus, Plato, Francis or Diderot, not to a new ship.
- **Not queued:** the second seeds (a position counts at a size only on two seeds): written after the first are read, so Phaleron's window is not filled twice over; and the nine-meter forks. **Predictions stand as written above.**
- **One casualty on the way, and its repair.** `fu-ogden-v0-2~pan-v02@300k-pre-s1` died 30 seconds after it started, before training: `uv run` re-synced because `backend/pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` had been changed by another hand, and the spaCy model's wheel answered 504 from GitHub. Nothing was wrong with the voyage. After one `uv sync --project backend`, a launch is a no-op again (0.3 s, no network), and the arm is re-queued at the head of the queue with a comment saying why. The prune took the `torch` group out of the environment too; no voyage in the queue needs it, and the `--group compare` readings will re-sync when they are next run (when GitHub answers).
- **The second seeds are queued** (Justin, 8 November: "Queue seeds now"): `fu-ogden-v0-1-maths2`, `plato2`, `francis2` and `diderot2` at seed 2 (`@1m-pre-s2`), the seed-1 specs copied with the seed and name changed, each through the gate, at the end of the queue (`scripts/make_position_fitouts.py --seed 2`). Augustus's arm gets its second seed too, so every position counts on two seeds; the plain Augustus already has both. About 40 minutes each, 3 hours in all. **A position counts at the 1m only if its own items rise above the plain Augustus's and Diderot's on both seeds.**

## The first 50m's reading stage, docked (8 November, 19:10; the session on foreign waters)

- **What sailed:** `fu-ogden-v0-2~table-talk-reading@50m-pre-s1` (run `c353647d`), the 50m (38.8M parameters) on an RTX 4090 (instance 59672864, $0.369/h), 20,840 steps, 682,885,120 tokens in **140.3 minutes**, exit 0, **$1.1 against a quote of $5.40** (the week: $1.55 of $30, credit $22.87). Launched 16:26, finished on the box 18:47, 138k to 145k tokens a second with its readings. Three hires before it: two lost (one went offline in 48 seconds, $0.004; one crashed at step 0 on Postgres, $0.113: "first attempts" above).
- **Home:** all 21 checkpoints (9.7 GB), the run `finished` in the ledger (642,099 metric rows, 336 evals, 580 artifacts). The charter's last pull failed three times (`database disk image is malformed`) and the charter exited with that error, but the box was released (audit: nothing listed) and the voyage is whole. Two causes: the box's ledger was copied while training wrote to it (every mid-run pull failed the same way; I imported a consistent snapshot by hand at 17:00 and 17:30), and stale local `-wal`/`-shm` files from those pulls no longer matched the final main file (deleted by hand; the final import was run by hand from the pulled copy).
- **Against Drake's five predictions** (cards read as `wager.falls` on the final full reading, step 20,840):
  1. **Judge-book bpb under 1.40: held.** 1.310 (the 9m twin 1.501; the 21m on v0.1 1.397). It fell steadily (1.67 at step 2,048, 1.46 at 6,144, 1.34 at 14,336, 1.319 at 18,432).
  2. **At least the 13 cards leg 1 played: missed by one.** 12 at the end (comparator, delimiter, duplicate word, induction, nevermore, number-spotter, order-word, premise-marker, previous-word, rare word, sink, words-back); 13 at some reading (the Scales flagged first at step 1,792 and fell away). **Not played: the Bridge, the Counter, the Mover, the Scales.** The Bridge, the card the full deck's riddle is for, was not played at the end on a 12-layer ship.
  3. **Copy gain above leg 1's 0.97 and under 2.5: held.** Natural copy gain 2.08 (the 9m twin 0.47), with induction and nevermore played from step 512.
  4. **The Mover: not played** (the coin toss went tails).
  5. **Chaining on the 40-item deck under +0.10: held, but not as expected.** It was not flat: about zero to step 7,168, then up to **+0.093 at step 16,384**, and **+0.072 at the end** (readings after the release at step 18,756: 0.073, 0.087, 0.072). By relation at the end: counterfactual **+0.20** (+0.27 at step 20,480), membership **+0.11**, order +0.06, cause −0.02, proportion 0.00. The 8-item deck reads +0.05 to +0.09 since step 7,168. Against rivals: Gemma 3 270M +0.069, SmolLM2-360M +0.034, Qwen3 4B +0.141 (all single readings; the 50m's last 8 full readings run between +0.07 and +0.09).
- **Also recorded, not predicted:** the crowns gain +0.041 at the end (`moves` −0.132, `ask` +0.05: still nothing that follows the asker's standpoint both ways); loss 2.47 at step 20,750 (up from 2.43 around the release, as the pull let go).
- **Reading:** a better reader than every Thorp so far, chaining a little under the mark on the invented-word deck (counterfactual and membership carry it; cause does not), and the Bridge not played. The pull's release did not collapse the chaining, which stayed at +0.07 to +0.09. This is the reading-only twin for the dialogue stage (the dialogue stage may cost the Forgotten Tomes no more than 0.05 bpb against bpb 1.310).
- **Charter faults seen, not fixed:** (1) a mid-run pull of a live ledger fails (needs a consistent snapshot on the box: `sqlite3` backup, then pull that one file); (2) stale local `-wal`/`-shm` files break the final pull; (3) the foreign-waters box has no Postgres at all (fixed by the cutover's box-papers commit, which worked: every reading was recorded on the box). Parity at 9m is still open (P1 mark 3 waits on the second MLX seed).


## Before 1500: incunabula as a discovery set (8 November, Justin: "also i want to go back before 1500s can you fetch those books using your pipeline")

- **The open route is Boston Public Library's incunabula** (`bplscinc` on the Internet Archive): 315 books printed 1450–1500, 307 with the public-domain mark. `thorp gather incunabula --set incunabula --allow-unsigned` reads them through the same S3 gate, with a new **incunabula** set that is a discovery shelf, not an English recipe.
- **The filter is the archive's noise, answered.** The Internet Archive's `date:[1450 TO 1500]` returns modern books with wrong dates, manuscripts, single leaves and ProQuest scans. `thorp/sources/incunabula.py` requires a strict four-digit year, a public-domain licence, no manuscript/fragment, no `early-european-books`/`pub_early-english-books-1475-1640` collection, and a text of at least 5,000 characters. The item fetcher is `incunabula:<archive id>`, so a list can name one.
- **What the set is.** 315 in the BPL set, 180 pass the strict filter; the pass fetched **177 books, 141.4 MB, about 39.3M tokens, printed 1466–1500**: 150 Latin, 11 German, 9 Italian, 3 Greek, 2 Hebrew, 1 French and **one English** incunabulum, *Mons perfectionis* (1497). The `scanned-incunabula` variation holds 177 books, 23.6M words. The English incunabula are the wall: the rest are mostly in EEBO's ProQuest set, which Thorp does not have. The BPL set is the lawful route to the first printed books; EEBO is the route to the first printed English. Gallica and the Bavarian State Library hold more continental incunabula and are the next open routes.
- **The gate.** `READ_SETS` now names `recipe` and `incunabula`; the deck rules (title and shingle overlap) apply to both, while the English rule still applies only to `recipe`. The incunabula are not `recipe`, so no fitout composes them by default. The Library opens a `scanned-incunabula` variation from `lists/incunabula.csv` (written by `thorp gather ... --export-list`), and a selection can carry that list if a fitout is ever meant to read them.

## The Library filters branches by the ship sizes that work them (8 November, 19:15, Justin: "can you add a filter http://localhost:8787/#/library so that we can filter branches by which ships size they can work on")

- **What a branch's ships are, and how they are known.** **Evidence, not intent**: the sizes that have read any of a branch's variations, read from the ledger (each run's `data.recipe`, or its first stage's) and grouped by `ship_store.size_of`, so a variant counts as its rung (`300k-l1w96` → `300k`, the same rule the size pages use). A variation that has never sailed has no sizes; a branch of selections (The whole library, Decks and yardsticks) has none and says **"no size has read it yet"** rather than guessing. At 19:15: Ogden 300k, 1m, 9m, 21m, 50m (the 50m reads v0.2); Data levers and pools 300k, 1m, 9m; the rest none yet.
- **Where it lives.** `_browse_build` gives every variation and branch a `sizes` list, and the payload the seven rungs (`ship_store.sizes()`), so the page never hardcodes a ship. `_sizes_by_list` is the one query (the ledger's voyages); it and `_sizes_all` fail soft — no store, no filter, never no page.
- **The page.** The branches grid opens with a row of chips, **Ships that work it**; a pick dims the branches that size cannot work on and a `clear ×` puts them back. Each branch card names its ships (`ships: Pan, Augustus, The speller, The stylist`) and a branch no size has read says so. Inside a branch, the variation cards dim under the same filter and each names its sizes. A dimmed branch is **not hidden**: a filter that hides work which has not started yet would hide the work ahead.
- **Built:** `pnpm --dir frontend build` into `backend/thorp/dash/static`, and the dashboard restarted (it serves the built page and answers the API from memory), so the page at :8787 shows it on a reload.
- **Known gap:** the sizes come from voyages, so a fitout written for a size but never sailed does not show, and "can work on" reads as "has worked on". If Justin wants intent instead — a variation declaring the sizes it is for — that is a column on `s3.library_variations` and a field in `library_branches.DEFAULTS`, not a change to this query.

## The ancient world: Latin, Greek and cuneiform discovery corpora (8 November, Justin: "can we try build out latin then also ancient greek and cunieform would be good to have even if we don't use them anytime soon")

- **Three sets, not one, and none of them a recipe.** `classics` holds the Perseus editions (Latin and Greek); `cuneiform` holds ETCSL. They are **discovery** sets: the period rules (English, dead by 1955, printed before 1930) are not applied, because a Perseus edition's imprint is modern, its ancient author has no modern death year, and ETCSL has no printing date. The deck, duplicate, cleaning and provenance rules still apply. No fitout composes them by default; the Library opens an **ancient world** branch with a variation for each.
- **Perseus** (`thorp/sources/classics.py`): the canonical Latin and Greek TEI repositories, CC BY-SA 4.0. The first gather downloads the GitHub archive (48 MB Latin, 75 MB Greek) into `data/classics/`; after that it reads the local XML. Each edition gives its title, ancient author, editor, imprint and text. **344 Latin editions (52.5 MB) and 799 Greek editions (134.5 MB)** were admitted; the filter keeps only the language's editions (`*lat*` / `*grc*`), skips `__cts__` metadata and translations, and requires at least 500 characters of text.
- **ETCSL** (`thorp/sources/cuneiform.py`): the 243 compositions of the Electronic Text Corpus of Sumerian Literature. The catalogue is one page; each composition is two requests (the composite transliteration and the translation). The text is stored as transliteration + translation, `language="sux"`, anonymous, no printing date. The record's `origin` is the ETCSL page.
- **The gate's new distinction.** `DISCOVERY_SETS = ("classics", "cuneiform")`; `READ_SETS` now includes them, so the deck rules by title and by shingle apply. The period rules return nothing for a discovery set; the English rule still applies only to `recipe`. The audit therefore reports no period breaks for these sets, and the books remain outside every selection that draws on `recipe`.
- **The branch and the lists.** `lists/perseus-latin.csv`, `lists/perseus-greek.csv` and `lists/etcsl.csv` are written by `thorp gather ... --export-list`; the `ancient` branch's three variations read them. `library._ids` now resolves a list row naming a bulk source (`perseus-latin:<edition>`) to its manifest id, so the same list can be carried into a fitout if one is ever meant to read the classics.
- **What is not here.** First1KGreek (1,231 more Greek editions, a superset of much of Perseus), the TLG (licensed), the PHI Latin texts (not open), CDLI's 6 GB ATF dump, and ORACC's Akkadian corpora. The open next steps are First1K and CDLI; TLG and PHI are walls.

## Pan, three ways to push the 50m's cards in, compared (8 November, 19:50, Justin: "i want to do all three ways to push and then compare the results")

- **What is being pushed.** The 50m (`fu-ogden-v0-2~table-talk-reading@50m-pre-s1`, trained on an RTX 4090 through Vast this morning, 20,840 steps) holds the deck Pan lacks: duplicate-word 0.88 against 0.00, the sink 0.69 against 0.05, offset-2 1.00 against 0.14, previous-word 1.00 against 0.34, induction 0.84, the comparator 1.00. Its checkpoint is on this Mac and loads here: torch and MLX write the same `model.safetensors` under the same names.
- **The three ways, one variable at a time.** The same four metre cards — the most Pan has room for (2 layers x 2 heads): previous-word (L0H0), duplicate-word (L0H1), induction (L1H0), sink (L1H1) — and where there is a scaffold, the same schedule (full weight to 20% of the voyage, faded to zero by 60%, the 9m's rule from the night).
  1. **A, the rule** (`~pan-scaffold4`, two seeds): the scaffold's target is each card's pattern read off the page. No teacher.
  2. **B, the outputs** (`~pan-teach50` alpha 0.5, `~pan-teach50a9` alpha 0.9, the teach5 recipe at temperature 2): the 50m's softened next-word distribution, no scaffold.
  3. **C, the attention** (`~pan-from50`, one seed): the target is the 50m's own attention at its best head for each card (previous-word L1H0, duplicate-word L1H1, induction L3H6, sink L0H0). The heads cannot be copied across classes (12x8x64 against 2x2x32), so what crosses is the distribution the donating head puts on the same tokens — what the card does, not the weights that do it.
- **Built for C:** `train.scaffold_targets` (the teacher's `forward_attending` as the scaffold's `targets`), the scaffold heads take `teacher_layer`/`teacher_head`, and `optim.teacher.alpha: 0` loads a teacher for its attention without distilling its logits (the KL branch is skipped, so the teacher is forwarded once, not twice). Tried by `tests/test_scaffold_teacher.py`.
- **Found on the way:** `offset_2` is not a metre card — it is a star without a scannable rule — so the fourth slot is the **sink** (`METRE_PATTERNS` has sink, not offset_2). The scaffold refuses any other card by name.
- **Pre-registered reads, at the final checkpoint** (the plain Pan 0.208/0.200; the bigram table 0.303): A holds previous-word (>=0.8) and gives duplicate-word 0.3 to 0.7 while pinned, by heart 0.2 to 0.5; B (alpha 0.5) moves duplicate-word to 0.1 to 0.4 but nothing like A's pin and leaves `keyed_top1` within 0.01; C beats A on the Stage-1 cards (induction's copy gain and duplicate-word after the fade), because a real distribution is a sharper target than a rule; **none of the three moves `keyed_top1` past 0.22**, and the honest comparison is the cards, not the table.
- **What it costs.** A is 20 minutes an arm; B and C run the 50m forward every step, about 2.6 hours an arm (the 21m teacher took 65 minutes). Five arms, about nine hours: A, A, C, B 0.5, B 0.9, queued ahead of the card census at 22:00 and finishing about 06:30. **The census's 69 arms wait behind them** — that is the cost of the comparison, and they are still queued, not dropped.

## Babel: does cuneiform in the reading bend Pan's English? (8 November, Justin: "another expedition which is discovering how pan changes based upon whether we use english with cunifiorm and without it. does any evidence of bias start showing")

**Status: pre-registered; seed 1 of three arms queued (control, 1.92%, 4.99% cuneiform by bytes).** A signal is read before seed 2 is queued.

- **The question.** Pan (the 300k class) reads Ogden v0.1's English. Does adding a measured share of cuneiform transliterations change what it does with English: the held-out English score, the probability it gives cuneiform words on English contexts, and the words it samples?
- **The arms.** The same 592 English books (`lists/babel-english.csv`, a copy of Ogden v0.1) plus cuneiform until the share of the bytes is **1.92%** (`lists/babel-cuneiform-2.csv`: ETCSL + one CDLI volume) or **4.99%** (`lists/babel-cuneiform-5.csv`: ETCSL + five CDLI volumes). The control is 0%. The cuneiform is Sumerian transliteration from ETCSL (239 compositions) and CDLI (five 2M-character volumes), `set cuneiform`, not an English recipe.
- **What is held still.** The 2,048-piece tokeniser trained on `lists/loop-pool.csv` in every arm, so the difference is the reading and not the vocabulary; the 300k architecture, context 1,024, learning rate, batch, four passes and seed. The control is a fresh run on the same tokeniser, not the older Pan runs (`fu-ogden-v0-1@300k-pre-s1` trained its tokeniser on Ogden v0.1).
- **The measures.** `eval/hole-cards-unseen/bpb_mean_en` (does English get worse?), `script/cuneiform_mass_en` (the probability mass the model gives a fixed list of Sumerian words on English held-out contexts; the same token ids in both arms), `script/cuneiform_max_en`, and the `samples` artifacts (do Sumerian forms appear in English continuations?).
- **The predictions, fixed now.** (1) English bpb rises with the cuneiform share, but at 2% by less than the seed spread; at 5% by a small but visible amount. (2) `script/cuneiform_mass_en` is near zero in the control and rises monotonically with the share; at 5% it is at least twice the control. (3) Samples at 5% occasionally carry a Sumerian word; at 2% they do not. (4) The cuneiform itself is learnt poorly at 300k: its own mass rises, but the model does not become a Sumerian model. **A finding contrary to (1) or (2) is the interesting one.**
- **The stop rule.** Seed 1 of the three arms; seed 2 of any arm that moves `script/cuneiform_mass_en` by more than the control's seed spread or English bpb by more than 0.01. No 10% arm until 5% is read: the corpus has 12.2 MB of cuneiform, 5.6% of the English, so a 10% arm would repeat it.
- **The S5 exceptions.** The Babel runs are excepted from `ogden` (they are not Ogden), `prechecks` (a new fitout, no prechecks), `period` (the cuneiform is not English) and `expedition` (the Babel write-up is this entry), by pattern `fu-babel-*`, recorded in `s5.policy_exceptions` with Justin's request as the reason. The control is English and passes `period` without the exception; the treatment does not.
- **Why this is worth doing even if the answer is no.** A null result is the first evidence that a small English model ignores a 5% foreign-script minority; a positive one says the corpus ceiling work has a bias cost that must be measured before the ladder reads scans, classics or cuneiform.

## The SOP, v0.0.1: one standard for what a ship reads, how it is trained, and how it is crowned (8 November, 20:15, Justin: "we need to have some form of crown doctrine or sop across all ships. That's what i meant by 0.3. But let's do the first SOP v0.0.1" ... "and that dictates content, parameters, phases of training everything")

- **What it is.** `ships/plans/sop-v0.0.1.md`: one standard for every size, in one place, so a voyage can be checked against it — **content** (Ogden v0.1, the shared 2048 spm tokeniser trained on v0.1, `unwrap-v2` cleaning, the three judge decks refused), **parameters** (context 1024, rope, shuffled, 4 passes, batch 32,768, AdamW 0.9/0.95, wd 0.1, cosine to 10%, clip 1.0, bf16), and **phases** (P0 the plain keel; P1 metre, P2 fade, P3 content; riddles staged after what they rest on; `release` as the only by-heart reading).
- **v0.0.1 is a recording, not a reform.** It writes down what the fleet already does; the six places where the practice is silent are marked **OPEN** for Justin, not decided.
- **What varies by size, and only this:** the shape and the learning rate with its warmup — 300k 2×64×2 at 0.003/100, 1m 4×128×4 at 0.002/100, 9m 6×192×6 at 0.001/200, 21m 8×320×8 at 0.0008/300, 50m 12×512×8 at 0.0006/400. A ship may not choose them; they are what the class is. Everything else in §2 is invariant, which is what makes a size's reading comparable with the size beneath.
- **Found while writing it:** the invariant is real and steady across the five baselines — one corpus, one tokeniser, one context, four passes, one batch — and only the 50m's baseline carries a scaffold (hold 0.10, fade 0.60). The reading rule that the record keeps proving is written in: **read the end of the voyage, not the peak** (the bridge 2.27 at step 1,280 was 0.11 at the end).
- **The crown doctrine, as coded:** `leaderboard.crown` decides on chaining lift, noise 0.03; two sailings; within the noise, the tie-break is a logic reading, then sailings, then lift; a version rises only when a different ship beats the incumbent by more than the noise, and the register is `s1.crownings`.
- **The six OPEN, for Justin:** (1) the crown decides on chaining lift for every size, but each size has its own goal — crown on the goal, with chaining as the cross-size check? *(the largest)* (2) should a crown require no held card to regress? (3) what is a "sailing"? (4) which changes need Justin's explicit word rather than the automatic crown? (5) may a new corpus version change the tokeniser, and how is it then compared? (6) should a challenger that misses three times be retired automatically?
- **Not built:** a `sop:` field in the spec, and a gate check or `thorp sop` command that holds a voyage to the version it names. The document says so; nothing enforces it yet.
- **The corpus candidate that "v0.3" could have meant** was composed and measured, and then held, not queued: the Ogden bar at 68% over today's library is **1,741 books, 110.7M words, +462 added and −101 dropped against v0.2 (+361 net)**. If a corpus v0.3 is wanted separately, that list is ready to write; the queue is unchanged by it.

## The two bets: the next word against the next word's type (8 November, Justin: "Pan must be the best at betting. so its current bet is the next word in a two word sequence. But the other bet should be about betting the type of the word too. and then we'd compare those scores")

**Status: the metric is built and the Pan checkpoints are to be re-read; the existing scores are already a first answer.** No new training is queued for this reading: the same Pan checkpoint is asked two questions, so the comparison is of betting rules, not of models.

- **The word bet** is Pan's current one: from the two words of context, the top guess of the next **keyed word** (Ogden's 850, `<name>`, `<number>`, `<thing>`), scored `shapes/keyed_top1` on the Forgotten Tomes.
- **The type bet** is the same distribution read by **type**: the probability of each fine UD part of speech is the sum of the probabilities of the words with that tag (from the tagged top-10), and the top type is the bet; scored `shapes/type_top1` on all points and `shapes/type_top1_keyed` on the same keyable points as the word bet, so the two are head to head.
- **The first reading, from the two finished Pan seeds (step 8216):** word bet **0.2083 / 0.1995**; fine type **0.3716 / 0.3516**; coarse Ogden type **0.5991 / 0.5859**. The type bet is already the better bet by a wide margin: the model knows what kind of word comes next far better than which word it is. That is the gap the new metric puts on one line.
- **What the expedition adds.** The `shapes` evaluator now writes `shapes/type_top1` and `shapes/type_top1_keyed`; the two Pan seeds are re-read with `thorp eval <run> --evaluators shapes` (the queue lines are in `ships/specs/queue.txt`, and the same command can be run by hand). The comparison is the two metrics on the same checkpoint and the same held-out points. A later arm, if the gap is interesting, is a type-only Pan: the same books retagged by UPOS and the same 300k, to see whether a model trained for the type bet beats the word model's type bet.
- **The prediction.** The type bet beats the word bet on every seed by at least 0.10 absolute; fine type is about 0.35–0.45 and coarse type about 0.58–0.62. If the type bet is *not* better, the tagger or the keying is wrong, not the model.

## The first launch under SOP v0.0.1: one corpus, one schedule, five cards, at 300k, 1m and 9m (8 November, 20:20, Justin: "take control of the queue and schedule the 300k, 1m and 9m runs on the SOP 0.0.1 so they all need to use the same content from the library, the same pretraining phases and then all the improvements for the 5 cards you suggested and any other improvements we can wrap into the SOP")

- **The three voyages, at the head of the queue:** `fu-ogden-v0-1~sop001@300k-pre-s1`, `@1m-pre-s1`, `@9m-pre-s1`. They differ in shape and learning rate alone — 0.003/100 warmup, 0.002/100, 0.001/200 — which is SOP §3.
- **The same content:** Ogden v0.1, the shared 2048 spm tokeniser trained on v0.1, `unwrap-v2`, the three judge decks refused, four passes, shuffled, batch 32,768, context 1024, rope. The SOP §2 invariant, unchanged from every size's baseline.
- **The same phases:** `weight 1.0, hold 0.10, fade 0.60`, no floor and no release flag — full pull for the first 10% of the voyage, linear to zero by 60%, content alone for the last 40%. The 9m's measured rule from the night, adopted as the standard; no floor because a floor is never by heart.
- **The five cards, in rank order, from the fleet's readings:** previous-word (L0H0), delimiter (L0H1), induction (L1H0, above previous-word), duplicate-word (L1H1), and the **comparator** as a riddle (16 composed items, pool 2048) where the size has a free layer: layer 2 head 2 at the 1m, layer 3 head 2 at the 9m.
- **The capacity rule, written into §4a:** a size pins the cards in rank order up to its heads. The 300k has four heads, so it pins the four metre cards and carries no riddle; withholding a card a size has room for, or pinning a Stage 1 below what it rests on, is not SOP-conformant. This is the one place the three voyages differ in method, and it is forced by depth and width, not chosen.
- **Read the same way:** the same evaluators at every size, and the end of the voyage rather than its peak.
- **Improvements wrapped into the SOP from tonight's runs:** the reading rule (end, not peak — the bridge's 2.27 was 0.11 at the end); no floor and no release flag; and an operational rule: **a launch must never depend on the network** — `uv run --no-sync` in `lanes.command` and in `queue.sh`, after a spaCy model wheel answered 504 from GitHub and killed nine voyages at thirty seconds each while the environment was intact.
- **Taking control, and what it exposed:** the coordinator had been started inside a sandbox where `pgrep`/`ps` return nothing, so `outside_voyage()` could not see a voyage at sea and launched into a held helm; it was restarted with real process visibility, and it correctly reports "in port: a voyage started outside the queue is at sea" until the stray that holds the helm docks. Also: another hand prepended its own lines above this block, so the SOP lines were moved back to the very top.
- **Casualties re-queued behind the SOP block:** `pan-v02`, the 1m second seed, the four maths shares, `pan-scaffold4` (s2), `pan-from50`, `pan-teach50`, `pan-teach50a9`. `scaffold4` s1 is at sea now — an orphan of the killed coordinator, finishing its 20 minutes.

## The SOP as data: s5.sop_versions, and a voyage that names its version is held to it (8 November, 20:35, Justin: "should the scaffold be part of the SOP data model with a relationship to a voyage in postgres and then the scaffold is just actually one line of the voyages sop.pretraining_phases.[content, scaffold] or something like that ... yeah do it and then keep an eye on the queue")

- **The row** (`s5.sop_versions`, migration `c1d9b0e4a2f7`): `version`, `adopted` (Justin's words), `doc` (the prose, `ships/plans/sop-v0.0.1.md`), `supersedes`, and `standard` (JSONB) — content (recipe, tokeniser, cleaning, passes, refusals), parameters (context, positions, batch, the optimiser), the sizes' learning rates and warmups, the five cards **in rank order**, and **the phases**: `[{scaffold, share 0.60, weight 1.0, hold 0.10, fade 0.60, floor 0.0}, {content, share 0.40}]`.
- **The voyage names it.** `sop: '0.0.1'` in the spec (the three queued launches already carry it) and, optionally, `departures` — what it does differently and why.
- **The gate asks** (policy `sop`, **advisory**, not enforced): a voyage that names a version must have its content, tokeniser, size learning rate and context, and its cards in the standard's order; it must carry no floor (a floor is never by heart) and must carry the scaffold phase. A voyage that names **no** version is not stopped — the cutover is Justin's to set, and everything already queued sails without one. `thorp sop list | show | check | seed`.
- **Why it is worth the table:** the question that prompted it — content first, then the missing cards, then fade — is now a row rather than a code path: `[{content, 0.40}, {scaffold, 0.40, hold 0.25, fade 1.0}, {content, 0.20}]` is v0.0.2 as data, compared on the same readings.
- **Not done, deliberately:** the trainer still reads `optim.scaffold`; `sop.resolve(spec)` is the bridge that would let a voyage omit its scaffold and take the standard's, and the phase walker (the runner stepping through a phase list rather than two fixed phases) is the next version's work. The readings are not duplicated into the row, and the cards are JSONB rather than tables, because there is one consumer today.
- **Tried:** `backend/tests/test_sop.py` (9 tests: the standard's shape, a grandfathered voyage, conformance, an unknown version, a wrong learning rate, a card out of order, a floor, a missing scaffold phase, and the store's row overriding the built-in copy).

## SOP v0.0.1 reaches the top of the ladder: the 21m and the 50m (8 November, 20:45, Justin: "can you also add a base SOP version of 21m and 50m to the queue")

- **The two voyages:** `fu-ogden-v0-1~sop001@21m-pre-s1` (the Mac lane, Phaleron — about 2.5 h, the 21m's measured wall time) and `fu-ogden-v0-1~sop001@50m-pre-s1` (**the Vast lane**, `vast:` — the paragraph's harbour, about 2.5 h on a 4090, ~$1 within the charter's $10 cap; a 50m on this Mac would take most of a day).
- **Both conform** to the standard (`thorp sop check`): the same corpus, tokeniser, cleaning, four passes, context 1024, batch 32,768; lr/warmup 0.0008/300 at the 21m and 0.0006/400 at the 50m; the same four metre cards in the same layers; the **comparator as a riddle in the middle layer** — `layers // 2`, so layer 4 of 8 at the 21m and layer 6 of 12 at the 50m. The middle-layer rule is now the standard's, not a per-run choice.
- **The queue at the head:** 300k, 1m, 9m, 21m (Mac), the 50m (Vast), then everything else. The 50m runs beside the Mac trio rather than after it: one ship out, one lane each.
- **Note on the 50m's backend:** it carries `model.backend: torch`, since that is what the charter sails; the torch trainer has the metre scaffold and the riddles, so the SOP's phases apply on both backends.

## Harbours and journeys as tables; the 100m is Invention (8 November, Justin: "harbour must be a table. journey must include a breakdown between pretraining and then posttraining. So pretraining is going somewhere and then posttraining is returning to harbour. then it would be journey.pretraining.stage[number].name, purpose etc."; "in that pretraining stage, if putting in cards, then we need to record which cards and those cards characteristics ... we'll always put basic cards in but not stage 1 cards, but in a bigger model we may change that"; "i just want to rename the 100m ship and then also have my quote appear on her in some form")

- **Harbours are `s1.harbours`** (`Harbour`): key, name, lane, where, depth and **ship builder**. The two are seeded from the old `waters.HARBOURS`: Phaleron (20M deep, the Mac) and Zayton (no bottom, Vast), Phaleron with `ship_builder = "Justin"` for now; **Zayton has none**, because ships are built at Phaleron and only sail from Zayton (Justin, 9 November: "there shouldn't be a shipbuilder in zayton. We don't build ships there, only sail there"). `thorp/dash/waters.harbours()` reads the table and falls back to the old two if a test has no store; `harbour_for` and the Ships page read it. The ship builder is a column, not yet a sign-off record: the signature over a manifest is the next step.
- **A journey is `s3.journeys` + `s3.journey_stages`.** A journey names its ship (a reference, not a foreign key), its home harbour, its aim, status, and the ship builder's signature fields (`signed_by`, `signed_at`, `manifest_sha1`). Its stages are split by phase: **pretraining is the outbound leg** (the ship goes somewhere to learn), **posttraining is the return** (it comes home with what it learned). The nested payload is exactly `journey.pretraining.stage[0].name`, `.purpose`, `.library`, `.tokens`, `.passes`, `.lr`, `.compose`, `.cards`; `journey.posttraining.stage[...]` is the return. `/api/journeys` and `/api/journey?key=...` serve it.
- **The seed journey is `invention-return`**: Phaleron, the 100m's outward Ogden v0.1 stage (four passes) and its returning modern-information stage (a draft; the library is not chosen yet). It is marked draft and does not sail.
- **Cards are recorded per stage, with their characteristics**: `journey_stages.cards` is a JSONB snapshot of `{"key","name","level","kind","metric","shared_metric","evaluator","threshold","about"}`. The Invention's pretraining stage carries the **seven basic cards** (level 0: previous_word, duplicate_word, offset_2, offset_3, attention_sink, delimiter, rare_word) and **not** stage 1 (comparator, Nevermore). `ship_store.card_snapshot(levels)` and `set_stage_cards(...)` change this by level when a bigger model adds stage 1; the snapshot keeps the characteristics as they stood when the plan was written.
- **The 100m is renamed Invention**, and the page carries the quote: *"Necessity is the mother of all invention, tedium is the step mother of invention, and being broke is the whore of invention." — Justin*. The overview says the Invention is the 100m: necessity sets the question, tedium is the reading, being broke is the budget. **The Medusa is not encoded yet**: her scored tests and metrics are still Origin/Leonardo, and no 3+ head metric was added.
- **A journey is a plan, not a voyage.** The name does not replace fitout/voyage/stage: the fitout is still the executable material, the voyage is one run, and the journey is the plan with its two legs. The foreign key from a journey to a ship was removed because a test's `truncate s1.ships cascade` deleted the plan; a journey may be written before its ship.
