- **Not done, because the scope is Justin's to set**: no name has been changed anywhere yet.

## The three harbours are Orbitals now: Schi'mint, Vavatch End, Masaq' Reach (11 November, Justin: "i love it, do it")

- **The rename is applied, and only the three places.** `phaleron → schimint` (**Schi'mint**, the first Orbital, where the young Culture trains its crews), `piraeus → vavatch-end` (**Vavatch End**, the great construction and fitting yard at the edge of the new worlds), `zayton → masaq-reach` (**Masaq' Reach**, the cosmopolitan Reach where every civilisation trades). Classes and expeditions are unchanged, as the mapping document argued: they are hulls and missions, not places in a world.
- **Old keys resolve.** `waters.canonical()` maps the three old keys at read time, so a stored `s1.harbours` row written before the rename (they still carry `phaleron`, `piraeus`, `zayton` and their old names) answers to the new key **and** the new name while keeping its own depth, builder and lane: no migration, and the page never shows a port the world no longer has. The frontend keeps the same alias table (`HARBOUR_ALIASES` in `views/harbours.ts`), the Astro route keeps the old three addresses, and `Harbours.tsx` forwards an old address to its Orbital with `history.replaceState`, keeping the sub-path.
- **The art is regenerated for the three, and the old plates are deleted**: `schimint.png` (a galleon of Drake's kind crossing black water toward a vast lit ring where a moon should be), `vavatch-end.png` (yards cradling half-built wooden hulls along an Orbital's rim), `masaq-reach.png` (a lantern-lit market concourse under a curved hull). The other hundred are untouched, as agreed: they wait for the rest of the naming.
- **What the rename cost**: nine files, four of them code. Verified: the API serves the three new keys and names; `/harbours/schimint`, `/harbours/vavatch-end`, `/harbours/masaq-reach` and the old `/harbours/phaleron` (forwarding) all answer 200; `harbour_for(15M)` is `schimint` and `harbour_for(50M)` is `vavatch-end`; the three art files serve.
- **Not done:** the 17 expeditions and 6 classes keep their names; the other hundred art files keep the old style until that naming lands.

## Goals in the store, and thirteen expeditions retired into four (11 November, evening; Justin: "the goals have to have a relationship to expeditions in the db ... audit all expeditions and their goals and then propose a queue ... so we start retiring expeditions and getting ready for our first model that we can chat to. This will be a 9m"; "all the arc de triomphe goals can be migrated to the knockout and we can cancel the arc de triomphe"; "a lot of the expeditions can actually be consolidated")

- **A goal is a row, and a queue line names it.** `s1.expeditions` (one row an expedition), `s1.expedition_goals` (one row a goal, keyed `expedition:position` — the same key `priorities` ranks by — with the verdict the store owns: `status`, `evidence`, `words`, `settled_at`) and `s2.queue_lines.goal_id`. The words are synced from `fleet.py` on every refresh; a sync never overwrites a verdict. Migration `v3q6r9s2t5u8`; `thorp goals [sync|show|set]`; `thorp queue add ... --goal`.
- **A goal may carry its own `position`.** Without it, deleting an earlier goal renumbers the later ones and their ids collide; with it, the knockout could lose its first two goals and keep `knockout:3`. `priorities.keys_for`, `priorities.goal_text` and the priority API read positions, not tuple order.
- **Knockout, reduced and widened.** Goal 1 (are the four cards causally necessary) is **answered**: six outside models read, every card `necessary: false` (Qwen3-4B's previous-word drop 0.025 against 0.258 for the largest random draw). Goal 2 (a reasoning budget in the return legs) is **dropped**: "for the 21m smoke smolm2, we don't need reasoning". Both stay as settled rows. The remaining goal is redefined: beat SmolLM2-360M on ARC-AGI-2 and match its 8,192 context window in the 21m. Arc de Triomphe's four goals move in as positions 4 to 7 and **the expedition is closed**; its investigations (`arc-qwen3-4b`, `deltanet-check-qwen3.8-27b`) now name the knockout.
- **Consolidation: 17 expeditions became 4 open and 13 closed.** Closed on their readings: book_value and apprentice (already), two_bets (type bet 0.3314/0.3145 against word bet 0.2159/0.2141 on both Pan seeds), induction (answered negative, 0.0087/0.0071 against its random twin), ogden (six runs home, previous-word 0.344-0.396 to 0.528-0.633 and the comparator 0.106-0.181 to 0.477-0.675 from 300k to 9m, two seeds), best_keel (15 of 55 300k runs hold all four rungs; `300k@v0.0.6-pins` leads at 0.573 at its final reading), first_light (duplicate-word 0.0136 at 1m against 0.3, but 0.876 at 50m), arc_de_triomphe (merged). Merged: Table Talk into kipling as goal 7; two-facts as knockout:8 and the logic tutor as knockout:9; the Smallest Bridge as card_census:2; the Apprentice II as seedling:2. The whole question and the queue are in `ships/reports/expedition-audit-2026-11-11.md` and `ships/specs/queue-proposed-2026-11-11.txt`.
- **What is left.** kipling (7 goals), knockout (9), card_census (2), seedling (2). The proposed queue runs the free readings first (the AI2 ARC audit, ~10 minutes a ship, and the two Qwen investigations), then the 300k control-token quartet and probe (kipling:2, kipling:4, ~12h), then the chat 9m (`9m@v0.0.6` and the new `9m@v0.0.6-return`, ~5h, closing kipling:3 and kipling:7 together), then the 1m closures, then the 21m (kipling:5, knockout:3).
- **The 9m pilot's IFEval is real but expensive.** `9m@v0.0.5-return-rigged` reads 0.8965 prompt-strict on the 541 against the 65% mark, but `eval/hole-cards-unseen/bpb_mean` went 2.09 → 4.75: the rig, trained on every prompt, cost the language. The honest held-out split (`ifeval-pairs-plain-split-v1.csv`) exists and has never been run.
- **The 300k failure of 11 November is fixed.** Every 300k ship had been dying in 30 seconds: `data/resolution.csv` (43 MB) was torn by two lanes writing it at once with no lock, and `prepare()` raised `UnicodeDecodeError` on the `refuse` lists. `write_resolution_csv` now reads past a torn row and writes under an exclusive lock with an atomic replace; the cache was repaired; `prepare()` succeeds on `300k@v0.0.6`, `-marks` and `-probe`. The earlier failures (22:03-22:47) were `OSError(24)` on the file limit, already fixed in `queue.sh`.
- **Not done:** quiet consolidation of the *closed* expeditions' rows (they stay as records); the 21m's 8,192-context spec (a real training change, not yet written); the honest split return leg; and the queue itself was rebuilt to the order on the evening of 11 November (the live queue is the plan).

## The order, set by Justin (11 November, evening): the instruct 9m first, then the SmolLM2 smoke

Justin: "let's establish order ... Chat 9m is high priority. I want an instruct model that I can use the UI to test ... the 21m is held off until I'm happy with the 9m ... the goal is to get a 9m I can have a tiny conversation with ... table talk needs to be folded into that"; on the knockout: ":1 update db with findings, :2 not drop, just low priority, :3 high priority, :4 this is part of :3 fold it into it ... Higher goal is to get an optimal 9m instruct model out to be used. Then the next primary goal after that is to smoke smol360m."

- **Priority order, recorded in `s1.expedition_priority`** (one row a rank; it was a JSON file until Justin, 11 November: "it shouldn't be saved in json it should be saved in postgres") and read by the queue page and the coordinator: kipling:3 (the instruct 9m) → knockout:3 (smoke SmolLM2-360M) → knockout:7 (the AI2 ARC audit, a reading that feeds the comparison) → kipling:4 (probe, medium) → kipling:2 (control tokens, low) → kipling:1 (IFEval, low) → knockout:2 (reasoning budget, low, **not dropped**) → knockout:6 (reasoning model, later) → knockout:5 (the arch) → knockout:8, knockout:9 → card_census:1, :2 → seedling:1, :2 → **kipling:5 (the 21m) last, held until the 9m is good**.
- **Folded.** Table Talk (`kipling:7`) into the chat 9m (`kipling:3`): the same goal — a 9m we can have a tiny conversation with, and the first instruct model the dashboard's Chat tab can drive. The Qwen audit (`knockout:4`) into the SmolLM2 smoke (`knockout:3`): "this is part of :3". Both stay in the store as settled records.
- **Un-dropped.** The reasoning-budget goal (`knockout:2`) is restored at position 2 as an open, low-priority goal: SmolLM2-360M does not reason, so reasoning is built after the 9m and after the smoke, not before.
- **Findings already in the store.** `kipling:6` (GQA): 300k two seeds, bpb 2.200/2.199 against the plain 2.197, mover 0.075/0.112 against 0.142, bridge 0.005/0.067 against 0.265 — no language gain, and it costs the mover and the bridge; not adopted. `knockout:1` (the four cards): six models read, every card `necessary: false`.
- **The live queue is rebuilt to the order and every line now names its goal**: `9m@v0.0.6` [kipling:3], `9m@v0.0.6-return` [kipling:3], the two investigations [knockout:3], the 1m control-token arms [kipling:2], then the 1m census and the 300k base [card_census:1]. `1m@v0.0.6-gqa` is dropped: the question it asked is answered.
- **Still blocked:** the coordinator is asleep on `ships/specs/kipling-candidate.hold`, the signature gate from the afternoon's plan — its release conditions (the probe reading and the control-token verdict) are the runs that failed today. Nothing sails, including the 9m, until Justin releases it.

## Every voyage carries milestones, and a missed one calls the ship back (12 November, Justin: "for every voyage we need to have milestones and based on those milestones being met or not met, then we can then call the ship back and then from there we can decide on whether to relaunch the ship's mission with revised parameters")

- **A milestone is a spec field**, judged **once**, at the first evaluation at or after the step it names: `at` (a share of the voyage) or `step`, then `metric` with `at_least` and/or `at_most`, `if_missed: recall|warn` (recall is the default), and `why`. The judgement is once on purpose: it is a gate at a checkpoint, not a monitor — a card that lights late does not retroactively pass the quarter mark; trends are what the readings and charts are for.
- **A missed milestone that asks for a recall stops the voyage at that checkpoint.** `train.py` checks after every evaluation, saves the checkpoint, writes the run's status as `recalled: milestone N missed at step S: <metric> <value> (wanted >= X) — <why>` and returns; the queue takes the next line. A `warn` milestone is recorded and the voyage sails on.
- **The verdict is a reading**: `milestone/<n>/value` and `milestone/<n>/met` metrics plus a `milestone` artifact (the JSON), so `thorp milestones [run]` prints each one's step, value and verdict, and the ship page can read them like any other number. A metric that this evaluation did not produce (a light eval runs only some evaluators) leaves the milestone **unjudged** and it is checked again at the next evaluation.
- **A milestone bug never stops a voyage**: the check is wrapped, reported and skipped — the gate that stops a run is policy (S3), not bookkeeping.
- **The first voyage to carry them** is the queued `300k@v0.0.6-marks`: `stars/previous_word/max_score >= 0.5` at half (it lights in five of the sixteen 300k census arms and reads 1.0 on the plain 300k), and `eval/hole-cards-unseen/bpb_mean <= 2.6` at three-quarters (the plain 300k reads 2.197; 2.6 is a catastrophe guard, and the rigged 9m pilot ended at 4.75). Both `recall`.
- **The revisit is a person's**: a recall keeps its checkpoint and its numbers; the revised parameters are a new spec (copy, change, `thorp queue add --goal`). Nothing relaunches itself — Justin: "from there we can decide whether to relaunch". The recall reason is the input to that decision.
- **Not done:** a per-expedition default set of milestones (the thresholds are per voyage and are Justin's); suggesting thresholds from comparable runs' readings; and the ship page does not yet draw the milestone markers on the charts.

## Every branch of the Library has its own page (11 November, Justin: "each branch needs it's own page http://localhost:4321/library")

- **The address is the branch.** `/library` is the branches' page; `/library/ogden`, `/library/scanned`, `/library/decks` and the rest are the branches' own pages. The branch card is a link to that address, so a link, a bookmark, a reload and a modified click (open in a new tab) all land on the same branch. The crumb back to `/library` is a link too.
- **The island reads the address after mount.** `Library.tsx` keeps the path in state, reads it through `libraryBranchOf` (`lib/nav.ts`: `/library/<key>`, the old `/shelves/<key>` and the old hash forms all answer), and follows the browser's back and forward with `popstate`. A left click calls `history.pushState`, so switching branches is instant and the address is still the branch's.
- **Every branch answers a direct load.** `pages/library/[...path].astro` writes a page for the eight default branches. A branch saved in the store under any other key is served by the dash's existing section fallback in production, and in development by a small Vite middleware that serves the Library's shell for any one-segment `/library/<key>` address (the island then reads the key). A key that names no branch says so ("no branch is called …") and links back rather than pretending it is `/library`.
- **The branches stay data.** No branch key is copied into the backend or into the branch page's content; the static route only enumerates the defaults, which is the one list the static build cannot read from Postgres.
- **Verified:** the branch links carry `/library/<key>`; clicking Ogden changes the address to `/library/ogden`, shows its seven variations, back returns to eight branch cards, forward returns to Ogden, and a direct load of `/library/ogden` renders Ogden. `/library/made-up` renders the not-found branch state.
- **Not done:** a variation is still picked in page state, not by an address of its own; and the branch page does not yet set the document title.

## A branch is a purpose; a configuration is a JSON of choices (11 November, Justin: "my thinking is that branches ... have not so much a version but rather a set of attributes. Like for ogden it woud be size, books, control tokens, language, content_makeup (this would allow sliders and saving different variations), date. So there's too many variables so my thinking is that a branch doesn't get a version but rather a json of choices")

- **The branch stays, the version goes.** A branch is its purpose (Ogden, the decks, the scanned corpus). A **configuration** is `{branch, choices}`: one JSON object, saved with a name and a content hash (`thorp/library_configs.py`). Saving the same choices twice is the same configuration; changing one choice is a different configuration, never "v0.3". The old named lists (`ogden-v0.1`, `v0.1.1`, `v0.1.2`, `v0.1.3`, `v0.2`, ...) remain in `s3.library_variations` as **sailed, locked** rows, so every old run and spec keeps its exact list; they are not rewritten into the new model.
- **The choices are the controls Justin named.** `size` is the corpus budget in words (`words`, plus `max_book_words`), not the model size, and the whole branch is the top of the slider. `books` is how the books are found (select from the library, an exact list, or the whole library) plus rank and title dedupe. `control_tokens` is a **format** choice (`wrap: none|bos-eos`, `turns: plain|marked`), deliberately not part of `selection.compose`: it marks the text after the books are chosen, which is why v0.1.1–v0.1.3 existed. `language` is shares of the words; `content_makeup` is shares by kind and voice; `date` is the period; `ogden` is the Ogden-ness bar.
- **The bridge is still the existing composer.** The book-choice half of a configuration becomes a `thorp/selection.py` spec (`to_selection`), with `carry_only` for an exact list. `POST /api/library_config/preview` composes it over the warm library facts and returns the books, the words, the makeup and the spec — so the number under the sliders is the real composed count, not a client guess.
- **The store is additive.** `s3.library_configs` (migration `x5s8t1u4v7w0`): key (branch + hash prefix), branch, name, note, normalised choices (JSONB), config hash, created-at/by. The CLI's `thorp select branches seed` and the branch payload are unchanged; `/api/library_browse` adds `attributes`, `default_choices` and `configurations`, and `/api/library_configs?branch=` reads configurations live so a save shows after a reload even though the branch payload is warm.
- **The page is the editor.** `/library/ogden` now opens with the branch's choices: size and Ogden-ness sliders, date bounds, books mode/rank/dedupe, control-token switches, language shares and content-makeup sliders; a live composed summary; the JSON in a box that can be edited and applied; Save, Copy JSON and Delete. Saved configurations are cards with Load and Delete. The old named lists sit below under **Sailed configurations**, marked locked, because history is not a template.
- **Defaults are branch defaults.** Ogden opens on its 68% bar, English, one copy a title; the other branches open on their first sailed variation. A partial JSON sent to the API still starts from the branch's defaults (a partial 20M save keeps the 0.68 bar).
- **Verified:** `thorp/library_configs`' tests pass; the migration applied to the live store; `POST /api/library_config/preview` composes 20M words to 412 books; the UI changed the size slider to 30M (546 books), saved, reloaded and loaded the saved configuration back. The test rows were deleted.
- **Not done:** the saved configurations are not yet the scope of the sailed facet browser below them (it still explores the locked lists); a fitout still names a list rather than a configuration key, so a configuration must compose to a list before a voyage can read it; the `control_tokens` format is validated and saved but the writer that stamps a composed list with it is not built yet (the old stamped lists v0.1.1–v0.1.3 remain the only stamped artifacts); deleting is a hard delete; the branch attribute schema is in code, not editable from the page; and the page does not set the document title.

## The talk leg: the first ship to talk to is taught on real exchanges, and a leg reads with its seed's pieces (11 November, night; Justin: "let's make sure the 9m return leg is bullet proof this will be our first talking model so I want to maximise the ability of it")

- **What the rigged leg teaches.** Its 499 turns answer every prompt with the shortest response the checker passes, and that response is a filler: "The man was in the house." (20 of the 139 held-out pairs word for word, most of the rest a variation). The pilot (`9m@v0.0.5-return-rigged`) asked eight new questions answered "The man was in the house." to six of them ("What is your name?", "Where is the dog?", "What colour is the sky?", "How many legs has a horse?"). 89.6% on IFEval is that sentence in the shapes the checkers ask for. `9m@v0.0.6-return` is the same recipe in marks: 2,274 of its 2,515 steps are those turns. Justin kept it queued, then (that night): "fix it i don't want to waste a trip"; see the last bullet.
- **A leg reads with its seed's pieces.** `9m@v0.0.6` reads `clean: raw` and its legs read `marks-v1`, and a tokeniser's id carries its cleaning, so each leg would have built a second tokeniser (`5bfdad65`) beside the seed's (`569b5d8f`). The merges are learned from the cleaned text and four of the 2,048 ids had changed hands (`▁On` with `▁fine`, `▁bes` with a run of twelve spaces): the seed's weights would have read each as the other. `seeded_master` checked the vocabulary's size and the list, not the table, and the S2 lint reported it ("reads marks-v1 text but is seeded from 9m@v0.0.6, which read raw") without stopping anything. Now `train.prepare` gives a seeded leg its seed's piece table under its own cleaning (`tokeniser.graft`: the seed's model file, this leg's settings, its own id `17df64b3`), a voyage with a checkpoint keeps the tokeniser it was written with, and `seeded_master` refuses a table that differs. The lint's finding is retired. `tests/test_seed_tokeniser.py`.
- **The turns** (`backend/scripts/make_talk_corpus.py` → `lists/talk-turns-marked-v2.csv`): 37,244 exchanges, a question and the reply that followed it, from the 1,667 dialogue candidates on this machine (36,440 from plays, 1,437 from philosophical dialogues, 835 from table talk, 70 from catechisms; 34,817 by a speaker's label, 3,961 from quoted speech in prose). Kept when the first speech ends in a question mark, both are short (a question of 2 to 30 words, a reply of 1 to 30), in plain letters, free of Elizabethan forms (thou, hath, 'tis), and 65% of their words are Ogden's (the dialogue stage's own bar). An identical reply is kept at most 25 times, so "Yes." is not the answer to everything (1,617 struck). One book in twenty is held out whole: 1,538 exchanges, 400 of them in `lists/talk-heldout-v1.jsonl`. This is the turn converter Table Talk's plan asked for on 7 November, for the marked format.
- **The legs** (`9m@v0.0.6-talk`, `9m@v0.0.6-talk-hot`, both kipling:3): the dialogue shelf once (241 steps), then the turns twelve times (45 steps a pass), 780 steps, the pull off, seeded from `9m@v0.0.6`. They differ in the learning rate alone: 0.0003 (a third of the SOP's; the seed finishes at 0.0001 and the pilot at 0.001 lost its language) and the SOP's 0.001, so the rate is read and not assumed. Read every 100 steps on 80 held-out exchanges (`answers/bpb`, the reply's loss given its question; `answers/stopped`, whether it ends its own turn) and on the hole cards, a checkpoint every 50 steps and all kept.
- **Milestones, on the scale the leg is read on.** The hole cards read through `marks-v1` are not the seed's raw reading: the seed at step 3,200 reads 2.15 raw and **1.70** as the leg reads it (its held-out replies 2.24 bits a byte, and it ends its turn 1% of the time). So: a recall at 40% if the hole cards pass 2.2; a warning at 90% if they pass 1.8 (Table Talk's third check) or if it ends its turn less than 80% of the time (the first).
- **The order.** The talk legs sail before the rigged leg (about 20 minutes each against its hour and its 541-prompt reading), so there is a ship to talk to soon after the 9m docks. `thorp queue move fitouts/9m@v0.0.6-return 1` puts it back first.
- **The chat's sampling** (`chat.DECODING`, `evaluators/samples.py shaped`): a Thorp ship's answer is drawn from its 40 likeliest pieces and the smallest set holding 90% of the probability, with a piece written in the last 64 made less likely (1.15), and a marked ship stops at `<end>`. Before, it was sampled over the whole vocabulary and looped when greedy ("the man of the man of the man"). The evaluators' draws are unchanged: with no shaping asked for, `generate` is what it was.
- **Not done, and Justin's.** Which leg the Chat tab should name as the 9m to talk to, once both are read (the best checkpoint by `answers/bpb`, not necessarily the last step: `chat.answer(step=...)` reads any of them). A handful of turns about the ship itself ("What is your name?"): nothing in the dialogue books answers for Thorp, and those are words for Justin to write. Whether the rigged leg should still sail at all. The torch backend's `seeded_master` has not been given the refusal (the graft in `prepare` covers it; the check does not).

## The architecture programme, built (11 November, night; Justin: "we need to do 1 - 7 and then 9, 10 and 11"; "Here, in order"; "finish off the architecture changes")

The ten items of `ships/reports/architecture-and-kipling-2026-11-11.md` (all but 8, the dashboard's open port, and 12, backup), built while `9m@v0.0.6` was at sea. Two rules held throughout: **no run id moved** (192 of 192 stored runs re-hash to their id; 414 of 414 specs on disk hash as they did before the programme; the one key added to `NOT_HASHED` is `expedition`, which no spec used), and **every new behaviour that could change a reading is opt-in**, or was proven identical first.

- **1, the two trainers.** The torch loop refuses any spec key it would not honour (`train_torch.HONOURED`, an allow-list; `UNSUPPORTED` is empty), before work and before a box is hired; it now honours per-head scaffold schedules, teacher-head targets, `kv_heads`, probes, milestones, `sop_version`; MLX and torch agree to under 1e-6 on every loss term on a twin leg. A test fails on the Mac when a spec uses a key the torch loop was never taught.
- **2, a death at sea.** What is at sea is a row (`s2.at_sea`); a restarted coordinator adopts live jobs and records dead ones as `lost`, never requeued; every line sails in its own process group; a hired box is never released for lost contact, only at the overrun deadline; `thorp charter attach` resumes a box still up.
- **3, what a run id means.** The id is unchanged; beside it every voyage records its provenance (`s1.run_provenance` and a `provenance` artifact): each list's bytes, the parents' run ids actually used, the code and its diff, the environment that changes results. A resume with a different tokeniser is refused; a finished spec whose lists changed warns. Parents may be named by run id.
- **4, the metrics write.** Step metrics are buffered (`ledger.flush_every`, default 25; always flushed before an evaluation, a checkpoint and the end); the fold from a hired box is incremental and in one transaction (`s2.fold_marks`) — and works again: no fold into Postgres could succeed after the cut-over.
- **5, evaluators.** `eval/seconds/<evaluator>` is recorded; `eval.offline` defers evaluators to `thorp score <run>`, which writes the same numbers at the same steps; greedy writing uses a KV cache (ifeval 143 s → 58 s on the 1m, outputs identical; `THORP_GENERATE_CACHE=0` turns it off and is recorded in the provenance). Sampled writing keeps the full pass: in bf16 the cached draws differ.
- **6, one store.** The queue is read only from `s2.queue_lines` (`queue_read.py`); the library is Postgres unless `THORP_LIBRARY_PG=0`; a store that is down raises instead of being answered from a SQLite file. `data/manifest.sqlite` is still written, as the copy a hired box reads.
- **7, the dashboard's reads.** `s1.metric_latest`, kept by statement-level triggers on `s1.metrics`; the slow readers are set-based, payloads identical: `/api/ships` 15.4 s → 0.44 s, the ladder 25.5 s → 0.78 s, `/api/bridge` 16.7 s → 0.01 s. `/api/metrics` takes `names=` and `points=`.
- **9, outside models.** `thorp/hooked.py`: one loader and one hooked model, edits by real layer index applied inside each layer, holding through cached generation; `Inside` is rebuilt on it and bit-identical on a pure-attention model. `math_heads causal` readings generated before today silenced the wrong heads after the first token and need re-running; a census assembled on a hybrid model before today needs re-running.
- **10, the stream and the checkpoints.** A pass gathers windows from the book memmaps, batches byte-identical (9m: 1,271 MB → 72 MB to build a pass); `checkpoint.keep_last/keep_every` and `thorp checkpoints prune` (never a parent's or an unscored step); SIGTERM/SIGHUP finish the step, save a checkpoint, write `stopped: SIGTERM at step N` and exit 143; the coordinator gives a stopping voyage 180 s.
- **11, data not code.** `expedition:` on a spec (not hashed) and `s1.run_expedition`; `s2.lane_defs` (a third lane is a row; the CHECK on `s2.lanes` is now a foreign key); `s1.run_state` with a heartbeat, and the coordinator marks a silent, uncovered run `lost`.
- **Also fixed on the way:** a Postgres read now ends the transaction it opened (the coordinator had held a lock on `s1.runs` since 18:42; the fix is live for it at its next restart); a seeded voyage grows from its overridden shape; `latest_checkpoint` skips a half-written `step-N.tmp`; the census's "Fitout: Thorp" runs are the census's again (39 placed).
- **Live only after a restart, by a person, in port:** the coordinator (pid 18653) and the 9m at sea run the code they loaded. The next voyage the coordinator launches runs the new trainer; the coordinator's own changes (run ids in `parse`, the read fix, lane rows, the lost sweep) need it restarted, and launchd needs the plist reloaded for `AbandonProcessGroup`.
- **Not done:** the single shared driver for the two loops (the seam is written in item 1's notes in `backend/CUDA.md`); `/api/compare`'s 27.9 MB (the frontend reads every point); archiving `ledger.sqlite` and `ships/specs/queue-done.txt` (Justin's go-ahead); the library's other files stay until trigram measures and resolutions are written to the store; 26 old runs the label chain cannot place.

## One stepping voyage on the Mac at a time (11 November, night; Justin: "happy with this: One stepping voyage on the Mac at a time")

- **Decided.** The Mac lane runs one line at a time. `s2.lanes` mac seats set 2 → 1 at about 21:00 through the dashboard's own handler (`api_constraints_set`); the coordinator re-reads it each loop, and the 9m at sea was not touched. With one seat, `cmd:` lines wait their turn too.
- **Why (the ledger, 154 voyages at 300k and 1m).** Two 300k voyages sharing the GPU each step 2.19× slower (a 9% net loss against sailing them in turn); three step 4.2× slower (40%). About 9 h of 300k step time on 9–10 November was spent paired, and the 21m of 9 November lost about 7 of its 14 hours to stalls at a 24.7 GB peak.
- **Not changed.** `lanes.TWO_AT_ONCE` and the `s2.lane_defs` mac row (seats 2, shared `{300k}`) still describe the old rule; they are inert while the live seat count is 1. Setting the Mac's seats back to 2 in the dashboard restores it.
- **The rigged leg, fixed rather than wasted** (Justin: "fix it i don't want to waste a trip"). `9m@v0.0.6-return` now reads, after the dialogue shelf, twelve passes of `lists/return-mix-marked-v1.csv`: the 1,490 books of talk turns, each also holding 28 of the 499 instruction turns (82 to 84 copies of each over the list, so about a thousand readings each over twelve passes, the pilot's dose), shuffled together in every book. The instruction turns are no longer read alone, which is what made the pilot answer every question with their filler. 3,096 steps, about 75 minutes. Still **rigged** for IFEval, and said so in its spec. Read every 250 steps (the hole cards, 80 held-out exchanges) with a checkpoint each time; IFEval on the 541 once, when it docks. Two milestones, both `warn` so the trip always brings home its IFEval reading: the hole cards past 2.2 at 40%, and ending its turn less than 80% of the time at 90%.
- **Each turn its own document** (`talk-turns-marked-v2`, replacing v1 before anything sailed on it): between two turns in a book stand `<eos>` and `<bos>`, so through `marks-v1` every turn reads `<bos>\n<question>Q<answer>A<end>\n<eos>`, exactly what the chat asks with. In v1 only a book's first turn followed `<bos>`.
- **The coordinator restarts itself from the queue** (Justin: "we need to restart the coordinator when the 9m docks. can we schedule it in the queue"): `cmd: sh backend/scripts/restart_coordinator.sh`, first in the queue, so it is taken when the 9m frees the lane and before the talk legs. It waits for any voyage at sea, checks that `lanes` and `train` on disk import (if not, it leaves the running coordinator alone and exits 1), stops the coordinator, and makes sure launchd (`com.thorp.queue`, KeepAlive on an unsuccessful exit) has started another within 90 seconds, asking with `kickstart` and then starting `queue.sh` by hand if not. The new coordinator adopts the line, as it adopted the hold at 18:42. `--dry-run` reports and stops nothing (tried 20:42: it would stop pid 18653).


## The harbour cards flip to five views each (11 November, Justin: "the cards to harbours to flip so each harbour has 5 images showing the different aspects of the harbour based upon our image style, themes and world")

- **Each Orbital has five views**: its own plate first, then four new ones made with fal in the three-layer brief (Risso's ink, Drake's voyages, the young Culture's world, each with its ring or drone). Schi'mint: the ring, the drill, the chart school, the fitting-out berth, the first voyage. Vavatch End: the yards, the slip, the forge, the launch, the hiring gate. Masaq' Reach: the market, the quay, the scales, the exchange, the gauge post. Twelve new files in `frontend/public/art/` named `<harbour>-<view>.png`; the prompts are `HARBOUR_VIEWS` in `generate_art.py` and the captions `HARBOUR_VIEWS` in `views/harbours.ts` (a view is added in both together).
- **The card** has a "5 views" button; it turns on its Y axis to an image viewer (large view, caption, five thumbnails, arrow keys, Esc to turn back, a link to open the harbour). The front is still the link it was. The back's images are fetched on the first flip, not at page load. The poll rewrites the cards every 15 s, so the flip state is kept in `views/harbourflip.ts` and painted back after each write.
- **Not done**: the three plates are still the 8-11 November ones; the new views were not given captions from live data (the notes are fixed prose).

## The evaluation harness stops repeating itself (11 November, night; Justin: "Yes, build them")

- **Built, three savings, none of which changes a number an evaluator returns.** (1) `bpb_split` reads the per-piece losses `ce_bpb` already worked out at the step (`EvalContext.cache`, `ce_bpb.losses_of`), so a full evaluation no longer runs the judge books' forward passes twice: `bpb_split` 11.7 s → 0.4 s on a 300k. (2) `tagger.last_tag` remembers each text's tag for the life of the process (the tagger is pinned), so `shapes` tags only the texts whose top guess changed since the last reading (64–88% were already tagged): about 1.6 s → 0.6–0.9 s. (3) `logic.score_deck` runs each distinct (prefix, idea, rival) pass once: 464 passes → 288, about 2.6 s → 1.6 s.
- **Proved on saved weights, not argued.** A 300k at steps 640, 5120, 8708 and a 1m at its first and last checkpoints, read before and after: all 435 numbers, every artifact hash and every `evals` row identical (largest difference 0). New tests in `backend/tests/test_eval_speed.py`; 121 evaluator tests pass.
- **A first version batched `logic`'s asks into 64-row passes (0.14 s of forward passes) and was rejected.** The model is read in bf16, where a different batch shape rounds a little differently: the readings moved by up to 0.0024 (the gain by 0.0005, the score not at all) against an old reading that did not move with pair order. The chaining gain is the 50m's headline measure, so its rounding is not for a speed-up to change. Lesson: never batch an evaluator's asks together; keep each pass the shape it had.
- **Measured effect is smaller than I estimated** (I said a light evaluation 5.4 s → 1.5 s; it is about 5.4 s → 3.5 s, `logic` still being 1.6 s of that). A 300k's ~136 light and 13 full evaluations save about 7 min of ~52. Timings were taken beside the live 9m, so the ratios are better than the seconds. Not done: deferring heavy evaluators to after the return leg (`eval.offline` exists; no spec uses it), and `ce_bpb`'s own 11 s a full reading (the judge books are scored in 16-window batches of 1,024 pieces; not touched).
- **Live only for voyages launched after this**: the running 9m loaded the old code.

## The 300k is the towing tank and the 1m the measured mile (11–12 November; Justin: "we need an analogy for the 300k ... we mix a bunch of combinations there and then measure it to see what comes out"; "i like towing tank, please update the fal images and the naming"; "Yes, rename 1m")

- **The names.** The 300k is **The towing tank** (was Faces since the morning of the 11th, Pan before it): the trough of still water a shipwright draws scale hulls down, one shape after another, timing each. The 1m is **The measured mile** (was Augustus): the marked open water where a real hull is timed between two posts, to check what the tank predicted. The pair says what the two smallest sizes are for: change one thing and read it at model scale, then run the finding again one size up before it is trusted. It also carries the caveat already on this log, that a tank reading does not always scale (a 300k passing 21m and 50m tests a 9m fails).
- **What did not change.** The objectives: the 300k's first readings are still the four Face cards, the 1m's is still De Morgan's maths book. The presets and the `kind` values. The closed two-bets expedition keeps its run label "Faces". Augustus, Plato, Francis and Diderot, the positions at the 1m, keep their names and plates.
- **Where it lives.** Two migrations set name, role and overview in `s1.sizes` (`c0x3y6z9a2b5`, `d1y4z7a0b3c6`; each downgrade restores the name before). The 1m's overview still described 4 layers of 128; it now says twelve layers of 80, four heads of 20, about 1.3M parameters at vocabulary 4,096 (counted, 1,333,200). The class slugs are `towing-tank` and `measured-mile`; `faces`, `pan`, `keel`, `seedling`, `augustus`, `pilot` and `proxy` forward (`CLASS_RENAMES` in `server.py` and `router.ts`, and the static paths of `/ships` and `/sizes`).
- **The rung cards say the same name.** The harbour's cards called the two smallest "the sounding" and "the survey" (the soundings' language of 9 November) and wore those plates, so a renamed class would not have shown. `RUNG_METAPHOR` and `RUNG_ART` in `views/harbours.ts` now give them the class's name and plate; the five larger rungs keep the soundings' names (the landfall to the new world). A card whose rung and class share a name says it once. `sounding.png` and `survey.png` stay on disk, unused.
- **A two-word name found a bug.** `server._class_names` matched `CLASS_RENAMES` against the name with a space ("towing tank"), so the old names (keel, pilot) stopped resolving and the 300k would have been asked the speller's questions. It now matches the hyphenated slug too.
- **The plates** (`generate_art.py`, `CLASSES`): `towing-tank.png` and `measured-mile.png`, which the class cards pick up by slug, so `CLASS_ART` in `views/harbours.ts` is empty. Froude's tank and the Admiralty's measured mile are Victorian and the world is Drake's, so both are drawn as a yard of that age would build them: a timber trough, a line and a falling weight, a sandglass; two fire beacons on a headland. The tank took three generations and the mile three; the second tank and the third mile were kept. `pan.png` and `augustus.png` stay on disk (Augustus the position still wears his).
- **Not done.** The dashboard on 8787 was not restarted (it keeps the names it read at start, so it still says Faces and Augustus under the new card titles until it is).

## The next batch, by goal, and why the talking 9ms answer beside the question (12 November, morning; Justin: "go through all the expeditions that are open and all their goals and then look at the queue and then go through all the learnings to schedule the next batch of voyages"; "do a deep analysis of the first chat enabled 9ms ... it keeps answering gibberish. What needs to happen so it answers in something semi legible")

- **What the night brought home.** `9m@v0.0.6` (277.8 min), then its three legs. `-talk` (lr 0.0003): `answers/bpb` 1.776, ends its turn 100%, hole cards 1.815 (the 1.8 warning missed by 0.016). `-talk-hot` (lr 0.001): 1.787, hole cards 2.017. `-return` (the mix): 1.836, hole cards 2.635, IFEval prompt-strict 0.9057 on the 541 (rigged), ends its turn 98%. The lower rate keeps the language at no cost to the replies. The restart line exited 1 ("no coordinator was running": its `pgrep` saw nothing from inside the coordinator's own children), so **coordinator 18653 is still the one started 18:42 on 11 November, on the code it loaded then.**
- **Two goals had nothing queued and nobody had noticed.** The 300k control-token octet and the probe pair all exited 1 on the torn cache on 11 November and left the queue; the rebuilt queue never got them back. kipling:4 (rank 3) had no line at all, and kipling:2 had only its 1m arms, without the 300k proof that was pre-registered.
- **Queued, in Justin's order** (24 lines, about 25 h of the Mac at one seat, each tagged with its goal): the deltanet check [knockout:3]; five AI2 ARC readings of our own ships, `investigations/arc-ai2-{300k,1m,9m,21m,50m}` (`thorp eval <ship> --evaluators arc_ai2`, 45 min deadline each) [knockout:7]; `300k@v0.0.6-probe`, `-probe-s2` and the probe reading [kipling:4]; `300k@v0.0.6`, `-s2`, `-marks`, `-marks-s2` and the four return legs [kipling:2], ahead of the 1m pair already queued (the tank before the mile); then the census lines as they were, with `1m@v0.0.6-hold25` added before `-hold25-double`, which is written to be read against it and had no such run [card_census:2]. Lint clean; every spec passes the gate and is a new run id.
- **Not queued, because no line can serve them yet.** kipling:3 needs a corpus (below). kipling:1's honest split at 9m needs the mix rebuilt on the 360 training prompts (the 300k return legs give the honest number at 300k). kipling:5 is held by Justin. knockout:2 and :6 need the thinking pieces and the two-phase budget, which are not built. knockout:5 waits on the audit. knockout:8 is a reading once the batch gives one standard at 300k and 1m (logic gain today: 300k +0.006 to +0.03, 1m +0.003, 9m −0.049, 21m@v0.0.5 +0.024: it does not climb). knockout:9's generator and writers do not exist. card_census:1's twenty-one `cd-1m-*` arms, the `sl-*` and `ma2-*` specs are pre-SOP and the gate refuses every one. **seedling:1 cannot be sailed on the deep ladder at all**: `growth.grow` needs the width to be a whole multiple and the head size equal, and the 300k is 48 wide with heads of 24 where the 1m is 80 wide with heads of 20.
- **Known handicap in two queued arms.** `1m@v0.0.6-hold25` and `-hold25-double` carry `kv_heads: 2`, written before kipling:6 found that shared heads cost the mover and the bridge at 300k. The pair is still a fair comparison with each other; neither is one with the plain 1m.
- **The talking 9ms: the question is hardly read.** Asked as it was taught (`<bos>\n<question>Q<answer>`, checked against the rendered prompt in the saved threads), the talk leg reads a held-out reply at 1.7065 bits a byte given its own question, 1.7440 given another exchange's question and 1.7526 given none (400 held-out exchanges). The question is worth 0.037 bits a byte, 2%. **That figure was 0.037 at step 50 and is 0.037 at step 816**: the leg taught the ship what a line of a play sounds like (1.93 → 1.71) and nothing about using the question. Where the true reply reuses a word of its question the gain fell, 0.083 → 0.058. The mix leg is the same (0.017 → 0.041 over 3,096 steps).
- **The cause is the corpus, not the ship or the chat.** In the mined exchanges a reply shares a content word with its own question 19.5% of the time (3.2% with a shuffled one): a play's reply answers the plot, which the ship never sees. So the right bet is a short, fluent line that fits the question's shape and ignores its content, and that is what it writes: "Are you hungry?" → "Yes, I am."; "Where is the dog?" → "Here, sir, I have been here."; "What colour is the sky?" → "I have a little picture in the house." The same seed draws nearly the same line for different questions ("Her friend, my lord!").
- **`answers/bpb` does not measure answering.** It fell 0.22 while the question's share stood still. The reading a talk leg should be judged on is the **question gain**: the reply's bits a byte given another question, less given its own. Not built as an evaluator; the two scripts that read it are not in the repo.
- **What would make it semi-legible, in order of cost.** (1) Draw colder: greedy answers are the more sensible ones above, and the console draws at 0.7. (2) A corpus where the reply is determined by the question: every reply reusing its question's words or restating something stated in the prompt, in Ogden's vocabulary, each pattern many thousand times. The 19.5% of mined exchanges that already do this are about 7,000, a seasoning and not a corpus. (3) Judge the leg on the question gain with a milestone, so a leg that learns only the register is recalled. The diary-entry question (kipling:3) is of the right kind, since its content comes from the prompt, but it needs the ship to carry words from the description into the entry, and `9m@v0.0.6` reads the mover at 0.013 and the bridge at −0.004.

## Our ships on AI2 ARC, and the extra small board as three test cards (12 November; Justin: "can you score all our models on http://localhost:4321/leaderboard/xsmall on the arc easy and the arc challenge and see if we have any that score and would make it into the leaderboard"; "the leaderboard ... needs to have cards for each test ... with fal images the user must click and then they flip over with the leaderboard"; "can you also add a card to xsmall for IFEval")

- **Every sailing of the five ships read** with `thorp eval <run> --evaluators arc_ai2` (the full 2,376 and 1,172, nothing skipped): 300k v3 26.8/24.2 and 26.0/24.5 (Easy/Challenge, two seeds); 1m v2 25.0/22.4 and 25.8/21.7; 9m v2 25.6/21.8, 25.5/22.2 and the torch sailing 25.6/22.4; 21m v1 26.5/21.7. **All at chance** (25%): the best, the 21m, is +1.5 points, about one standard error (0.9 points on 2,376 questions). None beats pythia-14m's 29.0, the bottom of the board, and none clears the floor on Challenge. The 50m's reading is below, when it docks.
- **The board reads a ship's own metrics now** (`leaderboard._ship_arc`): each split averaged over the sailings read, at each one's latest reading; `_ships` carries `run_ids` for it. The board was reading `s4.outside_readings` only, so a ship read by `thorp eval`, as the page told you to do, would still have said "not read yet". A read ship ranks with the outside models and says "ours · n sailings averaged"; only an unread one stays below the line.
- **IFEval in the payload** (`leaderboard._ifeval_rows`, `payload()["ifeval"]`): every voyage with an `ifeval/prompt_strict`, its n and `rigged` when the spec says RIGGED. Today there are two, both 9m return legs on the 541 and both rigged (90.6 and 89.6): the card's board ranks nothing and says so. No outside model has been read on IFEval.
- **Three test cards** replace the ARC and steps panels on `/leaderboard/xsmall` (`StepsBoard.tsx` `TestCard`, `.testcards`/`.tflip` in global.css): AI2 ARC, chaining two facts, IFEval. The face is the plate, what the test reads, the link to it (the AI2 page, our method page, the IFEval paper) and its headline; a click turns it over and the turned card takes the whole row for its board. The plates are `test-arc`, `test-chaining` and `test-ifeval` in `generate_art.py` (`TESTS`): a master holding up a candle as the question while an apprentice chooses among four slates; two bearings ruled from two beacons crossing at the fix; sealed orders on the mainmast carried out knot for knot. The first draws had a wristwatch and a propeller drone, so they were drawn again.
- **Not done:** the 50m's ARC reading (its fitout reads in PyTorch on the CPU, and it was still running); the other session's five `arc-ai2-*` investigations in the queue will re-read the best sailing of each ship and get the same numbers.
- **pythia-14m and pythia-31m read whole, in float32** (Justin: "do the float32 fix and rescore the two pythias"). `score_arc_ai2.py --dtype float32` casts the weights before reading and keeps `dtype` in the reading. Every question now reads: 14m 29.5 Easy / 20.6 Challenge (was 29.0 / 20.7 over 2,299 and 1,133), 31m 32.8 / 21.8 (was 33.4 / 21.2 over 2,355 and 1,161). The order of the board is unchanged; 29.5 is now the bottom row our ships would have to beat.
- **IFEval for every model in the band** (Justin: "can you also score all the xsmall models on ifeval and add them to the leaderboard"). `scripts/score_ifeval.py` reads an outside model with the evaluator's own prompts and checks (`ifeval._cases`, `ifeval._check`), greedy, one sample, a 512-piece cap, and keeps a receipt row a prompt as `ifeval-<model>` in `s4.outside_readings`. The format follows the checkpoint and is stated in every row: Qwen3-0.6B and Qwen3.5-0.8B through their chat templates with thinking off, the base checkpoints (Pythia, SmolLM2-360M, gemma-3-270m) given the prompt bare. Our ships are read by `thorp eval --evaluators ifeval` at the same cap (`THORP_IFEVAL_MAX_NEW`, new, beside `THORP_IFEVAL_LIMIT`). The card ranks a full-set reading that is not rigged; rigged ones stand below the line. The readings were still running when this was committed.

## The conversation corpus and the question gain (12 November; Justin: "i want it to be the best at conversation even if it's just short back and forth conversations"; "build the conversation corpus and the question-gain evaluator")

- **Why.** The talk leg came home using its question for 2% of its guess at the reply, 0.037 bits a byte at step 50 and at step 816, while `answers/bpb` fell from 1.93 to 1.71 (the section above, "why the talking 9ms answer beside the question"). A play's reply answers the scene, and a single exchange cut the scene away. A conversation keeps it.
- **The corpus** (`backend/scripts/make_conversation_corpus.py` → `lists/conversations-marked-v1.csv`, books `conv-m-<n>`, 12 conversations a book). It reads runs of adjacent speeches in which two voices take turns, out of the same 1,667 dialogue candidates, with the talk corpus's own speech finder, bars and held-out rule. A third voice ends a run, and so does narration. Runs are cut into conversations of 2 to 6 exchanges. The first voice is written as `<question>`, the second, the one the ship learns to be, as `<answer>`; the first speech need not be a question. **14,953 conversations, 42,605 exchanges** (8,488 of two exchanges, 3,185 of three, 1,420 of four, 766 of five, 1,094 of six), 94.8% from plays. An identical reply is the ship's at most 40 times. 697 held out; 400 written to `lists/conversation-heldout-v1.jsonl`, each with its history and its last exchange to read.
- **How tied a reply is to what came before**, by shared content words: to the speech it answers 19.7%; to the conversation so far 32.8% (39.6% for the replies that have a history); to another conversation's opening speech 2.8% (the floor). The history doubles what a reply can be read from. That is still a play's dialogue, not an answer key, and the leg will be judged on it.
- **Two fixes in the builder, none in the talk corpus.** A speech beginning `'tis` or `'twas` after a space now fails the archaic filter: `make_talk_corpus._ARCHAIC` writes `\b'(?:tis|…)`, and a space then an apostrophe is not a word boundary, so those slipped through the talk corpus's rule (it is not changed there, and its v2 list stands). The build's own check, that every conversation reads through `marks-v1` exactly as `turns.conversation` asks it, found one of 14,953 unmatched: `unwrap-v2` turns a spaced `--` into a dash, and the check compared the reply's raw letters. It now compares the cleaned conversation: 14,953 of 14,953.
- **`turns.dialogue` and `turns.conversation`.** A conversation as it is written into a book, one exchange a line, and the prompt mid-conversation, cleaned as the book was. With no history `conversation` is `prompt`.
- **The evaluator** (`thorp/evaluators/question_gain.py`, `question_gain`). For each held-out row the reply is read after its own history and prompt, after another row's (row `i + 37` of the same file, so it is still a conversation of the same kind and only the subject is wrong), and, where the row has history, after its prompt alone. Each ask is its own pass, never batched (bf16 rounds by batch shape). Readings are pooled bits per byte of the reply's text. **`gain/question`** = other − own (the headline); **`gain/history`** = alone − own; `gain/own_wins` is the share of rows read lower after their own words; `gain/bpb_own`, `gain/bpb_other`, `gain/bpb_alone`, `gain/n`. It writes nothing; 400 rows take 17 s on a 9m, 80 take 3.4 s. It reads `lists/talk-heldout-v1.jsonl` too, as single exchanges with no history. Tests: `backend/tests/test_question_gain.py` (13).
- **The readings on the docked ships** (400 rows each, read by hand from the last checkpoints, nothing written to the ledger):

  | ship | talk held-out: own / other / gain | conversations: own / other / **question** / **history** |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `9m@v0.0.6` (the seed) | 1.9271 / 1.9659 / +0.0388 | 1.8571 / 1.9394 / **+0.0823** / **+0.0976** |
  | `9m@v0.0.6-talk` (lr 0.0003) | 1.7065 / 1.7440 / +0.0375 | 1.7248 / 1.7699 / **+0.0450** / **+0.0235** |
  | `9m@v0.0.6-talk-hot` (lr 0.001) | 1.7225 / 1.7679 / +0.0453 | 1.7575 / 1.8011 / **+0.0436** / **+0.0199** |
  | `9m@v0.0.6-return` (the mix) | 1.7943 / 1.8347 / +0.0405 | 1.9004 / 1.9419 / **+0.0415** / **+0.0252** |

  The talk-held-out column matches the scratch readings of the morning to four places. The new reading is the right-hand column. **Every leg made the ship worse at using what it was asked.** The seed, which never read a turn, gets 0.082 bits a byte from the question and 0.098 from the history; each leg roughly halved the first and cut the second to a quarter. Taught each exchange as its own document, between `<eos>` and `<bos>`, they learned to stop reading what came before the turn. They also learned the register: every leg reads the reply itself 0.1 to 0.2 bits lower.
- **The leg, written and not queued:** `ships/specs/fitouts/9m@v0.0.6-converse.yaml` (goal 3). It is the talk leg with the conversations in place of the single exchanges: the dialogue shelf once, then `conversations-marked-v1` twelve times, seeded from `9m@v0.0.6`, lr 0.0003, the pull off. 0 of 1,325 books missing; about 864 steps, so about 26 minutes beside the talk leg's 24. It is read every 100 steps by `ce_bpb`, `answers` (the talk held-out, 80) and `question_gain` (all 400 conversations). The gate passes it; run id `6b00313e`. Its milestones are the talk leg's hole-card recall at 40% (2.2), a warning at 90% if the hole cards pass 1.85, and two **proposed** warnings at 90%: `gain/question` at least 0.082 and `gain/history` at least 0.098, the seed's own readings, so a leg that comes home using its conversation less than the ship it started from is flagged. Both thresholds are Justin's call, and both are warnings so the first sailing brings its readings home.
- **Not done.** The chat (`backend/thorp/chat.py`, `_ask`) still asks each question alone. A ship trained on conversations cannot be given its history from the console until the saved thread's earlier exchanges are passed to `turns.conversation`. Nothing here is committed. The corpus is 95% plays, with their kings, lords and servants; whether a conversation leg can come home talking about a day in plain words is what its sample answers will say.

## The kinds of knowledge a ship sails with are a choice of the branch (12 November; Justin: "in here /library/ogden we need to be able to have a selection which is the types of knowledge in the branch a ship will sail with: general knowledge, metaphysics, empirical knowledge, logic and maths. And then configure the percentage")

- **A fifth weighted dimension, by a rule the Library already had.** `library.category_of` sorted a book into the four positions of 8 November (a mathematical or logical title wins; then philosophy, religion, sermons and dialogues are metaphysics; science, history, biography, travel, practical books and periodicals are empirical; the rest is general). The rule now lives in `library_facts.knowledge_of`, and `selection.compose` weighs by it (`weights: {knowledge: {...}}`) beside kind, voice and source. It is read from a book's facts and not stored in them, so the facts' fingerprint did not move.
- **The choice.** A configuration's JSON gains `knowledge`: shares of the words, any of the four, empty meaning the books fall as they fall. Ogden and the scanned branch expose it. An empty `knowledge` is left out of the configuration hash, so anything saved before today keeps its identity; the same shares in another order are one configuration. In the selection it is the last weight, because with a budget of words the composer fills the shares of the last weighted dimension: with a size set, the knowledge shares are filled and the content makeup's own shares are not also held.
- **The page.** `/library/ogden` has a Knowledge block of four sliders. Until one is moved they stand where the other choices put the books and nothing is in the JSON; moving one sets all four, the other three keeping their proportion. Each row says what the composition holds of that kind (share, books, words) and turns red when it holds less than was asked.
- **What Ogden holds today, at its 68% bar:** general knowledge 74.5% (1,307 books, 77.9M words), empirical 19.8% (270, 20.7M), metaphysics 5.2% (83, 5.5M), logic and maths 0.4% (10 books, 459k words). So logic and maths is the scarce one: asked for 20% with no size, the whole corpus shrinks to 90 books and 2.4M words; asked for 20% of 20M words, it fills 2.8%. A ship that is to sail on a fifth logic and maths needs more such books above the bar, or a lower bar.
- **The browse payload's warm name carries its shape** (`library_browse-2`): a dashboard restarted on new code otherwise serves the last build from `data/warm/` for up to an hour, in the shape the old code wrote, and the block would not have appeared.
- **Live and not done.** The dashboard on 8787 was restarted on this code (07:11, and again at 07:23 after another session's restart left the port empty), so the block shows at :4321. A voyage still names a list and not a configuration key, so a configuration must be composed to a list before a ship can read it (as before). `test_selection.py::test_branches_are_seeded_once_and_a_hand_change_is_kept` fails on the branch seeding order and did before this.

## The chat sends the conversation (12 November; Justin: "wire the chat to send the conversation history")

- **A Thorp ship is now asked mid-conversation.** The console and the Chat tab already sent the thread to outside models. They now send it to our ships too (`ChatConsole.tsx`, `Chat.tsx`), and `POST /api/chat` and `/api/chat/stream` pass it on (`server._chat_own_answer`). `chat.exchanges_of` turns the thread into exchanges, each question with the answer that followed it; a question left unanswered (a failed or stopped ask) is left out. `chat.prompt_for(spec, question, history)` writes those exchanges before the question with `turns.conversation`, exactly as the voyage's turns were written and cleaned. For a marked leg the prompt reads `<bos>\n<question>…<answer>…<end>\n<question>…<answer>`, which is what the conversation leg reads. A ship taught no turns gets the same history in its old prose (`Question:` / `Answer:` lines, or the bare marks for a v0.1.4 voyage). With no history every prompt is what it was.
- **The window is kept whole-exchange.** The writer keeps only the last 1,024 pieces, so an overlong prompt would lose its `<bos>` and cut a turn in half. `chat.fit_exchanges` drops the oldest exchanges, whole, until the prompt and the answer's room fit. That room is `min(tokens, 160)` pieces: the console asks for 768, which would leave the thread no room at all, and a 9m's reply is a sentence. The question itself is always kept. The reply's `meta` says how many exchanges were sent and how many dropped (`exchanges`, `exchanges_dropped`), and `meta.rendered` shows the whole prompt.
- **A count fixed on the way.** `chat._own_tokens` asked our tokeniser for `add_special_tokens=False`, which it does not take, so every Thorp answer reported 0 prompt pieces, 0 answer pieces and 0 pieces a second. It now counts.
- **Checked.** Tests in `backend/tests/test_chat.py` (15 pass). On a fresh dashboard (8797) the talk leg was asked "Is the water cold?" and then "Will you go in?" with the first exchange as history: the rendered prompt carried it (27 pieces), and it answered "I will." Through the chat code a third turn ("Why not?" → "Because I'm not in love.") carried two exchanges, 42 pieces.
- **Not done.** The dashboard on 8787 was restarted on this code at about 07:20 and answers with history (checked through it). The audit traces the heads over the whole prompt, so with a long thread it reads more of the history than the question. A thread that changes ship mid-way sends the other ship's answers as this ship's own.

## Tool calling for the foreign ships, and vsm_status (12 November; Justin: "are any of our foreign ships wired for tool calling?"; "Yes, wire it"; "what would be the most basic tool call we could make for our VSM system?"; "Yes, add it")

- **None were wired.** The console's foreign path (`chat.foreign_stream`) rendered user and assistant turns through each model's template and never passed `tools`, had no tool turn and parsed no calls. Fourteen of the 29 foreign ships have templates that take tools.
- **The tools** (`thorp/chat_tools.py`), each read-only: `calculate` (arithmetic from the syntax tree, never `eval`; exponents over 1,000 refused), `current_time`, `board_score` (a model's ARC and IFEval readings from the leaderboard payload) and `vsm_status` (S2's view: every seat at sea from `vsm.helms()` and the head of `queue_read.pending()`, with a plain summary line first, because Qwen3.5-0.8B read a bare `"at_sea": "nothing is at sea"` as the fleet being at sea).
- **Two call grammars read, the rest said not to be.** `json` (`<tool_call>{name, arguments}</tool_call>`: Qwen3, Qwen3 30B-A3B, SmolLM3 Instruct) and `qwen-xml` (`<function=…><parameter=…>`: Qwen3.5, Qwen3.8 27B). Phi-4-mini, Gemma 4 and gpt-oss take tools in their own grammars and are listed as not wired yet; a base checkpoint whose template takes tools gets them and is said not to be trained for them (`chat.tools_support`, in each model's `facts.tools`).
- **The loop.** With `tools: "on"` on `/api/chat/stream`, a call is run and its result read back as a `tool` turn, up to `TOOL_ROUNDS` (4) rounds; each round opens with a `start` event and each call is a `tool` event; a call's own markup is held back from the stream (`_CallHider`); the reply's meta lists every call and result. The console has a tools switch beside thinking (on by default where the template takes tools, inert with the reason where not) and draws each call and its result as a line in the thread.
- **Proven:** Qwen3-0.6B calls `calculate` (37×41 → 1517) and `board_score` (SmolLM2-360M, 64.8 Easy) and answers from the result, in the console and on the API; Qwen3.5-0.8B chains two `calculate` calls; both answer "what is the fleet doing?" through `vsm_status`, after its description was made to say when to call it (Qwen3-0.6B did not call it at first).
- **Not done:** the Phi-4-mini, Gemma 4 and gpt-oss grammars; any tool that changes the system (queueing, recalling a voyage) — those should ask before each call.

## The 21m at 8,192 pieces, written for a hired GPU (12 November; Justin: "write the 8192 context spec for the 21m")

- **The spec** (`ships/specs/fitouts/21m@v0.0.6-ctx8192.yaml`, `knockout:3`, run `3e528d7b`): `9m@v0.0.6` as it docked (Ogden v0.1.4 kept raw, the marked tokeniser, the four metre cards and the comparator riddle) carried to the 21m's learning rate (0.0008), warmup (300) and pin layers (16 and 8), with one thing changed: the window, 1,024 → 8,192. Positions are rotary, so the window needs no table of its own. `batch_tokens` stays 32,768, so a step is 4 windows where it was 32, and the step count and what `hold` and `fade` mean are the standard's. The existing `21m@v0.0.6.yaml` was not the base: it fails the gate on four counts (the 9m's learning rate, warmup and pin layers).
- **It cannot sail on this Mac.** One training step of the 21m's shape at batch one, bf16, no scaffold, measured: 1.20 GB at 1,024 pieces, 2.70 at 2,048, 5.39 at 3,072, 9.17 at 4,096. That is about 0.54 + 0.54 × (window ÷ 1,024)² GB: the MLX kernel holds every head's attention map for the backward pass. At 8,192 it is about 35 GB for one window and about 138 GB for the batch of four, against 32 GB. So the spec says `model.backend: torch` and the charter lane. The pinned heads' maps stay dense on any backend (4 × 8,192 × 8,192 in fp32, about 1 GB a tensor a head); `optim.scaffold.sample` is the lever if the box cannot hold it, and it is a new run id, so it is not taken here.
- **The window is now a declarable departure.** `sop.check` refused any window but the standard's with no way to say why; `field: context` in a spec's `departures` now excuses the window and nothing else, as `content` and `tokeniser` already were (`tests/test_sop.py::test_another_window_is_refused_unless_it_is_declared`).
- **Its readings will not all sit beside the others'.** The hole cards are scored in windows of the ship's own context, so its bits a byte are read with up to 8,192 pieces of the book behind each guess; the cards, stars, Nevermore, pips and logic deck read fixed lengths and are comparable. `ce_bpb`'s 16-window batch at 8,192 would need about 68 GB on the Mac, so the full evaluation runs on the box.
- **Not done, and Justin's:** the milestones are proposed, not confirmed (previous-word at 0.5 or better by 30%, the hole cards at 2.3 or lower by half, both `recall`); it is not queued (the 21m is held until the 9m is good); no charter quote was asked for, and memory on a CUDA box is unmeasured, so a few real steps should come before the voyage. It gives the 21m the room for ARC-AGI-2 (118 of 120 tasks fit) but nothing in Ogden teaches a grid. AI2 ARC's questions are short, so the SmolLM2 goal below does not need it.

## The goal: the smallest ship that beats SmolLM2-360M on ARC-Easy and ARC-Challenge (12 November; Justin: "my goals ... are to get the smallest possible Nothing up my sleeve ship size to beat SmolM2 360m on both Arc easy and arc challenge questions ... I think 50m seems like a good candidate and then we need to work backwards from there"; then "let's audit all the questions on the test and then check which of the foreign ships vs our ships score ... then we can audit smolm2 on a hypothesis on why it scores the highest, we can then also add modern text to ogden. The ogden branch should contain books from all periods, but then when running a voyage the time period is a parameter that can be set")

- **The mark.** SmolLM2-360M reads 64.8 on ARC-Easy and 37.4 on ARC-Challenge on the board's reading (zero-shot, option text, mean loss). Every ship of ours from 300k to 21m reads at chance (25.0–26.8 Easy, 21.7–24.5 Challenge), flat across a 70-fold range of size; the first 50m reads 26.3 / 20.7. Size is not what is missing; the diet is.
- **The 50m is the working candidate**, because it is cheap to test: the first one cost $1.10 and 140 minutes on a hired RTX 4090, and it already beats SmolLM2-360M on our chaining deck (+0.072 against +0.034, single readings). Against it: nothing under 360M on the board reaches 64.8 Easy, and pythia-70m, a generalist on modern text, reads 34.3. A 50m can only win as a specialist on school science.
- **What the two papers say (read 12 November).** SmolLM2-360M read 4 trillion tokens of FineWeb-Edu, DCLM and The Stack (its model card), and its paper reports that FineWeb-Edu "achieves higher scores on the educational benchmarks MMLU, ARC, and OpenBookQA"; our first 50m read 0.68 billion tokens of period fiction. The ARC paper defines Challenge as the questions "answered incorrectly by both a retrieval-based algorithm and a word co-occurence algorithm" (Easy is the rest); a retrieval baseline over its released ARC Corpus (14M science sentences) reads 62.6 on Easy and 20.3 on Challenge, and no baseline in the paper beats chance on Challenge by more than its ±2.5 interval. So Easy is reachable by knowledge, and the route to it is the questions most models already answer, not the hardest.
- **The order, worked backwards** (my recommendation, discussed with Justin): (1) a reading with a gradient (the answer's rank and share, now built); (2) the ceiling, what a model can reach on its diet: the per-question audit, SmolLM2-135M and -1.7B for the size curve, the format checks (question stripped, options shuffled, no-model baselines) and the answer-swap probe for recall; (3) content, the main lever; (4) vocabulary, since 2,048 pieces spell science words as fragments; (5) answer format, as the talk legs found a reply that ignores its question; (6) the recipe sailed at 9m, 21m and 50m on the hired lane to find the smallest size that wins. Design from ARC's train and validation splits (3,370 and 869), score on the test.
- **Modern text in Ogden, the period a voyage's parameter** (Justin's design, agreed). It makes the cleanest experiment there is: one recipe with only the period window changed measures what period-only costs on ARC. A library configuration already has a `date` choice (start, end, undated). Missing: the library holds no modern text; a book carries its authors' death dates, not when it was written; a voyage reads a list, not a configuration. **Two gate rules refuse it**: `period` and `ogden-only`. The proposal is to split `period` into a licence rule, always enforced (authors dead by 1955), and a period window each voyage declares (pre-1930 by default). Candidate sources, licences not yet checked: the ARC Corpus, Simple English Wikipedia, OpenStax.
- **Parameters.** Our size names are budgets, not counts: 300k is 0.26M, 1m 1.09M, 9m 3.36M, 21m 11.0M, 50m 40.6M, with 93–98% of each in its layers (a 2,048-piece vocabulary). The outside models are named by their full counts, and much of theirs is embeddings. Recommended: report both counts on the board and leave the presets alone (the ladder is evenly spaced, and nothing yet says capacity is the limit); if parameters grow, grow the vocabulary first.
- **Not decided, and Justin's:** whether the ship that beats SmolLM2 must stay pre-1930; the gate split; the board's parameter labels.

## AI2 ARC kept question by question, and the queue's clock (12 November; Justin: "yes, build the per-question audit and the also here http://localhost:4321/expeditions/queue when scheduling tasks like investigations they should also have an estimated length so the queue is accurate"; "can't all those arc re-reads just be put on the queue"; "strike the duplicate arc-ai2 lines from the queue"; "Yes, add them")

- **Every answer a row.** A reading used to keep two totals a split and throw the questions away. `s4.arc_answers` (`thorp/arc_audit.py`, made by `ensure()`, no migration) keeps one row a reader a question: the bet, whether it was right, the answer's `rank` among the options (0 is the bet) and `p_answer`, its share of a softmax over the options' negative mean losses, so a ship at chance can still be read moving towards the answer. `scripts/score_arc_ai2.py` keeps an outside model's rows and reads the validation split with `--parts`; `arc_audit.read_ship` reads one of our checkpoints with the same scorer and writes nothing to the ledger. The 300k control read 26.8 / 24.2 that way, the board's numbers exactly.
- **The report** (`python -m thorp.arc_audit report` → `ships/reports/arc-audit-<date>.md`): the questions banded by how many outside models answer them, what only SmolLM2-360M answers, what none answers, by exam (the source the question id names), with our ships beside each band. Difficulty is read off the outside models only; our ships are at chance and their right answers mostly luck. A first signal from four pythias, worth no more than that: on the questions all four answer the 300k is right 33.5% of the time, against 23.8% on those none answers.
- **It sails from the queue.** `investigations/arc-audit-questions` (`knockout:7`, estimate 40 minutes) runs `python -m thorp.arc_audit reread`: the nine outside models (test and validation) and 13 of our ships (test), each skipped once it has every question, then the report. It is first in the queue behind the Qwen reading. It had first been run beside that reading, sharing the GPU, and was stopped.
- **Two mistakes of mine, put right.** That first run rewrote four board readings: pythia-14m and -31m back to float16 (139 and 35 questions skipped again, undoing the float32 fix above) and pythia-70m and -160m by a tenth of a point, because it read at batch 32 where the board reads at 64. All four were re-read at 64, 14m and 31m in float32, and match what was recorded (14m 29.5 / 20.6, 31m 32.8 / 21.8, 70m 34.26 / 21.76, 160m 38.85 / 22.61). Since then the scorer has `--no-board` (keep the questions, leave the board's reading), and `reread` uses it, the scorer's own batch, and float32 for those two. And the `--no-board` help text, as first committed, broke the script with a string split across lines; fixed before anything ran it.
- **Of the five `arc-ai2-*` lines, one was a duplicate.** I had called all five duplicates of the morning's hand reads, but those were other runs. Only `arc-ai2-50m` repeated a reading the board has; it is struck. The other four put the first board readings on `300k@v0.0.6-pins`, `1m@v0.0.5`, `9m@v0.0.5` and `21m@v0.0.5`, so they stay, and the audit now reads those ships too.
- **An investigation has a length.** A command took no time on `/expeditions/queue`, so every line behind the eight-hour Qwen reading was shown starting at once. `investigate.estimate`: the investigation's own last finished run, else its spec's new `estimate:` (`40m`, `8h`), else its `timeout:` as an upper bound (shown "up to"). Another command's length is its last successful run in `data/runs/queue.out`; a failed run is no longer a length (the probe reading had shown 0 minutes after dying in 30 seconds). A command at sea now holds the Mac's seat with what it has left. `arc-qwen3-4b` has `estimate: 500m`, from its own log (45 minutes with thinking off, about 226 seconds a task with it on).
- **Not done:** the five older ARC investigations and the deltanet check still show their deadlines; the probe step has no time; a voyage's in-run `arc_ai2` keeps no per-question rows (only the audit's reads do); the 8787 dashboard serves the new queue only after a restart.

## vsm_status sees investigations, finds a named job, and the IFEval readings go to the queue (12 November; Justin: "which model would actually use the vsm_status and return decent info"; "Yes, add it"; "why is nothing at sea? I thought the investigations were runnign?"; "move those tasks to the queue")

- **Which models use it.** Four questions on five models, thinking off: Qwen3-1.7B and Qwen3-4B called `vsm_status` on every fleet question and none on the control (the capital of France); Qwen3.5-0.8B on most, once calling `board_score` instead; Qwen3-0.6B only when the question named the fleet or the queue. SmolLM3-3B Instruct called nothing, because its template reads its tools from `xml_tools`, not `tools`: they never reached the prompt. `tool_format` now names the argument the template reads (`arg`), and SmolLM3 sees its tools, but it pastes raw results and writes malformed calls, so it is not one to use. Qwen3 30B-A3B and Qwen3.8 27B were not tried (the Mac was full).
- **`find`.** No model could count the lines ahead of a named job from a five-line list. `vsm_status(find=...)` matches any part of a name and says it in a sentence first ("investigation arc-ai2-21m is number 6 in the queue, with 5 line(s) ahead of it", or "is running now"); all four Qwens then answered 5. "Is the deltanet check running yet?" still sends every one of them to guess or stall without calling.
- **It said "in port" while `arc-qwen3-4b` had been at sea six hours.** It read only the helm locks, and an investigation takes no helm. It now reads the coordinator's own record (`queue_store.at_sea()`), each row checked for a live process, and adds a helm held outside the queue.
- **`arc-qwen3-4b`**, found that way: thinking off read 0 of 120 (a grid on 56%, cell accuracy 0.403); thinking on is reading "thinking ran out" on every task at its 4,096-piece cap (74 of 74 when looked at), so it will add nothing but its length. Kept at sea on Justin's word.
- **The IFEval readings went to the queue.** Seven ad-hoc jobs sharing the GPU had slowed `arc-qwen3-4b` from about 230 s a task to 1,634 s. They were stopped (50 to 100 prompts in, nothing kept) and the 12 readings still owed were queued as lines after `arc-ai2-21m`: the 300k, 1m, 9m and 21m by `thorp eval --evaluators ifeval` (541 prompts, cap 512), then the nine outside models by `score_ifeval.py --no-helm`, smallest first. The 50m's reading was done: 10.5 prompt-level strict, 21.5 instruction-level, on the 541.

## The purser, built; a line keeps its goal at sea (12 November; Justin: "We need to build the router"; "the current voyage has no goal. I thought that wasn't possible"; "Yes, fix it first")

- **Why the Vast lane sat idle** (the audit that morning). The lane was enabled and keyed, $0.88 of the week's $30 was spent, and the credit stood at $19.99, but nothing reached it. S2 routes a line abroad only when it is written `vast:` or is a torch spec. The purser decided on 8 November was never built, and its policy lets only Table Talk sail abroad.
- **Built as decided** (`thorp/viable/s3_control/purser.py`). It uses greedy list scheduling in the queue's order. It moves only unpinned voyages of an expedition in S5's `foreign_expeditions`, and never a `cmd:`, `wait:` or `hold:` line or anything behind a hold. A line is sent abroad when it is home at least `margin_hours` (1 h) and `margin_fraction` (25%) of its wait sooner, keeps one voyage's budget, and fits the day's allowance (the week's remainder spread over the days left). Its local time is the queue page's own clock (`dash/timing.RunTimes`). Its foreign time is that local time divided by the class's **measured** speed-up on the size, plus the set-up. The speed-up is the class's rate from the spend log over this Mac's best rate on that size, which errs slow. Only the RTX 4090 at 9m is measured today (2.0x); a class that has not sailed a size does not get its voyages.
- **S5 terms** (in `s5.charter_terms`, changed with `thorp s5 term ... --words`): `purser` is `off`, `advisory` (the default, as decided: a week of advice first) or `routing`. `foreign_expeditions` is `["table_talk"]`. `margin_hours` is 1 and `margin_fraction` 0.25.
- **The record**: `s3.allocations` keeps each call when the line's waters or class change: both estimates, the cost, the allowance, the reason and whether it routed. The queue API carries each queued line's latest call (`purser`) and the mode. `python -m thorp.viable.s3_control.purser` prints the calls on the queue now and writes nothing unless given `--keep`.
- **In the coordinator**: `lanes.run` asks the purser after reading the queue, at most every 5 minutes or when the queue or what is at sea changes. In `routing` mode a voyage called foreign sails on the Vast lane. A routed voyage that exits non-zero there comes back to the top of the queue pinned as `mac: <spec>`, so a failed hire costs a retry here, never the voyage. `mac:` is now a pin the lanes read; before this the prefix was kept as part of the spec's path.
- **On the live queue that morning**, the purser routed nothing, as the policy says.
