Simulated with Kipling and the card census allowed abroad, the five 9m arms (candidate, candidate-bridge, gqa, ctx2048, v4096) go to a 4090 for $4.95, about 2.7 h each, and the Mac's last line comes home at 43.5 h instead of 66.7 h. **Not decided:** which expeditions may sail abroad, and when advisory becomes routing. Both are Justin's. The 9m arms are single seeds compared against Mac voyages, so sending them abroad mixes the backend with the seed.
- **A line keeps its goal at sea.** A line that sailed left `s2.queue_lines`, and with it its goal, so the page showed 9m@v0.0.6-converse (kipling:3) with none. `s2.at_sea` and `s2.queue_history` now carry `goal_id`: from the queued row when a line is taken, then into the history. `queue_store.backfill_goals()` filled the rows written before (the one at sea and 9 of 347 history rows; the older specs name no `goal:`). Also, a rewrite of the queue no longer blanks every line's `added_by` and `added_at`.

## The medium band's tests, and Ling 3.0 Tiny pulled but not yet loadable (12 November; Justin: "add Ling 3.0 tiny to my foreign ships and pull it and then also in the leaderboard http://localhost:4321/leaderboard/medium add cards for IFBench, Multi IF, Artifacts Bench, Terminal Bench and others as how we've done on the xsmall leaderboard")

- **The medium band gets test cards, as the extra small band has.** `/leaderboard/medium` now carries **The tests**: sixteen flip cards (`frontend/src/components/islands/BenchBoard.tsx`, the shared `components/TestCard.tsx` lifted out of `StepsBoard.tsx`) — IFBench, Multi-IF, LIFE-Bench, ArtifactsBench, Terminal-Bench 2.1, SciCode, BFCL v4, TAU3-Banking, GDPval v2, AA-LCR, AA-Omniscience accuracy and non-hallucination, GPQA Diamond, HLE, HMMT Feb 2026, IMO-AnswerBench. Each turns over to its board of the five medium models with a published reading: Ling 3.0 Tiny (7.9B), Qwen3.5 4B (4.66B) and 9B, Gemma 4 E4B (8.0B) and 12B. A card is linkable (`?open=ifbench`), and a test with no art of its own gets its group on a short plate rather than an empty 16:9 frame.
- **The readings are published, and say so.** `frontend/src/data/benchmarks.json` holds Artificial Analysis's November 2026 numbers, taken from the Ling 3.0 Tiny card's comparison table (itself AA-sourced). Every card, and the panel's note, calls them published and not our measurement: no medium model has been read on these tests by this harness. When one is, its row and its own card join the board and the published rows stay beside it. The band's models are placed by `bands.ts` (`bandOf`), so Qwen3.5 4B, published at 4.66B, is medium and not small.
- **Ling 3.0 Tiny is pulled, but neither published MLX build loads.** `mlx-works/Ling-3.0-tiny-oQ4e` (4.65GB) and `rapid-mlx/Ling-3.0-tiny-MLX-4bit` (4.45GB) are both in `~/.cache/huggingface`; `mlx_lm.load` fails on each, for different converter-layout reasons: the rapid-mlx build keeps a **quantized `kv_b_proj`** (`ValueError: Quantized Bailing V3 kv_b_proj weights are not supported`), and the oQ4e build keeps a **quantized router `mlp.gate.gate_proj`** where mlx-lm 0.32.2's `BailingGate` expects a plain `mlp.gate.weight` (`Received 69 parameters not in model`). The mlx-lm release is the latest (0.32.2) and its bailing_moe_v3 does not read either layout, so this is not a version to upgrade to. The catalogue and `thorp.foreign` entries were reverted rather than offered: a foreign ship the chat cannot load is worse than one not listed. **To make it a ship:** convert the official bf16 (`inclusionAI/Ling-3.0-tiny`) with `mlx_lm.convert` so the sanitizer absorbs `kv_b_proj` and leaves the router plain, then teach the catalogue a local model path (it holds repo ids today, and `foreign._cached` looks only in the Hub cache); or run the GGUF under llama.cpp, outside this chat.
- **So Ling went in as the band's first candidate, with art** (12 November, Justin: "i dont' see it here http://localhost:4321/harbours/masaq-reach/medium"; "also ling needs a fal image"). `candidates.MEDIUM` now leads with Ling 3.0 Tiny (7.9B total, 1.3B active, InclusionAI), so it stands on `/harbours/masaq-reach/medium` and on the medium approach board as a **candidate** — a target, not a result: it has no reading, and cannot be given one until it loads. The card's link is the model card, not `/leaderboard/competitor/<model>`: that page is built from readings and 404'd for Ling, and does for every unread candidate, so a candidate card now opens its `source` (or is a plain card when it has none). Its fal image is `frontend/public/art/ling-3-0-tiny.png`: the generator gained an `inclusionai` lab look (dove-grey sails, a gold leech band, a silver ring on the main sail) and a `ling-3-0-tiny` subject (a narrow three-masted clipper overtaking a far larger galleon at night).
- **Not done:** no card art for the fifteen new tests (the plate stands in); the medium page's chaining and approach boards are unchanged; the 8787 dashboard serves the new page only after a frontend build (the `4321` dev server has it now), and its process was restarted with `backend/.venv/bin/thorp dash` because `uv run` cannot take its cache lock under the workspace sandbox.

## Ling 3.0 Tiny loads, and the unread page is not a 404 (12 November)

- **The fix was the router, not a new download.** Neither published MLX build loads in mlx-lm 0.32.2: rapid-mlx keeps a quantized `kv_b_proj` (mlx-lm only absorbs an unquantized one) and mlx-works keeps a quantized `mlp.gate.gate_proj`, where `BailingGate` expects a plain `mlp.gate.weight`. The oQ4e checkpoint is otherwise loadable, so its 23 router projections were dequantized (`mx.dequantize`, the config's own bits and group size, 8 or 6) and renamed into `mlp.gate.weight`, and the result written to `data/models/ling-3.0-tiny-oq4e-mlx` with the tokenizer and template. One config value needed a cast (`kda_lower_bound` -5 → -5.0: transformers refuses an int for the float field). It now loads and answers: "Reply with exactly: OK" → `OK`, 3.5 s, thinking on.
- **It is a local model in the registry, not a Hub repo.** `thorp.foreign` gained `LING_LOCAL` (`data/models/...`), its params, label, lab (InclusionAI · Ling), a `TUNING_OVERRIDES` entry (`instruct`: this is the post-trained model, not `-base-30T`), and `_cached` now treats an existing directory with a config and safetensors as cached. `chat._cache_file`/`chat._cached` read a directory in place, so its context (131,072), template, thinking toggle and tool support are read like any other ship's. `/api/chat/models` lists it **available**, and the console's filter (instruction-tuned, available) keeps it, so it can be chosen and answers from its own weights.
- **A candidate's page now says "no reading yet" instead of 404ing.** The competitor route is generated for Ling too, and the island turns a 404 from `/api/competitor` into a panel: a candidate, not a result; its published benchmarks are on the medium board, its model card is on the Hub. Any other error keeps its message. The earlier attempt — an Astro redirect keyed on the `%2F` URL — worked in dev but broke `pnpm build` (Vite cannot turn it into a path), so it was removed.
- **It is in the console's picker.** The console groups models by lab with a regex table that knew Alibaba, Meta, Microsoft, EleutherAI, Google, Hugging Face, OpenAI and Maincode: it gained `[/ling/i, 'InclusionAI']`, so the lab list shows InclusionAI and its model list shows Ling 3.0 Tiny, selectable and answering (checked in a browser: lab "InclusionAI", model "Ling 3.0 Tiny").
- **The blank page was a stale dev server, not the change.** After the build and the config edit/revert, `/leaderboard/competitor/...` rendered an empty `<main>` with `_jsxDEV is not a function` in the console (Competitor and ChatConsole both): the old Astro dev process held an outdated optimized-deps graph. Restarting `pnpm dev` cleared it; the page renders and both servers (`:4321`, `:8787`) answer 200 for the encoded address. The 8787 process was restarted with `backend/.venv/bin/thorp dash` (uv's cache lock is outside the workspace sandbox).

## The Server tab's two machines, and where a Vast hire's time goes (12 November; Justin: "in the ui http://localhost:4321/settings/server we need two subtabs. Mac Mini and then Vast and then in the vast subtab I want specs detailing how much time each task is taking so I can analyse where to optimise")

- **The Server tab is two subtabs** (`#servernav`): **Mac mini** keeps the performance chart and the process and machine facts; **Vast** takes the Vast.ai market table and the new timing table. The main tabs stay address-based; the subtabs are a client state, so a refresh lands on Mac mini.
- **Where the time goes** (`frontend/src/components/islands/VastTimings.tsx`). One row a recent hire: provision, ssh, code, data, board (the manifest, spec and papers), env (`uv sync --group torch`), train and home, each in seconds, minutes or hours, with a bar of each task's share of the hire and a red total when the hire ran past its quote. The open task is bold and timed to now; an aborted hire keeps the release's own words (a refused ssh, a stalled copy). The numbers are parsed from `data/runs/charter.log` (the charter flushes every step) and joined to `s3.spend_events` by instance for the hire's date, name, quote, cost and rate (`thorp.dash.settings.charters`, `/api/settings`'s `charters`).
- **What it says on the live box**: the 9m candidate hired at 09:32:55 on an RTX 4090 (quote 0.98 h, $0.36) spent 55 m 32 s of a 62-minute hire tarring **9.07 GB** of `data/` to the box — 6.82 GB of it `catalogue` and 1.60 GB `classics`, against 0.14 GB of the candidate's own shards — then ~6 minutes on the manifest. The 50m of 9 November shows the shape when fit-out is sane: data 23 m 46 s, env 6 m 2 s, train 1 h 8 m, home 7 m 55 s, $0.88 over 1 h 51 m. The optimisation is in the payload, not the GPU.
- **Not done**: the tar is unchanged (still `data/` broadly), the subtab is not linkable, and the 8787 build serves the page only after `pnpm --dir frontend build`.

## The 9m candidate's dead box: a bounded fit-out and 0.65 GB aboard, not 9.28 (12 November; Justin: "vast failed 9m@v0.0.6-candidat so why wasn't it killed and how do we optimise and fix all the things that are taking so long on vast")

- **What happened.** The 09:32:55 hire on an RTX 4090 spent 55 m 32 s tarring **9.07 GB** of `data/` aboard (`catalogue` 6.82 GB and `classics` 1.60 GB were 8.4 GB of it), then began the 211 MB manifest rsync at 10:29:36. The box's ssh died mid-transfer (`Connection timed out during banner exchange`), but Vast's API still said `actual_status: running`, the TCP connection stayed ESTABLISHED, and rsync had no timeout: the charter sat in "fitting out" for 18 minutes until it was killed by hand. The spend log recorded `recalled (SIGTERM)`, $0.44.
- **Why it wasn't killed.** The overrun deadline (the quote times 1.5) is computed only after `box.launch` and is checked by `_watch` while the voyage is at sea. `Box.sh`, `put`, `put_tar`, `get` and `sync_env` had no timeouts, so nothing bounded the fit-out; ssh's ServerAlive (30 s × 3) did not fire because the connection to Vast's proxy was alive even though the box was not answering.
- **The payload.** `carried_data` no longer carries `catalogue`, `classics` or `models`: the sources' catalogues, the classics shelf and the local foreign models are acquisition/chat material, not what a voyage trains or reads on. The data tar is now **0.45 GB** of shards and records plus the voyage's own texts (the 9m candidate's ~0.2 GB), and the 0.20 GB manifest — **~0.85 GB against 9.28 GB**; the 9m's own shards are 0.14 GB. `THORP_CARRY_ALL_TEXTS` still carries every text, deliberately.
- **The bounds.** Every ssh/rsync/tar step has a timeout (`sh` 600 s, transfers 1,800 s, pulls 900 s), rsync runs with `--timeout=120 --contimeout=30`, ssh with `ServerAliveInterval=15 ServerAliveCountMax=3`, and the whole fit-out has a deadline (`Box._limit`, `FITOUT_MINUTES` 30, `THORP_FITOUT_MINUTES` to change it; 0 removes it). A step that runs past it raises `BoxError`, so `sail`'s `finally` releases the instance. Transfers log their size in MB, and a release whose status is not home/finished (lost, recalled, gone) reads as **aborted** on the Vast page with its reason, not as a normal release.
- **Not yet proved.** The 4090 retry with the trim reached the code tar (113 MB) and was stopped for the smoke tests; no full 9m voyage has sailed on the new payload. The Vast lane is paused and both 9m arms (`candidate`, then `candidate-bridge`) sit on it, unpinned, waiting.
- **The smoke test** (`backend/scripts/smoke_vast_gpus.py`): one hire a class, `nvidia-smi`, a timed bf16 and fp32 CUDA matmul (achieved TFLOPS) and one AdamW step in the image's torch, then release; each hire/release is in the spend log as `thorp:smoke-...`. H100 SXM/PCIE had no offer inside the S5 filters that morning, so the smoke run may relax the price ceiling by 15% and reliability to 0.94, says so in its result, and leaves the terms themselves untouched.
- **The four passed** (12 November, ~$0.20, every ship released; `data/runs/smoke-vast.json`): **RTX 4090** 148.6 bf16 / 42.8 fp32 TFLOPS, 23.5 GB, 128 SMs; **RTX 5090** 239.4 / 67.8, 31.4 GB, 170 SMs; **H100 SXM** 725.4 / 52.0, 79.2 GB, 132 SMs; **H100 PCIE** 472.7 / 36.7, 79.2 GB, 114 SMs. Achieved = a dense 8192³ matmul (100 bf16, 30 fp32 with TF32 off) in the image's torch 2.7.1+cu128, after `nvidia-smi` and an AdamW step. The H100 PCIE hire took the only offer there was — unverified, 0.94 reliability, 196 s to provision — and still ran clean; SXM found a $2.344/h offer inside the terms. The 5090 is 1.6× the 4090 on bf16 and 3.7× on price; SXM is 4.9× the 4090 and 6.3× on price.
- **What the smoke test does not prove.** It is the GPU, driver, CUDA and the image's torch, not a Thorp fit-out: no `uv sync --group torch`, no data, no `thorp run`. The 4090 full charter did reach the code tar with the trimmed payload before it was stopped for these tests; the 0.65 GB fit-out and the bounded steps are tested, not yet exercised end to end.

## The console as the chat: answers read as prose, and Ling's tool calls (12 November; Justin: "Can you improve the formatting and make the chat terminal much more readable for someone that will spend a lot of time reading the answers"; "the main chat page can be deprecated. We'll only use the terminal"; "why is the tool failing")

- **An answer is read as blocks, not one run of spans** (`frontend/src/lib/richtext.tsx`). A blank line in the model's text is a paragraph on the page; `- ` and `1. ` runs are lists; `###` is a heading; `**bold**`, `*italic*` and `` `code` `` are folded before the whitelist is walked. The whitelist is unchanged — tags outside it lose the tag and keep the words, attributes never survive — so an answer that writes `<script>` still arrives as text (checked in a browser: no execution, no `img`/`iframe`).
- **The console is a reading surface** (`ChatConsole.tsx`, `global.css`). The transcript is a measured column (`76ch`), 15.5px at 1.72 line-height, with a hairline between turns and the speaker's name in brass. Turn metadata (tokens, thinking, cache, time, budget, window share) is a dim line under the answer, never inline with it; the reasoning is its own disclosure. The column is centred and the composer sits on the same measure, so the eye does not travel the width of the window.
- **The reader owns the size and the height.** A−/A+ step the answer's type (13–20px, kept in `thorp.chat.consolefs`); the drawer's top edge drags to resize, or is focused and moved with ↑/↓, and the chosen height is kept in `thorp.chat.consoleh`. A long thread opens on its newest turn. The default drawer is now 72vh (84vh on a phone) rather than 62vh/560px.
- **The `/chat` page is retired, not deleted.** The console is the chat: `/chat` is a short notice that hands its `?model=` or `?ship=` to the console and opens it, so old links still work; the console's "Full page" link is gone, and ⌘K hands the chosen model to the console instead of navigating away. The embedded chat tabs on ship, size and foreign pages are unchanged.
- **Why the fleet tool failed: Ling's grammar, not the tools** (`backend/thorp/chat_tools.py`). Ling 3.0 Tiny (Bailing V3) asks its model for `<tool_call>NAME<arg_key>k</arg_key><arg_value>v</arg_value></tool_call>`, but the template's prose says "function name and arguments", so `tool_format` saw `<tool_call>` plus "arguments" and chose the JSON parser. `json.loads` then failed on the name at character 0, and four rounds of `unknown() → the call is not JSON: Expecting value: line 1 column 1 (char 0)` followed. It is now its own format (`bailing-xml`), with a JSON fallback for a model that ignores the template and arguments-as-a-string handling in the JSON path. A call that still fails keeps its raw text, and the console's Debug panel shows it.
- **Tested.** `backend/tests/test_chat_tools.py` (11 tests: the Ling template is detected, its calls parse with typed argument values, a JSON call and a JSON-string `arguments` parse, a call with no name or no JSON is kept with its raw text, qwen-xml and the no-tools template are unchanged). `pnpm check` and `pnpm build` pass (1,518 pages).
- **To see the parser fix on :8787, restart it.** At the time of writing the running dashboard still served the old `tool_format` (`/api/chat/models` said `json` for Ling); `backend/.venv/bin/thorp dash` picks it up. The :4321 dev server has the console now.

## 8787 serves the API only (12 November, Justin: "8787 shouldn't serve a UI")
- **Changed:** `thorp dash` no longer serves the built page. Any non-`/api/` path other than `/onboard` answers 404 with a JSON note pointing at the frontend dev server. `_static`, `STATIC`, `TYPES` and `NOT_BUILT` are gone from `server.py`. The dashboard is `pnpm --dir frontend dev` on :4321, which proxies `/api` to :8787.
- **Left alone:** the first-run wizard at `/onboard` (Python-served, never needed the build). `thorp/onboard/build.py` still builds into `backend/thorp/dash/static`, which nothing serves now, so that install step and the onboarding hand-over to `/` need rethinking. A production install has no UI until a host for the build is chosen.

## The 9m is the sea trial and the 21m the coasting run (12 November; Justin: "Can you suggest new metaphors for the 9m and 21m ships. the 300k is the towing tank and the 1m is the measured mile. What should the 9 and 21 be?"; "Yes, rename both")

- **The names.** The 9m is **The sea trial** (was The speller; its rung card said "the landfall"): the finished ship taken past the harbour bar for the first time, helm, pumps and canvas all worked at once, to see whether she does at sea what the tank and the mile said she would. It is the first size taken out whole, where what the 1m ranked is confirmed, and the caveat already on this log (a 300k passes tests a 9m fails) is what a sea trial is for. The 21m is **The coasting run** (was The stylist; "the passage"): a proven ship's first working trips, port to port with a cargo aboard and the land always in sight. It is the first size given something to carry (grammar, a held style, and the logic questions from the 21m up), and it comes before the 50m's crossing, where a ship first leaves sight of land. The ladder now reads: towing tank, measured mile, sea trial, coasting run, crossing, ocean, new world.
- **Considered and avoided.** Shakedown (the 10-minute Vast spec's word), fitting out, soundings, and anything with "voyage" in it, since a voyage is a run here; so "maiden voyage" was out for the 21m. Alternates if either name goes flat: swinging the compass for the 9m (a ship turned through every heading against a known bearing, close to what the speller is scored on) and the working-up for the 21m (a navy term; the crew drilled until they handle her).
- **What did not change.** The objectives (the 9m's English surface, the 21m's grammar and style), the presets, the `kind` values (both null) and the **goal classes**: `speller` and `stylist` stay the class names in `tricks.py`, `heads.py`, `fleet.RUNGS`, `bet.py` and the question sets (`ogden-v0.1-questions.json`), and `leaderboard.class_of_preset` still says speller and stylist. They resolve through the rename tables. The speller's pools in the library and their branch names are about the corpus shelf, not the ship, and keep their names. `onboard/catalogue.py` still lists keel, pilot, speller and stylist (as it did for the 300k and 1m, it was not touched on the 11th).
- **Where it lives.** Two migrations set name, role and overview in `s1.sizes` (`c6d9e2f5a8b1`, `d7e0f3a6b9c2`; each downgrade restores the text the store held before, checked equal). They chain off `b5c8d1e4f7a0` (the queue cycles), so that migration has to be committed first or with them. `CLASS_RENAMES` in `server.py` and `router.ts` gained `speller` to `sea-trial` and `stylist` to `coasting-run`; `bet.PRESET_OF` gained the two old names as aliases; `fleet.py`'s three lookups by name say "The sea trial"; `RUNG_METAPHOR` and `RUNG_ART` in `views/harbours.ts`, `RUNG_CLASS`, the ship builder's `SHIP_NAMES` and the two static-path lists (`/ships`, `/sizes`) carry the new slugs. `speller` and `stylist` forward as addresses.
- **The plates** (`generate_art.py`, `CLASSES`): `sea-trial.png` (a new galleon crossing the bar into open water, the ring in the sky) and `coasting-run.png` (a laden bark close along a dark coast, harbour lights ahead). Both rung cards and class cards pick them up by slug. `landfall.png`, `passage.png` and the old class plates stay on disk unused. The sea trial's came first time. The coasting run's took four tries and the model kept painting an ordinary moon where the Culture's ring should be, even with the ring leading the prompt; the fourth was kept for its scene (laden deck, coast, harbour lights) and still shows the moon.
- **Not done.** The dashboard on 8787 keeps the names it read at start, so it still says the speller and the stylist until it is restarted. The migrations are applied to the store and the test database; nothing is committed. `bet._measured_mac` fails on a Postgres type error (`json_extract(...) = ?` with the integer vocabulary 2048 becomes `text = smallint`), which breaks `tests/test_bet.py::test_the_bets_ladder_is_the_registrys_ladder`; it comes from the SQLite-to-Postgres translation (`ledger_store._translate`), predates this rename, and is not fixed here. Five other tests in `test_harbours_journeys`, `test_starchart`, `test_fleet` and `test_presets` fail on things the rename does not touch (a fleet label, the journey and SOP fixtures, the 300k star-chart sizes, the presets-only-in-registry scan).

## The console's VSM tools: vsm_status, system_status, expedition_status (12 November; Justin: "can you give me an ontology of tool calls for the VSM in order for me to be able to manage thorp and nothing up my sleeve via tool calls from ling 3tiny"; "at most i want 5 tool calls that provide the most value and breadth"; "i like queue_status, I like vsm_status and then I'd like to be able to check status on each individual System within the VSM and then I'd like to be able to query expeditions for example how we're tracking against our v0.1")
- **Four VSM tools, all read-only** (`backend/thorp/chat_tools.py`), beside `calculate`, `current_time` and `board_score`. `queue_status` is unchanged. `vsm_status` gives a status line for each of S1, S2, S3, S3*, S4 and S5, and what in each needs attention. `system_status(system)` gives one system in detail; it takes `S3*`, `audit`, `s3` or `Control`. `expedition_status(find)` answers for the one expedition, Nothing up my sleeve v0.1 (`thorp/launch.py`): its phases, how much of the master charter is signed, the goals still deciding a parameter with the queue lines serving each, and the fleet. With `find` it answers for one preparation (Kipling), one goal (`kipling:3`, or words from a goal) or one class (`21m`).
- **Each reads what the pages read**, so the tool and the page cannot disagree: `vsm.audit()`, `cycle.payload()`, `lanes.lane_defs`, `vsm.lint_queue`, `schedule.jobs`, S3's spend log, S5's terms, `launch.payload` and the queue page's plan. A reading that fails is named in `could_not_read`, and the rest still answer. `vsm.audit()` takes about 9 s, mostly the cleaning audit resolving two book lists, so it is kept for two minutes.
- **Written for Ling 3.0 Tiny, and tried on it.** Ling chose the right tool for every question asked: Thorp overall, S2, v0.1 and Kipling. Three things it misread on the first try are now written differently. A v0.1 ship and its rehearsal are said apart ("9m@v0.1: not laid; latest rehearsal 9m@v0.0.6 (home)" had read as "the 9m is home"). A goal's role is in words, never the bare `delivered`, which read as done. `vsm_status` no longer says "call system_status for detail", which Ling took as an instruction and called all six. One slip remains: asked about v0.1, it said the 21m "has a spec written", which is its rehearsal's state.
- **Nothing acts yet.** No tool moves the queue, a lane, money or a rule. Anything that changes something is for a later step, with Justin signing what spends or changes a rule.
- **Tested:** `backend/tests/test_chat_tools.py`, 18 tests. The new ones cover each system's attention, a failed reading named and the rest still answering, the system's aliases, v0.1 with its preparations, goals and classes, every tool having a schema, and a Ling call to `system_status`.
- **Not yet live in the console:** the dashboard on :8787 runs the old `chat_tools` until it is restarted.

## What Ling's chat taught: an answer left in its thinking, wording a small model cannot misread, collapsible tool calls (12 November; Justin: "what improvements can we learn from this chat ... Can you validate it's findings and also check why it didn't complete"; "also tool calls text need be able to be collapsible")
- **Why the expedition answer was empty.** After a tool result the template opens `<think>` again, and Ling 3.0 Tiny wrote its whole answer inside it and ended its turn without closing it. The turn was saved as 861 thinking tokens and 0 answer tokens, `finish: stop`. Now a turn the model ended itself, whose thinking was never closed and has nothing after it, is taken as the answer, flagged `answer_in_thinking` (`chat.answer_left_in_thinking`). A turn cut off by the budget is not.
- **Ling's findings, checked against the store.** Most were right. Where they were wrong, the tool's wording was the cause: "refused lately" counted the gate's last 400 decisions, five days of them, and now counts the last 24 hours. The 11:56 cycle's "open the Vast lane" was passed on with the lane open, so a proposal the world has answered is dropped and the rest carry their cycle's time. The H100s read as measured without ever sailing, and are now "assumed". "The judge … is at sea" now has the judge as its subject. The fleet's ships are two plain sentences, not nested brackets. One expedition is said first as a preparation for v0.1, not the launch.
- **The console.** A tool call is a `<details>`, closed to one line with the result's summary and open to every field. A numbered list whose items are a blank line apart stays one list (it rendered as seven lists numbered 1). `**S3* — Audit**` is bold, and the `*` in S3\* no longer opens an italic.
- **Tested:** `backend/tests/test_chat_tools.py`, 20 tests. `pnpm check` passes. In the browser, the tool calls are closed by default and the list is one of seven. Asked again, Ling closed its thinking itself, so the fallback has a unit test but has not yet fired live.
